# Research log

> ⚠️ **Disclaimer: this is a research log, not a forecast.**
>
> This repository is a personal research and engineering log. Nothing in it is an earthquake prediction, forecast, warning, advisory, or operational product of any kind, and it issues no alerts. The figures recorded here are retrospective research metrics computed on public catalogues; they are exploratory, may contain errors, and have not been validated for operational use. Do not rely on anything in this repository for safety, evacuation, business, or any other disaster-response decision. For official earthquake and volcano information in Japan, use the Japan Meteorological Agency (https://www.jma.go.jp/en/) and your local government. Everything here is provided as-is, without warranty of any kind, and the author accepts no liability for any loss or damage arising from its use. Data remains the property of the providers listed under Data Attribution.
>
> ⚠️ **免責事項: 本リポジトリは研究記録であり、予報ではありません**
>
> 本リポジトリは個人の研究および開発の記録です。地震の予知・予測・予報・警報その他の運用情報ではなく、いかなる警報も発信しません。記載の数値は公開カタログを用いた事後的な研究指標であり、探索段階のもので、誤りを含む可能性があり、実運用に向けた検証は行っていません。安全確保・避難・事業判断その他の防災上の判断に、本リポジトリの内容を用いないでください。日本の地震・火山の公式情報は気象庁（https://www.jma.go.jp/）および各自治体の発表をご確認ください。本リポジトリの内容は現状のまま無保証で提供され、利用により生じたいかなる損害についても作者は責任を負いません。データの権利は Data Attribution 節に記載の各提供元に帰属します。

Every research narrative entry from the README, moved here unchanged on 2026-08-23 when the file passed 2,000 lines.  The entries are in the order they were written, oldest first; the index below is newest first.  Nothing has been edited: each entry is the line that was published, byte for byte, with a heading added above it so it can be linked to.

| [124](#update-124-2026-09-04) | 2026-09-04 | a per-cell rate that borrows from its neighbours, and a frozen independent check that failed -- for a reason worth more than the result it was checking |
| [123](#update-123-2026-09-04) | 2026-09-04 | a floor that was really a partial oracle, and a lever that only worked once its shape was changed |
| [122](#update-122-2026-09-04) | 2026-09-04 | three corrections to the last entry first, then the result they set up: counting beats fitting -- a per-cell map built by counting alone, with no model at all, is three times the fitted readout it replaces, and is judged REAL against the same floors |
| [121](#update-121-2026-09-03) | 2026-09-03 | the walk on the large-mainshock aim comes back REAL, and the controls say what the gain is -- a map of which cells produce large mainshocks |
| [120](#update-120-2026-09-03) | 2026-09-03 | a correction that matters more than the entry it corrects -- the harm reported last time was mostly a weight left pointing at the wrong target, and once the second factor is aimed at large mainshocks instead of at mainshocks the sign turns over; the floor for that claim is running as this is written |
| [119](#update-119-2026-09-03) | 2026-09-03 | three corrections to the previous entry first, then the result they set up: the mainshock readout does not merely fade above magnitude five, it reverses, and a floor built for the purpose shows the reversal is real and not an artefact of the instrument |
| [118](#update-118-2026-09-03) | 2026-09-03 | the floor the previous entry admitted it had not run is run here and the result holds, but a second control -- one that keeps each cell's own mainshock rate and destroys nothing else -- reproduces about half the effect, so half of it is a static geographic map and half is discrimination between events |
| [117](#update-117-2026-09-03) | 2026-09-03 | the two replication boxes named last time do not supply the missing events, the global catalogue does, and in an arena built from it -- where every cell fires and no easy negatives exist -- the mainshock readout clears its floor for the first time outside Japan |
| [116](#update-116-2026-09-03) | 2026-09-03 | changing what the combiner is trained against is not the lever, but moving the mainshock readout onto every member column is, and then raising the magnitude threshold shows that the design cannot resolve the effect at the sizes that matter -- which is the resolution of the instrument, not a ceiling |
| [115](#update-115-2026-09-02) | 2026-09-02 | counting the pairs behind the unheralded positives retracts a reading from the previous entry, moves the target to a cleaner definition of mainshock, and then a change of form -- factoring the label into "is this row positive" times "given that, is its driver a mainshock" -- clears its floor on a production member, on the published stack, and under three different declustering conventions |
| [114](#update-114-2026-09-02) | 2026-09-02 | the positives are split into the ones a larger earlier shock already claimed and the ones it did not, and the arm turns out to be an aftershock predictor: 0.963 on the claimed positives, 0.737 on the ones that arrive after a year of local quiet, where its margin over the causal climatology no longer clears its own bar |
| [113](#update-113-2026-09-02) | 2026-09-02 | a fifth and a sixth member are built and both leave the stack where it was, so the limit moves from the members to the combiner; then a ranking loss is found that gains three times more than anything else this year, is carried to production, and reverses -- and taking the reversal apart shows the gain exists only at a base rate the production fit never sees |
| [112](#update-112-2026-09-02) | 2026-09-02 | which rows the members are fitted on is measured for the first time: down-weighting the older rows is negative and monotone in the time constant, an ensemble over training spans is indistinguishable from an ensemble over negative draws, and what survives is the averaging itself, which is carried onto a production member and judged REAL |
| [109](#update-109-2026-08-29) | 2026-08-29 | an arm is fitted for the within-cell ordering, it comes out below every one of its nineteen floors, and measuring why finds that the feature columns carry a one-step shadow of each earthquake which the arm learned instead of the signal |
| [108](#update-108-2026-08-29) | 2026-08-29 | the within-cell effect is read the strict way, one window at a time, and eight of nine columns survive every condition and rank first of twenty at the three-day label while not one of them survives at thirty-four days |
| [107](#update-107-2026-08-29) | 2026-08-29 | the missing floor for the within-cell metric is built out of nineteen worlds that differ from the real one in exactly one thing, eight of the nine informative columns clear it at the three-day label by ten to fifteen standard deviations, the same columns barely clear it at thirty-four days, and the redraw turns out to manufacture forty percent more positives than the real catalogue has |
| [106](#update-106-2026-08-29) | 2026-08-29 | the structural lever named one entry earlier is withdrawn because the same entry's arithmetic refutes it, a per-cell level nobody had measured is measured and lands below the simplest one there is, and the within-cell base a future arm has to beat is put on the record |
| [105](#update-105-2026-08-29) | 2026-08-29 | an upper bound is proved on what any within-cell ordering can be worth to the pooled score, the horizon turns out not to change the scoring geometry at all, and a waveform fetch that was about to be started is called off before a single request is made |
| [104](#update-104-2026-08-29) | 2026-08-29 | the geodetic field is read again with its direction kept and every cell covered, the reading is null, a control that was supposed to hold the result down turns out to have been silently doing nothing, and two ways of reshaping the column are killed in fifteen minutes without running either |
| [102](#update-102-2026-08-26) | 2026-08-26 | the level is carried the other way, as a twelfth feature column with the model refitted, the model uses it, the arm gets worse, and three statements that had been treated as one turn out to be three |
| [101](#update-101-2026-08-26) | 2026-08-26 | an observable that is not the catalogue is read as a level for each cell and window, the reading is null, the level underneath it is real, and an audit finds that three quarters of the stations had been put in the wrong cells |
| [100](#update-100-2026-08-26) | 2026-08-26 | the ceiling is split, the arm turns out to beat every static per-cell rate that does not read the year it is scoring, and a claim made three entries ago has to be taken back |
| [99](#update-99-2026-08-26) | 2026-08-26 | the coarser readout is carried onto the configuration the published forecasts actually use, where it is worth nothing, and the reason turns out to be that there was nothing there to win |
| [98](#update-98-2026-08-25) | 2026-08-25 | making the readout coarser does what making the features finer did not, a first floor that no causal transform could ever have passed, and the same reading against a floor drawn from the past |
| [97](#update-97-2026-08-25) | 2026-08-25 | the pairs that make the judged figure are counted, and the arm turns out to sit exactly on a per-cell rate built from every window except the one it is scoring |
| [96](#update-96-2026-08-25) | 2026-08-25 | the frame is shortened with the horizon held still for the first time, and the gain this write-up has been attributing to the frame since update 73 turns out to belong to the horizon |
| [95](#update-95-2026-08-25) | 2026-08-25 | two more null carriers, and the arithmetic that explains why all of them were null |
| [94](#update-94-2026-08-25) | 2026-08-25 | a round that finally passed the rule, an audit that showed the floor it passed was the wrong one, and the two rounds built to settle it, both null |
| [93](#update-93-2026-08-25) | 2026-08-25 | three different ways of carrying the short-horizon ordering into the thirty-four day product, and all three are null |
| [92](#update-92-2026-08-25) | 2026-08-25 | geodesy is read a second time, in a five-minute kinematic frame instead of a daily one, and it is null on the same dimension the daily solutions were null on |
| [91](#update-91-2026-08-23) | 2026-08-23 | the second of the three different observables is read on the within-cell dimension; the first run of it passed the pre-registered rule, an adversarial audit found two places where the code did not match the pre-registration, and with those corrected the round is null |
| [90](#update-90-2026-08-23) | 2026-08-23 | the first of the three different observables is read on the within-cell dimension and is null: the timing of deep tectonic tremor does not order the times of earthquakes inside the cells where tremor is measured, and the learner turns out not to lean on it at all |
| [89](#update-89-2026-08-23) | 2026-08-23 | the first of the three ways of carrying the within-cell ordering into the thirty-four day product is null, and a second round then closes the axis its null clause pointed at while replicating the ordering itself on an independent set of windows |
| [88](#update-88-2026-08-22) | 2026-08-22 | a claim this write-up has been making since update 82 turns out to be too strong: the thirty-four-day product does have a within-cell time ordering underneath it, and what removes it is the overlap of the label rather than the length of the horizon |
| [87](#update-87-2026-08-22) | 2026-08-22 | the finer input frame that update 86 said it would try changes nothing at thirty-four days, and the floor explains why: the member that was added is nearly a duplicate of the one already there, while a member that is uncorrelated by construction is worth more |
| [86](#update-86-2026-08-22) | 2026-08-22 | the floor that update 85 left for a round of its own now says the longer training span bought estimation and not information, and a baseline measured at the same time says the 0.90 threshold sits close to where climatology already is on this task |
| [85](#update-85-2026-08-22) | 2026-08-22 | a gate written in advance closed the road that added test windows, and the road it left open -- twenty more years to train on, with nothing else touched -- met the bar this project set for itself in advance |
| [84](#update-84-2026-08-21) | 2026-08-21 | three members that differ in resolution, in functional form and in horizon; all three sit inside the floor, and the floor says what a new member is really worth |
| [83](#update-83-2026-08-21) | 2026-08-21 | the drift a cell's own history can see is nothing, the stack that was found by accident is real and reproduces under a rule written in advance, and conditioning its weights on how much is triggering nearby adds exactly nothing |
| [82](#update-82-2026-08-21) | 2026-08-21 | two rounds aimed at the part of the forecast that carries the score, and then a measurement of how much room that part has left, which turned out to be less than the first version of the measurement said |
| [81](#update-81-2026-08-21) | 2026-08-21 | a finer grid clears the AUC half of the bar for the first time and fails the other half, and the reason it fails turns out to be the same fact that says where the work goes next: the pooled number is climatology and the part that should carry time points backwards |
| [80](#update-80-2026-08-20) | 2026-08-20 | the target's own number is measured on a longer catalogue and a sparser driver stream, comes out at 0.9010 with a positive Brier skill, and fails the bar that was written down before it was measured -- the bar being the point of the round |
| [79](#update-79-2026-08-20) | 2026-08-20 | four rounds spent on one question -- whether anything can be made to rank better inside a cell and a year than a raw count fitted to nothing -- and the answer, so far, is no; what the rounds did establish is that fitting is not free |
| [78](#update-78-2026-08-20) | 2026-08-20 | the catalogue was lengthened to thirty-three and a half years and the half-day result replicated in seven years that had never been used; then the nine candidate columns were re-measured at that frame, each against its own floor, and one of them, with nothing fitted at all, ranks better inside a cell and a year than the eleven-column model trained on it |
| [77](#update-77-2026-08-19) | 2026-08-19 | the Brier skill that kept thinning as the frame narrowed was not a calibration fault and there was nothing to repair; the score itself turns out not to compare across catalogues, so the axis has been replaced by how much each cell's own worst hours are enriched; and the quarter-day rung, published two updates ago against a borrowed floor, has now been given its own and stands |
| [76](#update-76-2026-08-19) | 2026-08-19 | the baseline was wrong: a catalogue stripped of its timing was run through the same pipeline, and one arm of this round came back REAL on it, so every arm is now measured against its own floor rather than against one half; the half-day result of the last two updates survives that test, one reading from this round is reversed by it, and one claim is withdrawn |
| [75](#update-75-2026-08-19) | 2026-08-19 | the quarter-day rung clears the bar as well, and is the strongest of the four: pooled 0.9234, within-cell 0.6574, seventeen windows of twenty-one, p 0.0022; but the Brier skill score thins to plus 0.0014 and that is the thing to work on next |
| [74](#update-74-2026-08-19) | 2026-08-19 | the half-day rung clears the between-window bar: on the same twenty-one windows the pooled figure is 0.9207 with a positive Brier skill score, the within-cell skill rises to 0.6487, and the test that update 73 failed now passes |
| [73](#update-73-2026-08-18) | 2026-08-18 | the frame shortening carried into the production form: on the one degree grid the pooled figure rises past 0.90, the within-cell skill rises with it rather than being destroyed the way finer cells destroy it, and judged between windows it still does not clear the bar |
| [72](#update-72-2026-08-17) | 2026-08-17 | the frame was the lever: shrinking the bin and the horizon together raises the honest metric at every step from three days down to fifteen minutes, and the axis never turns over -- plus the round that had to be thrown away because it was written to fit a machine rather than the question |
| [71](#update-71-2026-08-17) | 2026-08-17 | four rounds: a claim about second catalogues that survives the plain comparison and dissolves under a matched one, a verdict that flipped when the significance threshold was computed exactly instead of approximately, and then the frame itself turning out to be the thing that was costing signal |
| [70](#update-70-2026-08-16) | 2026-08-16 | nine candidate second columns, combined with the standing arithmetic by nothing larger than two parameters; none of them resolves outside Japan, and the one that works inside Japan turns out to be half explained by which catalogue it is read from |
| [69](#update-69-2026-08-15) | 2026-08-15 | one column of arithmetic replicates in three regions and fifty windows, the fitted configuration adds nothing to it, and the binding constraint turned out to be how many independent windows exist rather than how large the effect is |
| [68](#update-68-2026-08-15) | 2026-08-15 | the yardstick was nine tenths a map: under a metric that compares only rows sharing a cell and a year, the fitted model sits exactly at the permutation null while a single count of nearby earthquakes does not |
| [67](#update-67-2026-08-12) | 2026-08-12 | a planned round is refuted before it is run, the catalogue turns out to be seventy percent incomplete at its own threshold, and the column family that finally clears the bar does not survive the move to the operational frame |
| [66](#update-66-2026-08-12) | 2026-08-12 | three rounds that close the add-a-column family, one implementation fact worth publishing, and a bug that had wrongly excluded a whole subduction zone |
| [65](#update-65-2026-08-11) | 2026-08-11 | a correction: the reasoning at the end of update 64 had the direction backwards, and the measurement that shows it took twenty-seven seconds |
| [64](#update-64-2026-08-11) | 2026-08-11 | buying windows instead of seeds: the narrow scale fails a between-window test across three other subduction zones, and the yardstick that killed it is confirmed twice |
| [63](#update-63-2026-08-11) | 2026-08-11 | a narrow spatial scale wins twice and then fails on the window that was kept back, and the round produces a number for how wrong the usual yardstick was |
| [62](#update-62-2026-08-10) | 2026-08-10 | three axes close, and the most useful thing in the round is a defect in how the comparison was set up: nine arms built with a new operator were being read against a baseline built with the old one, so a flat surface could clear the bar on the operator offset alone |
| [61](#update-61-2026-08-10) | 2026-08-10 | the operator, not the kernels: replacing one-point sampling with exact cell integration recovers 92 to 99 percent of the deficit that four rounds had been reading as a statement about ETAS |
| [60](#update-60-2026-08-10) | 2026-08-10 | the pairing question closes inside the materiality floor, the sign reversal that headlined update 59 fails to replicate, and a probe costing no training at all moves the mechanism from the kernels to the operator that samples them |
| [59](#update-59-2026-08-09) | 2026-08-09 | the permutation control lands on neither of the two answers it was built to give, and the reason it cannot is itself the finding: the same reshuffle that loses on the earlier folds wins on the later ones, with both fold sets passing every gate in the same run, so what the hand-tuned pairing buys is a property of the window it is read on rather than of the model |
| [58](#update-58-2026-08-09) | 2026-08-09 | the decomposition promised in update 57 returns a negative answer that turns out to be more useful than a positive one: only one of the four form elements carries anything, swapping all four at once is worse than swapping none, and the round after it shows the winning element cannot be re-expressed as an explicit feature -- so the deficit sits neither in the functional form nor in the parameter values taken separately, but in the two together, which is what the round now running is built to probe |
| [57](#update-57-2026-08-08) | 2026-08-08 | a change is adopted on a ten-of-ten replication and withdrawn one round later, when it is finally tested on folds that were never used to select it; both moves update 56 proposed are answered -- extending the span further back flattens, and pooling other subduction zones as training rows is null with its mechanism pinned -- and the catalogues those zones supplied are turned into fitted ETAS parameters instead, which opens a four-round arc on the six kernel columns ending with the deficit located in the functional form rather than in the parameters, the discretisation or the diversity |
| [56](#update-56-2026-08-06) | 2026-08-06 | the factor of two between the two catalogues is decomposed and turns out to be the clock and the calendar rather than the catalogues, and the residue leads somewhere better: with the scored rows pinned and only the training start moved, genuine increment rises from +0.071 at three years to +0.159 at forty, and three pre-registered controls kill the three obvious alternative explanations -- it is not row count, not early stopping, and not the 2011 sequence, but the span of epochs the training rows are drawn from |
| [55](#update-55-2026-08-05) | 2026-08-05 | a short-horizon result is overturned as an artifact of the forecast grid, the resolution lever is then shown to be an easy-negative artifact in time exactly as it already was in space, redefining the arena rather than the resolution produces a genuine increment that survives a pre-registered select-and-confirm test -- and the AUC above 0.90 that appeared along the way, the first in this project with positive Brier skill, is refuted by replication on an independent catalogue before it could be claimed |
| [54](#update-54-2026-08-05) | 2026-08-05 | the round-38 fetch finishes and its pre-registered readout comes back null at exactly the power it was built to buy -- 418 covered positives against 201, seven live folds against five, and a positive control at +0.100 proving the pipeline would have seen a real signal on those same rows -- while two zero-fetch gates close the S-net idea for a geometric reason that station count alone had hidden |
| [53](#update-53-2026-08-01) | 2026-08-01 | sizing discipline applied to the last unstarted dense lever while the round-38 fetch runs: multi-station pre-window tremor on Hi-net fails both pre-registered gates -- 206 covered positives where 300 is the floor and the existing single-station lever already covers 369, and roughly 8,100 chain-hours of continuous data for the 7.5-day window -- so the branch is recorded as a mapped constraint for the cost of one script, before a doomed multi-month fetch rather than after it |
| [52](#update-52-2026-07-29) | 2026-07-29 | before spending anything on the two InSAR levers still listed as open, the noise floor they were sized against was measured instead of assumed -- and it is nineteen times larger than the assumed value, because the limiting term is per-epoch atmospheric delay that stacking interferograms cannot touch; both levers fail, and the eight nulls before them turn out to have been run below SNR one |
| [51](#update-51-2026-07-28) | 2026-07-28 | the follow-up update 50 left open is answered the same day and without fetching anything: asking the catalogue where it is actually incomplete shows 84 percent of the entire six-hour missing-event budget sits inside the first three minutes -- so the longer window has nothing to recover, and the same measurement turns the previous round's weakest caveat into its strongest evidence |
| [50](#update-50-2026-07-28) | 2026-07-28 | a lever aimed at the catalogue rather than at the model: the dense network can demonstrably detect aftershocks the catalogue omits in the first three minutes -- 81 percent recall on the ones it does list -- and feeding them in changes nothing, because a control needing no waveform at all shows that entire three-minute window is already inside the ETAS-based baseline |
| [49](#update-49-2026-07-28) | 2026-07-28 | the pre-registered follow-up runs: compressing each waveform block to a single column removes the width penalty exactly as intended, and not one of the five channels changes verdict -- the cost was real, but it was not hiding a signal; the fourth open-broadband lever also lands, completing the repaired-cohort sweep |
| [48](#update-48-2026-07-27) | 2026-07-27 | the corrected six-times-larger fetch lands, and it settles three levers at once: the dense-network directivity gain is reproduced exactly by a station-geometry control and is therefore hollow, while the post-foreshock coda, the source-spectrum and the seven-and-a-half-day pre-window levers all converge on decisive nulls -- and the negative point estimates that had accumulated across this arc are shown, by direct measurement, to be the price of block width rather than a property of the earth |
| [47](#update-47-2026-07-26) | 2026-07-26 | the dense-network directivity lever reads out as no signal -- and a control run six times over shows its negative point estimate is exactly what sixteen columns of pure noise do at this coverage; reading it out also uncovered why every dense channel in this project has been power-starved: the fetch cohort was silently restricted to magnitude 5 and above, and the corrected six-times-larger fetch is already running |
| [46](#update-46-2026-07-26) | 2026-07-26 | the third mis-windowed lever is re-run and comes back clean-null with a robust covered-subset overfit penalty; the dense-network directivity dataset passes its physical quality gates -- 200 of 397 events collected, 36 stations each, every window sitting on the rupture |
| [45](#update-45-2026-07-26) | 2026-07-26 | a timing audit finds that the whole open-broadband waveform arc was reading a window nine hours late: the catalogue is in Japan time and the data service is in UTC, so three of the four levers never observed the earthquake they were supposed to characterise; two are re-run the same day and the dense-network directivity build proceeds on the corrected clock |
| [44](#update-44-2026-07-24) | 2026-07-24 | correction: the dense national network is not gated for this project -- it has an account and a working fetch pipeline -- so the "gate-free" restriction on the three preceding waveform levers was an unnecessary self-limitation, and the dense-network test that update 43 deferred is now in progress |
| [43](#update-43-2026-07-24) | 2026-07-24 | the post-foreshock envelope null pointed to the nucleation-physics control itself; a 107-agent search plus a hard station-count probe show the near-source observable that would proxy it is either structurally impossible or self-similar on open data, and the one scalar version that is buildable returns an underpowered null -- the open-data source-parameter channel is mapped out, and the remaining physics is explicitly behind the dense gated network |
| [42](#update-42-2026-07-24) | 2026-07-24 | a 102-agent adversarial literature search retires the learned multi-station waveform model as redundant-by-construction and points instead to post-foreshock nucleation physics; the cheap open-network version of that lever is then built and tested same-day, and returns a clean null with no usable escalation signal -- the envelope shape carries real univariate signal but the catalogue already has it |
| [41](#update-41-2026-07-23) | 2026-07-23 | the one surviving lever from update 40 -- multi-station waveform coherence -- returns its covered-cohort result: an underpowered null, with the only weak structure sitting squarely in the ocean-microseism band that the microseism-immune channel does not corroborate |
| [40](#update-40-2026-07-22) | 2026-07-22 | update 39's three named remaining levers get resolved: triggering-versus-background turns out to already live inside the ETAS baseline, tide-gauge differencing is scoped out on coverage and signal-to-noise, and a fresh 99-agent adversarial search narrows the entire surviving space to a single untested channel — multi-station waveform coherence — now under test |
| [39](#update-39-2026-07-21) | 2026-07-21 | with both new observation channels null, the remaining escape from the aleatory ceiling was a different TARGET — one independent of the memoryless magnitude draw; a 106-agent adversarial literature search plus a self-diagnostic map that space as empty or hollow too, without closing the goal |
| [38](#update-38-2026-07-21) | 2026-07-21 | with InSAR closed, the one genuinely untested channel gets its cheap test: continuous-waveform emergent/tremor energy, gate-free and six times better powered than InSAR — a clean null, but with an honest limitation that keeps the heavier version open |
| [37](#update-37-2026-07-20) | 2026-07-20 | the properly powered global InSAR result promised since update 34: at 4.6 times Japan's covered-positive count, pre-mainshock surface deformation adds nothing to escalation forecasting — a clean, well-powered null, with one crustal thread left unresolved rather than refuted |

## 2026-06 update

**2026-06 update**: a full skill decomposition showed the monthly ML ceiling (AUC 0.871 @ 34 days) is ~98% static spatial climatology plus aftershock clustering, with all exogenous channels at net zero; re-measured on the CSEP information-gain axis, properly-fit continuous ETAS adds a genuine 1.6-3x probability gain over climatology (post-mainshock ~10x). Research focus moved to nucleation from raw waveforms; the Tohoku 2011 pre-registered prospective test closed at 0/3 - the apparent near-hypocentre rate excess proved to be a detection-sensitivity artifact (see the three 2026-06 sections below); a second, fully-onshore case (Kumamoto 2016) also closed at 0/3 on a 2,608-event detection-uniform catalogue, removing array resolution as the explanation; cross-correlation relative relocation of the same catalogue — horizontal and then depth-resolved 3D — returned two further pre-registered nulls, closing the arc at four for four; a fifth pre-registered test - repeater/template-family analysis as a slow-slip proxy - also returned null after matched-filter sensitivity equalization (the provisional migration PASS on the raw association catalogue proved to be another detection-sensitivity artifact). A sixth test took on the magnitude (b-value) channel - the Gulia & Wiemer (Nature 2019) "traffic-light" claim, made on this exact Kumamoto sequence - using independent network relative magnitudes: the only prospective, temporal criteria do not support a precursor (the background-drop test is unmeasurable in a 50 h window, and the b-vs-time trend is too sparse to score and runs the wrong way in the two bins available), while the one criterion that passes - the future rupture-initiation patch is a low-b asperity (b 0.76 near vs 0.86 in the surrounding ring, robust to the completeness cutoff but a thin margin) - is a static, retrospective spatial contrast, not a prospective signal. Four further tests closed the arc at ten-for-ten, all pre-registered: a below-catalogue masked-residual tremor channel (0/3; the one inward-migration PASS was exposed by a control as an amplitude-weighted array-aperture artifact), a seismic-velocity-change dv/v test (0/2; flat within inter-station noise over the 28 h window), a self-supervised SimCLR waveform-embedding test on Kumamoto (null; the strongest apparent late drift survives a time-shuffle but fails the surrogate-prospective and spatial-null controls), and a multi-case extension to the 2014 Iquique Mw8.1 megathrust (null, with no temporal drift even where geodesy reported foreshock migration). Across catalogue occurrence/geometry/family/magnitude statistics, below-catalogue emergent energy, the elastic velocity of the medium, and a learned waveform representation, onshore raw waveforms do not prospectively mark independent-mainshock nucleation in the two cases dense enough to test it. A subsequent eleventh test took the geodetic channel the waveform arc had pointed to - NGL 5-minute kinematic GNSS at GEONET stations on the Kumamoto rupture - and it too is null across model-free raw and common-mode-filtered statistics and a best-chance forward slip-model inversion alike: the inferred pre-mainshock slow slip is at or below prospective geodetic resolution, so even the proper detector does not mark the nucleation by this method - though a 2014 Iquique positive control, where slow slip was geodetically documented, is also null on the identical pipeline, showing the geodetic null is bounded by the reproducible method's sensitivity rather than proven absence. A synthetic-injection characterization then quantifies the floor - a uniform slip of Mw ~5.6 (Kumamoto's dense onshore network) to ~6.9 (Iquique's sparse offshore geometry), with the documented/inferred precursors sitting beneath it - and a network-design calculation (Okada Green's functions calibrated on the two measured floors) shows the offshore floor reaches the ~Mw 6 precursor scale only with seafloor GNSS-A above the rupture, so the negative result specifies the observing system that would make prospective geodetic nucleation detection feasible. The waveform line was then pushed to the near field: an S-net seafloor matched-filter on the three largest offshore M7+ events is null even below catalogue completeness (injection-calibrated, with an aftershock positive control confirming picker sensitivity), and an onshore Hi-net dense-array matched-filter on the 2016 Kumamoto M6.5 reaches the deepest open-data injection floor (dM-5.0, ~ML1-2) and returns a power-calibrated null on foreshock-rate acceleration (last-3h event count at the stationary expectation, p=0.58, with >=0.98 power to detect an inverse-Omori cascade of >=20 events). A second onshore high-density case (2019 Ridgecrest M7.1, SCSN, the best-instrumented foreshock sequence available) returns the same powered null, shown robust to an Omori-decaying baseline as well as a stationary one (last-3 h p=0.23-0.27), with a self-injection floor of dM-4.5. A magnitude-channel test on the same Ridgecrest sequence — the Gulia and Wiemer (2019) foreshock-traffic-light showcase — finds no prospective b-value drop before the M7.1 with independent magnitudes (the incompleteness-robust b-positive stays near 0.9 to the final approach), corroborating the parameter-sensitivity critique of Dascher-Cousineau et al. (2020). A third showcase, the 2016 Amatrice-Visso-Norcia Mw6.5 sequence on an 894k-event Mc 0.20 catalogue (the only Gulia and Wiemer showcase where the FTLS relative-drop test is computable), likewise dissolves the RED light: the Aki foreshock b-drop (-27.5% vs background) is an incompleteness artifact (the robust b-positive is a normal 1.14), there is no prospective b-drop and the run-up rate decays monotonically once the intervening Visso Mw5.9 aftershocks are accounted for (a naive Amatrice-only Omori excess of p 0.000 collapses to ratio 1.00 under the correct two-term superposition), and the spatial b-field is unresolved -- making the FTLS b-channel three-for-three null under incompleteness-robust estimators and correct triggering nulls. A fourth case then extends the b-channel to the canonical natural foreshock sequence (2009 L'Aquila Mw6.3): on a foreshock-specific template-matched catalogue (10x denser than the routine catalogue, Mc 0.80) the foreshock b is normal with no drop (the apparent drop on the sparse aftershock-dominated catalogue was a completeness artifact), the prospective b is flat, the rate is triggered Omori bursts decaying overall rather than accelerating, and there is no low-b nucleation asperity -- four-for-four null on the FTLS b-channel, now including a non-showcase natural foreshock case. A fifth case extends the channel to a subduction megathrust (2014 Iquique Mw8.1), the canonical accelerated-nucleation sequence: on the open IPOC catalogue (Mc 2.6) the foreshock b is not anomalously low, the prospective b is flat-to-rising, the acute seismicity rate decays with no excess over Omori cascade triggering, and there is no low-b asperity -- so the short-term FTLS b/rate seismicity channel does not capture even this megathrust nucleation, whose documented precursors (slow slip, migration, a ~270-day up-dip acceleration, a multi-year b-decline) are aseismic / long-timescale / at or below open resolution rather than absent. Synthesising the five b-value-channel cases: the durable result is that the incompleteness-robust b-positive shows no FTLS-magnitude foreshock drop in any case (foreshock-vs-aftershock ~ -5%, CI-overlapping, against the -27% to -34% Aki RED relative to a quiescent background), a controlled split-half experiment shows the Aki RED is sufficiently explained by an analysis-completeness artifact (sharp elevated completeness collapses Aki 0.88 to 0.18 while b-positive holds), and the one new methodological point is that b-positive -- robust to a sharp completeness threshold -- is only approximately robust to a smooth roll-off (biased low, in the conservative direction for these nulls). A population test then generalised this beyond the case series: across ~150 SoCal M>=4 mainshocks (QTM catalogue) there is no robust FTLS foreshock b-RED -- the apparent drop is background-definition-dependent (a large Aki RED and even a b-positive sign-flip against a tectonically heterogeneous whole-region background, collapsing to a small ~ -6% b-positive decrease against a spatially-matched background, itself partly incompleteness), with a >10% RED in only ~25-30% of individual sequences. Finally the SPATIAL channel breaks the run of nulls: on the SoCal QTM population, foreshocks migrate weakly but significantly and SPECIFICALLY toward the eventual mainshock hypocentre (mean Spearman rho ~ -0.1, mainshock-specific vs centroid/random, magnitude-controlled, 3D-robust, opposite to aftershock Omori expansion, and absent from a synthetic no-migration null), a real if operationally-modest precursor where the magnitude and rate channels carry none. It replicates in an independent region and catalogue (Northern California DD, rho -0.113, p<1e-4), confirming it is not SoCal-specific; the one offshore subduction test is resolution-limited (fails the aftershock-expansion gate). The open-data nucleation arc is thus null across geodesy, near-field waveforms, and the catalogue magnitude/rate channels, with ONE exception: a weak, operationally-modest, three-faulting-style-replicated foreshock-MIGRATION tendency in the spatial channel (later foreshocks lie nearer the eventual hypocentre; ~0.1 km/day net, no location-forecast skill, mechanism-agnostic). Further progress on detection requires denser or restricted data; the spatial-migration signal is the one open-data positive and the main remaining lever. A dedicated forecast-AUC ceiling study (research/RESULT_oef_auc_ceiling.md) then decomposed the operational M5+ skill honestly: the headline AUC is ~98 percent spatial climatology (cell position alone scores 0.861), real skill stripped of easy negatives is ~0.76 (active cells) to 0.81 (1-3 day aftershock window), and the tested features and resolutions did not exceed this baseline (identifying inputs that do remains the open objective) -- deep-learnable structural and aftershock-physics features add +0.0001-0.0002, a 70x-denser JMA catalogue (1.4M events) leaves the driver-density AUC flat, and finer resolution reaches AUC 0.9011 at 0.5 deg only as a hollow easy-negative artifact (BSS goes negative, uncured by isotonic calibration); the genuine probabilistically-skilful peak is AUC 0.895 at 1 deg (BSS +0.042). Breaking this catalogue ceiling requires information the catalogue discards -- the frontier direction is waveform foundation-model embeddings (SeisLM) + lab-to-Earth acoustic-emission transfer on continuous Hi-net/S-net data, an unproven high-failure-odds bet but the only out-of-distribution lever.

## 2026-07 update

**2026-07 update**: that frontier bet was run to completion, and single-axis it failed - but the paradigm break behind it did not. Every remaining open modality returned a leak-safe null against the catalog ceiling when tested one axis at a time: pretrained SeisLM waveform embeddings on 600 M5+/matched-control windows (four analysis angles at a 0.5-day gap, then a minimal-gap rerun with windows ending 15 minutes before the mainshock, including a foreshock-absent split - all null); tidal stress (real, textbook shallow-depth triggering physics, Schuster p~4e-4 confined to depth <=40 km, but zero forecast skill); slow earthquakes (Nankai deep tremor and off-Tohoku VLFE, both null, including the trench cells where catalog ML itself is near-chance); repeating-earthquake creep acceleration (a real confound-controlled precursor, Monte-Carlo p~0.001 incl. pre-2011-only, but no significant AUC contribution over ordinary seismicity acceleration); the exotic channels measured in-house rather than taken on literature authority (solar/geomagnetic/cosmic-ray OMNI indices, ionospheric TEC, outgoing-longwave radiation - all null); and S-net continuous ocean-bottom pressure directly above the hypocentres (NIED-approved data, leak-safe windows ending ~10 minutes before origin: 0/10 coherent near-field transients, Mann-Whitney p=0.99). The single-axis paradigm itself was then questioned: fusing all modalities into one nonlinear joint representation (33 co-registered local features plus SeisLM conv and transformer embeddings, gradient boosting, paired bootstrap) produces the project first robust catalog-ceiling break in the prospective-operational frame - FUSION minus ETAS +0.107 (95% CI +0.048..+0.163, p<0.001, positive under all five CV seeds), with the waveform marginal contribution over the 33 local features also significant (+0.079, p=0.002). The matched-control frame is borderline (+0.059, one-sided p=0.033); a window expansion from 600 to the full 1456-window universe is running to decide it. Group permutation importance confirms the break is genuinely waveform-driven (conv +0.052 and transformer +0.035 in the matched-control frame, ETAS a co-equal pillar, and every exotic channel at zero inside the fusion as well). Single-axis nulls and a joint positive are consistent: the interaction does not show up in per-modality delta-AUC. Research code and derived results live in the private research repo linked below.

## 2026-07-03 correction (leak audit)

**2026-07-03 correction (leak audit)**: a follow-up control battery found temporal-sampling leakage in the matched-window design itself: negatives sit only minutes away in time from some positive (near-twins) while remaining unbalanced across years, so a bare date index alone scores AUC 0.61-0.65. Under location-grouped cross-validation the twin pair is always split across folds, so the training twin's timestamp points at the held-out label - a structural leak that inflates any date-encoding feature in the matched-control frame. The borderline matched-control result above is therefore withdrawn. The operational-frame break, however, survives the strictest control available: on date-caliper-matched pairs where the date-only model drops to AUC 0.508, fusion still beats the catalog baseline by +0.082 (95% CI +0.026..+0.140, p=0.001) and the waveform marginal stays significant (+0.064, p=0.016) - so the headline stands in its honest form: a waveform x catalog fusion gain that a pure date model cannot reproduce. A clean redesign is now in place and verified: positives are paired into swaps (i<->j) so each negative reuses one positive's exact timestamp and the other's epicentre, with cross-validation folds cut along the resulting quads - a naive version of this idea actually anti-leaks (a bare date model scores 0.24 when twins straddle folds), which the swap-quad construction eliminates exactly (date-only = location-only = 0.5000 on the final design). In this provably clean frame the local 33-feature joint model does not beat the catalog baseline (delta -0.003, n.s.), so the decisive re-test - waveform fusion with only local waveform content able to separate twins - has now returned null. On the clean swap-quad frame (n=1388 windows, 347 complete quads) fusion no longer beats the catalog baseline: FUSION minus ETAS +0.0147 (95% CI -0.021..+0.050, bootstrap p=0.222; within-quad permutation-null p=0.293, NPERM=300; every foreshock-absent subgroup n.s.). Both earlier positives are therefore retracted as sampling leak - the operational-frame +0.082 was residual shared-day leakage that the swap-quad removes mechanically - so SeisLM waveform embeddings add no leak-free short-term M5+ skill over the catalog-ETAS ceiling, closing the multimodal joint arc consistent with every single-axis null. 

## 2026-07-06 update (offshore geodesy and borehole channels)

**2026-07-06 update (offshore geodesy and borehole channels).** Two further independent channels below the catalog ceiling were tested and are null. (i) Campaign GNSS-A seafloor geodesy -- the Japan Coast Guard SGO-A array (27 sites along the Japan Trench and Nankai Trough), the instrument a network-design calculation identified as the only open system reaching the offshore ~Mw6 precursor floor -- shows no coherent pre-event seafloor velocity transient before the ten M6.5-7.3 offshore earthquakes with site coverage (per-event coseismic-removed, matched-control; 0/15 event-site pairs significant, within-quad permutation consistent, and a synthetic-injection floor places the null at Mw~6, matching the network-design estimate). (ii) IODP/DONET borehole formation pore pressure (Holes C0002 and C0010) does resolve the Mw6 shallow slow-slip events that campaign GNSS-A cannot (1-minute sampling, inter-site migration), but on the dense JMA unified catalogue those SSEs do not elevate local seismicity above natural clustering variability (2015 and 2020 events, empirical p = 0.20 and 0.83 against the distribution of 30-day window rates). With these, every openly-accessible independent channel -- catalogue, waveform foundation-model, tidal, slow earthquakes, repeaters, exotic geophysical indices, ocean-bottom pressure, onshore GNSS, offshore seafloor GNSS-A, and borehole pore pressure -- is null above the genuine catalogue forecast ceiling (~AUC 0.895), did not exceed the genuine catalog forecast baseline (~AUC 0.895); these are channels that were tested and did not add skill, not a demonstrated ceiling, and the search for inputs that break the baseline continues. The one channel not exhausted here -- the multi-year continuous borehole record, which would only add more slow-slip events to the already-powered null coupling test rather than change the conclusion -- is presently inaccessible: the documented JAMSTEC J-SEIS/JOIN portal and its mirror no longer resolve, so it would require a direct data request rather than open download.

## 2026-07-09 update (sequence-escalation reframe)

**2026-07-09 update (sequence-escalation reframe).** A structurally different target was adopted after the aftershock-triggering/space-forecast literature review showed that axis is fundamentally distance-confounded at the same easy-negative floor as the rest of this project (Mignan 2019 arXiv:1904.01983; Sharma 2020; Iturrieta 2023 NHESS). The new target is a binary discriminator on ONGOING sequences: given an independent M>=5.0 mainshock, does a strong subsequent event (within one magnitude unit) occur, following NESTORE (Gentili et al. 2024, arXiv:2408.12956). Negatives here are real sequences that never escalated rather than empty distant cells, so the easy-negative trap is avoided by construction. A from-scratch reproduction on the JMA catalogue (1097 independent mainshocks, Gardner-Knopoff declustering, train 2011-2019 / test 2020-2024) reproduced the published NESTORE regime (6h-post-mainshock precision 0.69 / recall 0.73 / AUC 0.879, vs. published precision 0.75 / recall 0.75). A follow-up honesty check found this headline AUC is dominated by cases where the escalation has already occurred within the same 6h window (39 of 64 test-set positives, 61%); restricting to the 25 positives that escalate strictly after 6h (the genuine forecast target) drops AUC to 0.694, the honest number to beat. Four structural levers were then tested against this forecast-only metric: (i) within-window sequence-dynamics features (Omori acceleration ratio, aftershock-centroid migration, a foreshock-migration-geometry analogue applied to the early aftershock cluster, within-window b-value trend) raised it to 0.712; (ii) the Gulia-Wiemer FTLS pre-mainshock-vs-background b-value contrast added a further +0.009 in combination (negligible alone); (iii) NIED F-net moment-tensor-based Coulomb stress-transfer (dCFS) features, matched to 937 of the 1097 mainshocks (85% coverage, vs. under 5% that would be available from the now-defunct SRCMOD finite-fault catalogue), added +0.012 alone. All four deltas are bootstrap-confirmed directionally positive (P(delta>0) 0.73-0.85) but not significant at the conventional 95% threshold given the small (n=25) forecast-only positive class -- the shared bottleneck across every lever tried so far is this sample size, not feature engineering. Research code and derivation notes (including a documented Okada Green's-function sign-convention debugging trace, resolved against the original DC3D.f source rather than recalled convention) live in the private research repo; this arc remains open, with ETAS-based probabilistic declustering and a wider evaluation window as the next steps.

## 2026-07-10 update (fifth lever: ETAS stochastic declustering -- the n=25 bottleneck addressed; the levers remain directionally positive but unconfirmed)

**2026-07-10 update (fifth lever: ETAS stochastic declustering -- the n=25 bottleneck addressed; the levers remain directionally positive but unconfirmed).** The declustering step was upgraded from deterministic Gardner-Knopoff to ETAS-probabilistic (space-time-magnitude ETAS fit on the training period only, applied causally) and the mainshock threshold extended from M5.0 to M4.5, expanding the sample to 3,907 independent mainshocks and the forecast-only test positives from 25 to 144 (a 5.8x power gain). The headline is an object lesson in sample-definition sensitivity, caught by adversarial review: a first (loose) implementation normalised the bigger-parent trigger probability by total intensity, which dense swarms of small nearby triggers dilute -- it admitted 37% of 2011 Tohoku-zone M5+ aftershocks as independent mainshocks and produced an apparently significant combined-lever gain (forecast-only AUC 0.697 to 0.742, delta +0.046, 95% CI [+0.024,+0.069]). Tightening the rule (bigger-parent triggering measured against background only; Tohoku contamination 221 to 31 of 600) collapses the gain back to the familiar pattern: delta +0.018, CI [-0.007,+0.042]. Across five overlapping evaluations the dynamics+FTLS levers are positive every time (+0.013 to +0.046) -- a consistent weak lever of roughly +0.02, not the structural jump this target needs; the loose-rule significance was carried by sequence-embedded events (aftershocks of larger events), a causally valid but different population on which early-sequence features genuinely discriminate escalation better. Both declustering rules are heuristics on a non-fully-converged ETAS fit (caveats recorded alongside the numbers). The arc remains open; the next candidate reframings are magnitude-of-next-event regression and the low-frequency/high-signal M6+ target.

## update 2 (2026-07-10)

**2026-07-10 update 2 (sixth round: three reframed targets, all null or infeasible after a false-negative audit).** Three target reframings were tested on the strict ETAS-declustered sample: (a) magnitude-of-next-event regression (graded target, scored as a ranker on the same honest forecast-only task) -- no gain over the binary classifier once tested with fair instruments (balanced weights, quantile loss; delta -0.000, CI [-0.038,+0.037]); (b) an M6+ rare high-signal target -- infeasible on this sample and period (only 4-10 forecast-only test positives; a power fact, not a ceiling claim -- pre-2011 JMA deck backfill could grow it); (c) a 1-day feature window instead of 6h -- null, and confirmed null after an adversarial audit caught a real training-population confound (realized-in-window contamination 32% at 6h vs 48% at 1d) and the retest with matched training still showed no gain (delta -0.021, CI [-0.059,+0.015]): the extra 18 hours of observation adds precisely the trivial already-escalated signal the honest metric excludes, leaving forecast-only AUC at ~0.68 at either horizon. Every audit claim was independently recomputed from the saved arrays before being acted on (all reproduced). Untested levers remain queued: ETAS-context features at forecast time (background vs triggered probability, trigger intensity -- already computed, strictly causal), the 15/30-day label-window variants, Coulomb-feature extension to the expanded M4.5+ sample, and pre-2011 catalogue backfill.

## update 3 (2026-07-10)

**2026-07-10 update 3 (seventh round: ETAS-context features -- the first lever whose bootstrap CI excludes zero on the clean sample).** Five strictly-causal features describing the ETAS declustering state at forecast time (background intensity, background probability, bigger-parent probability, and the total and bigger-parent triggering intensities at t0, all from the train-period-only fit and events strictly before the mainshock) were added to the 16 static features. Result on the strict M4.5+ sample (144 forecast-only test positives): honest forecast-only AUC 0.682 to 0.719 (delta +0.037, 95% CI [+0.007,+0.069], P(d>0)=0.994); the single feature log-trigger-intensity alone does better still, 0.682 to 0.727 (+0.045, CI [+0.015,+0.077], P=0.999). Diagnostics rule out the obvious artifacts: the pure-location background feature alone adds only +0.010 (so this is not a spatial-climatology re-encoding); the gain is larger, not smaller, on the M5+ band (+0.066 vs +0.028); and -- the reverse of round 5's cautionary tale -- it concentrates on the cleanest subpopulation (mainshocks the deterministic Gardner-Knopoff rule would also call independent: +0.063, CI [+0.025,+0.103]) while being absent on GK-rejected swarm-adjacent admits (-0.023, n.s.). Adversarial review cleared all four attack surfaces, with a decisive leaky-contrast test: an otherwise-identical variant of the feature allowed to see 7 days into the future scores +0.122, an order of magnitude above the observed +0.045, which sits exactly at the causal signature; every review number was independently re-run and reproduced before acceptance. Sober context: 0.727 is real, verified skill but still far from the 0.90 goal, and the lower CI bound is thin. The natural escalation, queued next: feed the classifier the canonical ETAS forward forecast itself -- the expected rate of a within-one-magnitude event over the remaining 7-day window, computed from everything known at t0+6h including the mainshock and its early aftershocks -- which no current feature encodes.

## update 4 (2026-07-10)

**2026-07-10 update 4 (eighth round: the canonical ETAS forward forecast as a feature -- strongest verified lever of the arc).** The classifier was given the quantity the literature treats as the benchmark forecaster and that no existing feature encoded: the ETAS-expected number of within-one-magnitude events over the remaining 7-day window and GK disc, computed from everything known at t0+6h (prior events, the mainshock itself, and its early aftershocks as productivity-weighted parents; train-only parameters, KDE and Gutenberg-Richter b throughout). Honest forecast-only AUC rises 0.682 to 0.732 with the three forward-forecast features alone (delta +0.050, 95% CI [+0.025,+0.075], P(d>0)=1.000) and to 0.737 with the full verified stack -- and the raw single forecast number, with no other features at all, scores 0.709, beating the entire 16-feature baseline. Robustness: the gain is uniform across magnitude bands (+0.049 / +0.053) and again concentrates on the cleanest Gardner-Knopoff-independent subpopulation (+0.053, CI [+0.022,+0.085]). Verification followed the now-standard protocol with all review numbers independently re-run: an exact from-scratch recompute of the features (max difference 0.0), a leaky-contrast bound (letting parents see the full window scores 0.963, so the shipped 0.731 sits at the causal signature, and the harness demonstrably catches such leaks), and a re-encoding probe (five nonlinear transforms of the existing aftershock counts add nothing, 0.684 -- the ETAS productivity weighting and Omori forward projection carry information the counts cannot express). Honest flags: the increment over the seventh-round context features is thin (+0.015, CI touching zero -- the two levers are facets of the same ETAS state, with the forward forecast the better representation); the forecast is ~2x under-calibrated in count (monotone, irrelevant to rank skill) and noisier out-of-sample. Cumulative verified progress on the honest gap: 0.682 to 0.737. Next escalation, queued: per-sequence parameter adaptation (Omi-style real-time updating of productivity from the first six hours around the train-fit prior), the 15/30-day label windows, the Coulomb extension to M4.5+, and pre-2011 catalogue backfill.

## update 5 (2026-07-10)

**2026-07-10 update 5 (ninth round: per-sequence productivity adaptation -- null over the generic forecast, with the failure mechanism identified).** The natural escalation of round 8 was tried: adapt each sequence's forward forecast to its own observed first-six-hours aftershock count via a Gamma-Poisson update of a per-sequence productivity multiplier (prior mean 1 around the train-fit parameters, strictly causal throughout). The adapted features are strong against the raw baseline (+0.046 -- they embed the round-8 forecast) but add nothing over it: -0.006 versus the generic forward forecast, CI [-0.014,+0.003], and the full stack is unchanged (0.738 vs 0.737). A false-negative audit confirmed the null is real rather than a tooling choice: it holds at every prior strength (0.3/1/3), the multiplier alone carries almost no label information (AUC 0.561), and the mechanism is identifiable in the data -- median observed M3.5+ count in the first six hours is zero all the way to M6.5 mainshocks while the predicted count rises to ~3, i.e. short-term aftershock incompleteness suppresses the observed count magnitude-dependently, so the simple counting multiplier is dominated by a detection artifact exactly where adaptation would matter, and what survives detection was already a static feature. Recorded as a null for this simple counting formulation, not for real-time adaptation as a concept -- the principled revisit would estimate productivity jointly with a detection-rate model on the magnitude-time stream (the full Omi et al. treatment). Honest-gap state after rounds seven through nine: 0.682 to 0.737, all verified.

## update 6 (2026-07-10)

**2026-07-10 update 6 (tenth round: the Coulomb-stress lever retested at full power -- decisively null, resolving the last underpowered lever).** The round-two Coulomb/CRS features had scored +0.012 with a CI spanning zero on only 25 forecast-only positives. The F-net moment-tensor catalogue was refetched down to M4.5 (6,160 solutions across 2011-2024, per-year queries through the established headless-browser route), matching 82.6% of the 3,907 strict-sample mainshocks, and the previously verified Okada/Coulomb pipeline was rerun at 144-positive power. The result is as clean a null as the method can produce: +0.000 against the baseline (CI [-0.015,+0.014], P(d>0) exactly 0.500), slightly negative as an addition to the forward forecast, and unchanged in the full stack. A coverage-dilution audit rules out the obvious false negative -- restricted to the covered subpopulation (67 positives) the delta is still +0.007 with a CI spanning zero. The round-two +0.012 is thereby resolved as small-sample noise, for this formulation (point-source patch, self-similar receiver, six-hour aftershock sampling); richer stress modelling (finite-fault slip, secondary triggering, rate-state dependence) remains a different and heavier untested lever. Every lever from the 25-positive era now has a powered verdict: dynamics and b-value features are a consistent weak ~+0.02, Coulomb is null, and the verified stack stands at forecast-only AUC 0.737 -- the ETAS state and forward-forecast features carrying the gain. Next queued: pre-2011 catalogue backfill (to grow the rare M6+ target), the 15/30-day label windows, and detection-corrected productivity adaptation.

## update 7 (2026-07-10)

**2026-07-10 update 7 (eleventh round: the catalogue extended back to 2000 -- verified best rises to 0.752, and the rare M6+ target becomes evaluable with real signal).** Eleven years of pre-2011 JMA hypocentre decks were added (completeness checked per year: no cliff at the M3.5 driver band), the ETAS model refit on training data only -- now converging properly (nit=690, spatial decay q=1.66 versus the old non-converged fit's suspicious 12.7) -- and the whole verified pipeline rebuilt on the doubled sample. Two results. First, with the same 2020+ test period, the full stack rises from 0.737 to 0.752 (delta versus baseline +0.061, CI [+0.028,+0.095]); the review's decisive check, independently re-run, shows +0.016 on the 814 test events common to both samples, so the gain comes from better training rather than sample redefinition, and the ETAS levers replicate on a third sample and an independent fit. Second, moving the split to 2014 buys a ten-year test window (401 forecast-only positives, with the stack replicating again: +0.033, CI [+0.015,+0.051]) and makes the high-consequence M6+ target evaluable for the first time: for sequences whose strong follow-up reaches M6, the stack lifts forecast-only AUC 0.683 to 0.796 (+0.113, CI [+0.037,+0.194]), and on M6+ mainshocks -- where the static baseline has literally no skill, 0.483 -- it reaches 0.674 (+0.191, CI [+0.068,+0.323]). Sizing honestly, per adversarial review with every number re-run independently: the effect is jackknife-stable (no single event carries it), but the follow-up variant is about half driven by the Taiwan/Yaeyama cluster (outside it +0.053 on eight positives), while the mainshock variant is better distributed (+0.167 without Taiwan). Next: walk-forward multi-split fits (2012/2016/2018 alongside the existing 2014/2020) to accumulate strictly out-of-sample M6+ positives across 2012-2023 and size the rare-event effect properly.

## update 8 (2026-07-11)

**2026-07-11 update 8 (twelfth round: walk-forward multi-split -- the ETAS levers and the M6+ mainshock effect replicate strictly out-of-sample, with pooled honest sizing).** The round-eleven follow-up was executed: three further ETAS fits (2012/2016/2018 splits, each trained strictly before its split year, all converging in the same parameter range as the existing 2014/2020 fits), features and classifiers rebuilt per fold, giving five non-overlapping test windows spanning 2012-2024 and 575 pooled forecast-only positives (versus 144 in the original single split). Pooled with within-fold percentile normalisation -- and cross-checked with a normalisation-free per-fold aggregation that agrees to three decimals -- the full stack beats the baseline by +0.047 (95% CI [+0.031,+0.062], P(d>0)=1.000) on all forecast-only positives: the strongest replication of the arc's levers so far, strictly out-of-sample in every window. The rare-event verdicts, sized honestly at last: the M6+ mainshock effect is real but smaller than the round-eleven single-split estimate suggested -- pooled +0.094 (CI [+0.023,+0.171], P=0.996), positive in all four evaluable folds, surviving Taiwan-cluster exclusion (+0.081), leave-one-positive-out jackknife, and a Bonferroni correction across the six reported strata (one-sided p 0.005, x6 = 0.030); the M6+ follow-up variant, by contrast, does not survive pooling (+0.056 with the CI crossing zero, and only +0.025 outside the Taiwan/Yaeyama cluster) and is recorded as unestablished. Adversarial review attacked the leak surfaces (per-fold fit and train boundaries verified exact in the shared time convention), the pooling method (replaced entirely with per-fold raw-score aggregation, same answer), double-counting (3,261 pooled events, zero cross-fold duplicates), and multiplicity, and found nothing; every review number was independently re-run and reproduced before acceptance. Honest caveats stand: fourteen M6+ mainshock positives is still a thin class, the baseline sits near chance on it, and the single-positive 2020 fold is negative. State of the honest gap: verified single-split best 0.752, and the stack's skill now confirmed at every scale tested. Queued next: growing the M6+ class further (extending the catalogue back before 2000, subject to per-year completeness checks), the 15/30-day label windows, and detection-corrected productivity adaptation.

## update 9 (2026-07-11)

**2026-07-11 update 9 (thirteenth round: the label-window reframe -- the lever replicates at every horizon, and the 30-day task posts the arc's best absolute number).** The target window itself was made a parameter: alongside the 7-day definition, the same walk-forward harness was rerun with 15- and 30-day strong-follow-up windows (labels recomputed independently from the raw catalogue during review: zero mismatches on 250 samples), plus a variant with the ETAS forward-forecast feature's integration horizon matched to each target. Three findings. First, the ETAS lever replicates strictly out-of-sample at every horizon -- +0.038/+0.041 (15d) and +0.031 (30d), each P(d>0)=1.000, positive in all five folds at every variant -- so the round-12 conclusion is not an artifact of the 7-day label choice. Second, the 30-day task reaches pooled walk-forward honest forecast-only AUC 0.796 (1,190 positives), the best absolute number of the arc -- framed honestly: it is an easier target (40% positive rate, static baseline already 0.765), so this is not improved skill; the skill delta in fact shrinks with window, partly genuine near-horizon information decay (the Omori-weighted 7-day forecast already carries most of the 30-day information: horizon-matching adds +0.003 at 15 days and nothing at 30) and partly mechanical baseline compression, per the review's accepted caveat. Third, the M6+ strata do not extend to longer windows (all CIs cross zero; recorded unestablished) -- the established rare-event result remains the 7-day M6+ mainshock gain of round 12. The review also surfaced a structural honesty property worth recording: the realized-by-6h exclusion discards 200 thirty-day positives whose qualifying event genuinely occurs after the feature window -- early-loud sequences where the 6h features are most informative -- so the honest metric is, by construction, conservative against the model. Queued next: growing the M6+ class (catalogue extension before 2000, subject to per-year completeness), and detection-corrected productivity adaptation.

## update 10 (2026-07-11)

**2026-07-11 update 10 (fourteenth round: the catalogue extended to 1990 and the walk-forward doubled to ten folds -- the main lever survives its hardest test yet, and the rare-event stratum is honestly downgraded).** Ten more years of JMA hypocentre decks were added (1997 ships as two half-year files; month-coverage verified continuous), the extended cache's post-2000 portion verified multiset-identical to the reviewed catalogue, and the per-year completeness gate passed (1990s Mc 1.0-2.4, far below the M3.5 driver band). Five further ETAS fits (2002-2010 splits, all converged; parameters shift smoothly into the sparser-network training era, train-only so leak-irrelevant) yield five new test folds, making ten non-overlapping windows spanning 2002-2024 with 946 pooled forecast-only positives. Two results, in opposite directions and both worth recording. First, the main ETAS lever posts +0.047 (CI [+0.035,+0.058], P(d>0)=1.000), identical to the third decimal in the 2002-2011 and 2012-2024 eras separately and positive in all ten folds -- the strongest replication this catalogue span can offer. Second, the M6+ mainshock stratum, at 34 positives (2.4x the round-12 power), drops to +0.043 with the CI crossing zero and is downgraded to unestablished: the +0.191 (round 11) -> +0.094 (round 12) -> +0.043 shrinkage is the classic signature of small-sample optimism, and the formal era-difference test (late-minus-early +0.087, CI [-0.012,+0.189], p=0.083) says the apparent early/late gap is itself consistent with noise -- one homogeneous ~+0.04 effect, not era heterogeneity. The review attacked in both directions -- gain artifacts and dilution-as-bug -- and cleared both: the early-era positives are real events, the famous absences (Tokachi-oki 2003, Chuetsu 2004, Tohoku 2011) are the honest metric correctly excluding sequences whose escalation had already begun within the six-hour feature window, and the ETAS features are non-degenerate with era-matched distributions; every review number was independently re-run and reproduced. Where this leaves the rare-event goal: decks exist back to at least 1983, but the shrinkage pattern argues the deeper lever is methodological -- borrowing strength from the abundant sub-M6 class (hierarchical/transfer formulations) rather than only adding years. Queued: that, and detection-corrected productivity adaptation.

## update 11 (2026-07-11)

**2026-07-11 update 11 (fifteenth round: magnitude-weighted training for the rare-event stratum -- decisively harmful, with the mechanism identified).** The first methodological lever for the M6+ class was tested: retraining the full stack with Gutenberg-Richter-informed sample weights (10^(beta(Mm-4.5)), beta 0.5 and 1.0) to emphasise the sparse large-magnitude regime, against the unweighted reference -- whose scores were first verified bit-identical to the round-14 dumps, so the measured deltas are purely the weighting effect. The result is a clean dose-dependent harm: on the pre-registered primary metric (pooled M6+ union, 54 positives) the weighted model scores -0.052 (P(worse)=0.997) at beta=0.5 and -0.090 (CI [-0.144,-0.036]) at beta=1.0, with the M6+ mainshock stratum dropping as far as -0.136; the all-forecast-only guard degrades too at the stronger dose. The mechanism is textbook and quantified: the effective training sample collapses from 5,644 to 1,548 at beta=0.5 and to eight (the top ten events carrying 48% of total weight) at beta=1.0 -- upweighting a sparse regime discards the abundant sub-M6 signal without adding generalisable large-magnitude information. This negative resolves the within-catalogue reweighting family and reinforces the round-14 framing: the escalation signal is homogeneous across magnitude, and the M6+ class is short on statistics, not on emphasis. Remaining levers for the rare-event goal, queued: extending the catalogue to 1983 (decks confirmed to exist; the per-year completeness gate is the deciding check, as the 1980s network was sparser), and detection-corrected productivity adaptation.

## update 12 (2026-07-11)

**2026-07-11 update 12 (sixteenth round: the catalogue reaches 1983 and the walk-forward reaches thirteen folds -- the lever is confirmed magnitude-uniform, era-uniform, and the queued adaptation lever is closed).** Seven more deck years were added (the 1990-onward portion of the new cache verified multiset-identical to the reviewed one; the 1983 completeness sits at the M3.5 driver-band edge and is recorded as a caveat, though the MC=3.5 gate keeps sub-threshold incompleteness out of every feature), three further pre-2002 fits converged (their Omori p dips below 1 in the sparse-network era; the integral form was verified well-behaved), and three new test folds make thirteen non-overlapping windows tiling 1996-2024 exactly -- 7,195 test events, zero duplicates, 1,151 forecast-only positives. The main ETAS lever posts +0.044 (CI [+0.033,+0.055], P(d>0)=1.000), positive in all thirteen folds (sign-test p~1e-4, cluster-robust beyond the pooled bootstrap). The rare-event strata are now best summarised by the homogeneity tests: both M6+ strata are statistically indistinguishable from the single magnitude-uniform ~+0.044 effect (differences n.s. at P=0.687/0.383). The M6+ follow-up stratum does cross nominal significance (+0.055, P=0.993), but the review's decomposition -- independently re-run -- shows the crossing is carried by four sparse-era 1996-2000 positives scoring +0.200 while the 2002-2020 folds alone remain CI-crossing-zero: it is recorded as consistent with the uniform effect, not as a rare-event discovery, and the honest reading of "uniform" is "no premium larger than ~0.05 detected", not "proven equal". Separately, the long-queued adaptation lever was closed: the detection-corrected (Omi/Helmstetter) per-sequence productivity update -- whose first implementation used the wrong completeness convention and was caught inert by a mechanism audit before any conclusion -- scores -0.010 (CI [-0.018,-0.002]) against the generic forward forecast once corrected, the same null as the simple counting version, and the detection model demonstrably cannot explain the moderate-mainshock count deficit that motivated it. The verified honest state of this formulation: pooled walk-forward 0.753 (7-day window), single-split best 0.752, thirty-day window 0.796. Queued next: tectonic-context features from the Slab2 subduction geometry model (grids already staged and read-verified; 88% epicentre coverage, the open question being incremental value beyond the spatial climatology the model already holds).

## update 13 (2026-07-11)

**2026-07-11 update 13 (eighteenth round: Slab2 tectonic-context features -- a well-powered null).** The staged tectonic lever was executed: four static features from the USGS Slab2 subduction-geometry model (slab depth at the epicentre, the event's depth relative to the slab surface, slab dip, and a no-coverage flag -- Nankai lies outside Slab2 and enters as native missing values), computed for all thirteen sample sets at 88-90% coverage, with univariate label correlations around -0.15 suggesting real marginal structure. Added to the full stack in the same thirteen-fold walk-forward, they change nothing: -0.002 on all forecast-only positives with a tight CI [-0.006,+0.003] at 1,151 positives, and every M6+ stratum flat. The audit confirms this is a genuine null rather than a plumbing artifact -- the reference reproduces the round-16 scores bit-identically while the variant's scores differ on every test event, so the models demonstrably used the features and extracted nothing incremental: the spatial background and location/depth features already carry whatever tectonic information this target can use. The magnitude-weighting (round 15), adaptation (rounds 9/17), Coulomb (round 10), and now slab-geometry families are all resolved; the verified state stands at pooled walk-forward 0.753 (7-day), single-split 0.752, thirty-day window 0.796, with the ETAS forward-forecast/context levers the only confirmed skill source beyond the static baseline. Queued next, the largest untested axis inside this formulation: replacing the hand-crafted feature representation itself with a learned sequence model over the raw pre-history and first-six-hours event streams.

## update 14 (2026-07-11)

**2026-07-11 update 14 (nineteenth round: a learned sequence representation -- the blend is null, and the null is informative).** The largest untested axis inside the formulation was probed: a small two-stream GRU trained per fold on the raw event streams themselves (the first six hours inside the Gardner-Knopoff disc, and thirty days of pre-history within twice the radius), with temporal early stopping inside the training period and a fixed a-priori 50/50 rank-blend against the gradient-boosted full stack as the pre-registered primary. The blend changes nothing: +0.001 (CI [-0.006,+0.008]) on 1,151 pooled forecast-only positives, all M6+ strata flat, with the reference again verified bit-identical to the round-16 scores. The informative part is the network alone: from raw streams, with no hand-crafted features at all, it reaches pooled 0.722 -- above the 16-feature static baseline (0.709), most of the way to the full ETAS stack (0.753), and fold-competitive with it in four of thirteen windows. Two independent representation families -- hand-crafted seismological features plus ETAS model quantities, and a from-scratch learned encoder -- thus converge on the same skill band and share almost all their information, which argues the formulation is not representation-bottlenecked at this model scale (a caveat stands: a GPU-scale sequence model is a different, untested regime). The practical conclusion recorded for the arc: further gains most plausibly require new information rather than better encoding of the same catalogue streams -- with cross-region training catalogues (Southern California, Italy, and other open regional networks, used to augment training while keeping the Japan-only honest evaluation untouched) the leading queued lever.

## update 15 (2026-07-11)

**2026-07-11 update 15 (twentieth round: cross-region training augmentation from New Zealand -- a marginal null that sharpens the diagnosis).** The "new information" lever was executed with the nearest feature-compatible donor: the full GeoNet catalogue (106k events after box and type filtering; completeness verified below the M3.5 driver band), a New-Zealand ETAS fit on 1990-2005 only (converging to a genuinely different regime -- productivity exponent 0.96, spatial scale 14 km2 versus Japan's 0.5-0.7 and ~0.6), and the identical sequence/feature pipeline, augmenting the training set of the eight post-2006 folds while leaving the Japan-only honest evaluation untouched (no label leak is possible from a foreign region by construction; the Japan-only reference again reproduces the prior scores bit-identically). Doubling the training positives this way moves the pooled honest AUC by +0.005 (CI [-0.001,+0.011], P=0.949) -- a positive lean short of significance -- while the M6+ strata lean slightly negative (n.s.), and a region-indicator variant produces bit-identical scores because no tree ever splits on it: the model itself judges the regional distinction uninformative in this feature space. Recorded as a marginal null with honest caveats (single donor, magnitude-scale mismatch, and only ~1,800 donor rows at the late splits); the hundredfold-larger global NEIC catalogue (216k M4.5+ events, existence probed) remains the untested scale escalation of this family. Together with round 19, the diagnosis sharpens: the current feature space appears signal-saturated by the Japan catalogue alone -- the next gains must come from either genuinely new measurement channels or a structurally different target, and the arc continues on that basis.

## update 16 (2026-07-11)
