The reading: hand-built multi-station coherence of sub-catalog residual energy adds no escalation skill at this coverage, the only weak structure is a microseism artifact the immune channel refutes, and 22 positives cannot exclude a true couple-of-hundredths effect -- a mapped negative on the cheap multi-station version, explicitly not a ceiling. What remains genuinely untested is the heavier form the sweep has pointed to twice now: a learned multi-station embedding or waveform deep learning on GPU rather than hand-built coherence, dense Hi-net boreholes for weak-signal sensitivity behind their registration gate, and explicit catalog-rate detrending -- the fork pending a Hi-net data-access decision. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 42 (2026-07-24)

**2026-07-24 update 42 (a 102-agent adversarial literature search retires the learned multi-station waveform model as redundant-by-construction and points instead to post-foreshock nucleation physics; the cheap open-network version of that lever is then built and tested same-day, and returns a clean null with no usable escalation signal -- the envelope shape carries real univariate signal but the catalogue already has it).** With hand-built multi-station coherence null (update 41), the question was whether the heavier learned version -- a graph/attention model over raw multi-station waveforms -- was worth a multi-session GPU build. A deep, adversarially-verified search (102 agents, 25 load-bearing claims through three-vote refutation, 22 confirmed) answered no: there is no published hard-negative-controlled, leak-safe evidence that a learned cross-station model extracts pre-mainshock escalation information that single-station or foundation-embedding methods miss. Every multi-station deep-learning success is either a monitoring task (the PLAN and GRAPES graph networks genuinely beat single-station pickers at phase-picking, association, location and wavefield propagation, but those are event-characterisation tasks with no forward escalation label and no fizzled-sequence negatives) or a retrospective discrimination-with-leakage classifier (the Norcia CNN, SeisLM, the Gulia-Wiemer traffic light) where the mainshock is baked into the ground truth; and a decisive refuted claim showed single-station models match the full network on the one waveform classification task tested, so cross-station aggregation is not the differentiator. The catalogue frontier was independently re-confirmed as ETAS-capped (five neural point processes fail to beat ETAS on a corrected leak-safe benchmark). The search did surface one genuinely untested channel: the post-foreshock nucleation observable -- Passelegue and Fryer 2026 (Nature) show in the laboratory that a foreshock imparts a transient slip velocity whose impulse and overstress decide whether nucleation arrests into a fizzle or merges into a mainshock, and Lippiello 2025 claims the first 45 minutes of the post-foreshock ground-velocity envelope carries a sawtooth signature of this -- a different observable WINDOW (strictly after the largest event, not before it) from everything tested so far, though Lippiello is contested as instrument-sensitive by an expert critique. This lever was built and tested the same day. On the gate-free EarthScope broadband network, for each sequence the nearest station within 150 km, the first 45 minutes after the largest event were reduced to a three-band envelope and fifteen dimensionless SHAPE features (a sawtooth index, the residual from a fitted power-law decay, a late-to-early energy ratio, envelope periodicity, burstiness) deliberately normalised so they cannot re-track the aftershock rate the catalogue already encodes; the window is leak-safe because the forecast cohort already excludes any sequence that escalates within six hours, eight times the observation window. Pooled over seven walk-forward folds on 31 covered forecast-only positives, the pre-registered primary is minus 0.042 with CI minus 0.097 to plus 0.012 -- a null with a slightly negative point estimate. What makes this a strong null rather than an underpowered one: the pre-registered positive control, a synthetic informative feature injected into the same covered rows, is learned cleanly per fold (up to plus 0.19), proving the pipeline can carry a signal at this coverage -- while the real features run in the opposite direction, four of five active folds negative. The redundancy audit explains why: the shape features do carry a genuine leak-safe univariate signal, and more than the raw rate (the power-law-decay residual reaches AUC 0.71 on the leak-safe cohort against 0.60 for the in-window aftershock count, and it does not fade when the leaky fast-escalating rows are removed, so it is real, not an artifact) -- but it is fully subsumed by the strong ETAS catalogue baseline, which already encodes the same aftershock-productivity and rate-weakening physics. The post-foreshock envelope is a noisier restatement of what the catalogue holds, not an orthogonal channel -- corroborating both the critique of Lippiello and the aleatory ceiling on the projects own data. The adversarial review (Opus) cleared the leak, join and reproducibility, upgraded the positive-control framing, and flagged that the 2012 fold is a functional freeze (its augmented and baseline predictions are bit-identical at half a percent coverage); one of its own redundancy numbers was wrong and was corrected against a first-hand recomputation. Recorded as a mapped negative on the hand-engineered post-foreshock observable, explicitly not a ceiling: the heavier untested fork is a near-source inversion for the Passelegue slip-velocity/impulse control itself, rather than a hand-built envelope statistic. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 43 (2026-07-24)

**2026-07-24 update 43 (the post-foreshock envelope null pointed to the nucleation-physics control itself; a 107-agent search plus a hard station-count probe show the near-source observable that would proxy it is either structurally impossible or self-similar on open data, and the one scalar version that is buildable returns an underpowered null -- the open-data source-parameter channel is mapped out, and the remaining physics is explicitly behind the dense gated network).** With the hand-built envelope null (update 42), the lever became the physical control it approximates: Passelegue and Fryer 2026 show a foreshock's slip burst sets a transient slip velocity whose impulse decides arrest (fizzle) versus merge (escalate). A deep search (107 agents, 25 claims three-vote-verified, 22 confirmed) mapped which FIELD observable could proxy that control, and every branch closed: the nucleation-phase and source-time-function-complexity channels are physically dead (Munchmeyer 2022 shows a source-time function carries no size information until after half the rupture, and the one contrary complexity result is a processing artifact); rupture directivity is the closest analogue but is intrinsically azimuthal, needing a median of ~34 stations; and stress drop shows no precursory foreshock distinctiveness and is self-similar above about magnitude 3, so it merely re-proxies the catalogue scaling already in the baseline. A direct station-count probe on the actual gate-free network settled the directivity branch decisively: with 31 open broadband stations in Japan, not one of 259 escalation positives has the sixteen-plus stations directivity requires (median one station within 150 km, maximum six) -- structurally impossible without the dense gated array. That left one buildable gate-free observable, the scalar apparent stress / source spectral shape of the largest event, tested the same day: response-free velocity-spectrum shape features (peak frequency, centroid, band ratios) on the nearest few stations, magnitude-detrended, leak-safe (the spectrum is from the event at the forecast time). The corner-frequency and stress-drop fit had to be abandoned when it produced physically impossible values (a 23-gigapascal stress drop) -- open 20-hertz, one-to-two-station, response-uncorrected data cannot resolve a corner frequency -- leaving only the response-free shape features. Pooled over seven walk-forward folds on 40 covered positives, the primary is minus 0.031: a null with a slightly negative point estimate. The negative is not anti-signal but a covered-subset overfit penalty -- the all-forecast-only delta is exactly zero, so the degradation appears only where the finite feature lets the trees split on noise -- and while the default row-level bootstrap excludes zero, the honest fold-clustered bootstrap that respects the six-fold structure straddles it (minus 0.033, interval minus 0.069 to plus 0.001). Every source-spectrum feature is at chance in isolation (area under curve 0.51 to 0.53 on 369 events), so there is no univariate signal to subsume. The pre-registered positive control learns a planted signal cleanly (plus 0.092, interval excluding zero), proving the pipeline and coverage can carry a signal in principle -- but it operates at four times the signal strength of any plausible real effect, so it cannot claim a weak source-parameter effect would have been detected. The adversarial review (Opus) was clear that the honest framing is an UNDERPOWERED null for this crude few-open-station estimator, not a proof that source-spectrum information is absent in nature; an independent recomputation confirmed the negative is a fold-robust overfit artifact, corrected the significance claim (the fold-clustered interval includes zero), and flagged that the 2012 fold is again a silent functional freeze the coverage gate misses. Recorded as a mapped negative on the gate-free scalar source-parameter estimator, explicitly not a ceiling: the same observable done right (response-corrected, many-station) and the dense-tier observables it would enable -- directivity, borehole tremor -- require the gated ~800-station Hi-net network, which is now the concrete next structural unlock rather than another open-data channel. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 44 (2026-07-24)

**2026-07-24 update 44 (correction: the dense national network is not gated for this project -- it has an account and a working fetch pipeline -- so the "gate-free" restriction on the three preceding waveform levers was an unnecessary self-limitation, and the dense-network test that update 43 deferred is now in progress).** Update 43 concluded that rupture directivity is structurally impossible on open data and framed the dense ~800-station high-sensitivity network as a gated "next unlock." That framing was wrong: this project already fetches that network through an existing credentialed pipeline (the same one that populates the borehole-waveform coverage table), and a source-inversion script from an earlier arc confirms the dense data has been used before. The three preceding waveform levers (multi-station coherence, post-foreshock envelope, and source-spectrum apparent stress) were run on the sparse open broadband subset -- one to six stations within 150 km of a typical epicentre -- when the dense network offers dozens; their leak-safe walk-forward NULLS stand as valid results for that estimator quality, but the conclusion that the dense observables were unreachable does not. A directivity test on the dense network requires the sixteen-plus stations with azimuthal coverage that the sparse subset never had, and a first dense fetch for an escalation-positive event completed successfully, confirming the pipeline. The dense-network directivity lever -- empirical-Green-function spectral-ratio estimation under the same leak-safe hard-negative walk-forward -- is the next build, no longer deferred. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open, and the search space is wider than update 43 implied.

## update 45 (2026-07-26)

**2026-07-26 update 45 (a timing audit finds that the whole open-broadband waveform arc was reading a window nine hours late: the catalogue is in Japan time and the data service is in UTC, so three of the four levers never observed the earthquake they were supposed to characterise; two are re-run the same day and the dense-network directivity build proceeds on the corrected clock).** Preparing the dense-network directivity lever promised in update 44 required pinning down one detail: which clock the event catalogue keeps. It keeps Japan Standard Time. The JMA-derived catalogue and every escalation stack built from it store the origin as local wall-clock time — the Tohoku M9.0 sits at 14:46, its JST origin, not the 05:46 UTC one — while the open broadband data service serves UTC. All four waveform extractors handed the catalogue time straight to the data service, so every window they ever requested began nine hours late. A direct test settles it beyond inference: on the station 42 km from the Kumamoto M7.3 epicentre, the window built from the catalogue time read as UTC has a post-to-pre amplitude ratio of 7.6, while the window nine hours earlier has a ratio of 135.6 and amplitudes three hundred times larger. The rupture is in the second window. Nothing crashed and no feature came out missing, which is exactly why the error survived four levers and three adversarial reviews: a pipeline that runs and produces finite features is no evidence that it is looking at the right moment. The damage is specific and now measured. The post-foreshock coda lever (update 42) and the source-spectrum apparent-stress lever (update 43) were supposed to characterise the mainshock itself, so their windows contained no mainshock at all — re-extraction changes the features into different quantities, not noisier versions of the same ones: the late-to-early energy ratio moves from 1.03, meaning no decay whatsoever, to 0.24, the Omori decay of a real aftershock coda, with old-to-new correlation of minus 0.03. Those two verdicts therefore did not test what they claimed. The seven-and-a-half-day pre-window levers (updates 41 and the emergent-energy test) were displaced rather than voided, but the displacement pulled eight and a half hours of post-mainshock coda inside a window that was supposed to be strictly before the event. The InSAR arc — rounds 25 through 32, the largest body of results here — is untouched, and this was verified by reading the pair-selection code rather than assumed: it requires the second acquisition to precede the event by a full day, and a one-day guard absorbs a nine-hour ambiguity completely. Every catalogue-internal feature is likewise unaffected, since those compare catalogue times with catalogue times. Both mainshock-characterising levers were re-run the same day on the corrected clock, with the fold-2012 baseline bit-matching the reference run and no dead columns in any fold. Apparent stress, now measured on the actual rupture spectrum, returns minus 0.012 with CI minus 0.033 to plus 0.008 on 41 covered positives, while its pre-registered positive control reaches plus 0.123 on the identical rows — a clean null, and one whose interval is tighter than the earlier mis-windowed run and no longer excludes zero, confirming that the previous negative point estimate was noise-feature overfitting. The post-foreshock coda returns minus 0.003 with CI minus 0.054 to plus 0.046 on 31 covered positives, but here the positive control itself straddles zero, so the honest verdict is underpowered rather than null. The two pre-window levers are re-extracting now. Meanwhile the dense-network work continued on the corrected clock: the national inventory is 797 stations, and the escalation cohort with at least sixteen of them within 200 km is 397 events carrying 111 escalation positives — two to five times the covered-positive count of every dense channel tested so far, and the reason the directivity observable that was structurally impossible on the open subset is now buildable. A smoke test confirms the corrected windows land on the rupture, with envelope peaks arriving within a few seconds of the predicted S-wave time. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open, and one methodological rule is added: before trusting any new waveform source, anchor its clock on a known large earthquake and require the earthquake to be visible in the window.

## update 46 (2026-07-26)

**2026-07-26 update 46 (the third mis-windowed lever is re-run and comes back clean-null with a robust covered-subset overfit penalty; the dense-network directivity dataset passes its physical quality gates -- 200 of 397 events collected, 36 stations each, every window sitting on the rupture).** Following the timing correction in update 45, the seven-and-a-half-day pre-window emergent-energy lever finished re-extracting all 598 sequences on the corrected clock and was re-evaluated with the same gates (fold-2012 baseline bit-matching the reference run, no dead columns anywhere). On 56 covered forecast-only positives the pre-registered primary is minus 0.043, interval minus 0.085 to minus 0.001, and unlike the earlier mis-windowed run the negative is robust: a fold-clustered bootstrap that respects the six-fold structure gives minus 0.045, interval minus 0.074 to minus 0.010, and every leave-one-fold-out estimate lands between minus 0.033 and minus 0.059, so no single fold drives it. That is not evidence of anti-information. The same eighteen columns leave the full forecast-only cohort at minus 0.001 -- statistically indistinguishable from no change -- so the degradation exists only inside the small covered subset, where the extra dimensions let the trees split on noise; this is the same covered-subset overfit signature the apparent-stress lever showed in update 43, and it means the honest verdict is a clean null with a measured overfit cost, not a signal of either sign. The pre-registered positive control settles the power question: a synthetic informative column on the identical covered rows is learned at plus 0.093, interval plus 0.037 to plus 0.151, so this cohort can carry a real effect and simply is not carrying one here. One methodological caveat is carried forward rather than buried: the 2012 fold is a functional freeze -- its augmented and baseline predictions are bit-identical despite 83 covered test rows, because covered-train is 1.2 percent and the trees never split on the new columns -- so it contributes exactly zero and dilutes the pooled estimate toward zero; the standing fix is to assert that the augmented predictions actually differ per fold, not merely that no column is dead. The fourth lever, multi-station coherence, is still re-extracting and its earlier verdict stays provisional. Meanwhile the dense-network directivity dataset promised in update 44 is being built on the corrected clock, and the first half is in hand: 200 of the 397 covered-cohort events, reduced on the runner to compact per-event bundles of three-band envelopes and source and noise spectra. It passes every physical gate. All 200 carry the per-station amplitude normalisation that a smoke test had shown was necessary (raw counts near a magnitude 5 rupture overflow half-precision storage), none contain a non-finite sample, the median event has 36 stations, and -- the check that matters -- the envelope peak arrives a median 3.2 seconds after the predicted S-wave time with an interquartile range of 2.3 to 5.2 seconds, and the post-event to pre-event amplitude ratio has a median near ten thousand and a tenth percentile of 140. Every window is sitting on its earthquake. The azimuthal picture is exactly what the coverage probe predicted: a median gap of 211 degrees, with only a fifth of events enclosed within 90 degrees, because offshore epicentres can only be illuminated from the landward side -- which is why the gap is carried as a quality covariate rather than an inclusion criterion, and why the pre-registration names station geometry as the primary hollowness risk and requires a geometry-only control run beside the primary. The second half of the fetch is running, after which the azimuth-fitted directivity features and the three pre-registered evaluations -- primary, geometry-only hollow control, positive control -- run automatically. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 47 (2026-07-26)

**2026-07-26 update 47 (the dense-network directivity lever reads out as no signal -- and a control run six times over shows its negative point estimate is exactly what sixteen columns of pure noise do at this coverage; reading it out also uncovered why every dense channel in this project has been power-starved: the fetch cohort was silently restricted to magnitude 5 and above, and the corrected six-times-larger fetch is already running).** The dense high-sensitivity network fetch promised in update 46 completed, 396 events reduced to compact per-event bundles, and the pre-registered evaluation ran its three arms. On 38 covered forecast-only positives the azimuth-fitted directivity block gives minus 0.029, interval minus 0.072 to plus 0.011; a fold-clustered bootstrap that respects the six-fold structure gives minus 0.033, interval minus 0.069 to plus 0.017, with every leave-one-fold-out estimate negative. The geometry-only hollowness control, which carries station count, azimuthal gap and distances but no source physics, gives minus 0.037 -- essentially the same number -- and the synthetic positive control reaches plus 0.093. All the pre-registered gates pass: the 2012-fold baseline is bit-identical to the reference run, no fold has a dead column, the augmented predictions genuinely differ from the baseline in every fold of both real arms, and the pooled evaluator reproduces byte-for-byte on re-run. The interesting part is what the negative means. An adversarial review argued the negative could not be the covered-subset overfit penalty seen in earlier levers, on the strength of one matched pure-noise control that came out at plus 0.003. Repeating that control six more times with different random draws settles it the other way: the six draws give minus 0.018, minus 0.052, minus 0.041, minus 0.039, plus 0.019 and minus 0.029, a mean of minus 0.027 with five of six negative. Sixteen uninformative columns added to a 297-row covered cohort cost about three hundredths of area-under-curve on average, and the observed directivity delta sits exactly at that mean -- so this run carries no evidence that the directivity block helps or harms, and the review's single-draw rebuttal was an unrepresentative sample. The same review contributed a finding that survives independent recomputation and changes the verdict's wording: graded positive controls, tuned so the planted column has a known univariate strength, produce plus 0.093 at univariate 0.80, plus 0.029 at 0.70, plus 0.012 at 0.65 and minus 0.003 at 0.60, so the smallest effect this design can resolve sits between univariate 0.70 and 0.80 -- and worse, the positive control is entirely ignored in the 2012 fold, whose covered training fraction is nine tenths of one percent, even though that fold carries eleven of the thirty-eight covered positives. The honest verdict is therefore split rather than clean: null for strong directivity effects, underpowered for moderate ones. All sixteen features are at chance in isolation, with one column reaching nominal significance that does not survive a multiple-comparison correction, while the azimuthal fits themselves are sound -- the amplitude-versus-azimuth fit explains a median forty-five percent of variance and the median signal-to-noise ratio is above ten thousand -- so the block measures physically sensible quantities that do not separate escalation. A contamination audit the pre-registration required but the run had skipped was executed on readout: catalogued magnitude-3-and-above events fall inside the 180-second window for eleven percent of rows, slightly more often for positives, and excluding them moves the result by four ten-thousandths. Then the cohort defect. The fetch plan was built from the file this project has described for months as the magnitude-4.5-and-above escalation stack; its actual magnitude range is 5.00 to 9.00. The walk-forward evaluation stacks contain magnitude 4.5 and above, so over the identical time window the plan base holds 1,097 sequences against the evaluation stack's 3,777 -- twenty-eight percent of the eligible events, before any station gate was applied -- and the excluded 4.5-to-5.0 band actually escalates more often, not less. Applying the same sixteen-station gate to the evaluation stack over the same six test windows yields 1,243 covered rows and 245 covered forecast-only positives against the 38 realised, a factor of six and a half. That single stale file is a measurable, removable cause of the twenty-two-to-fifty-six covered-positive ceiling that has limited every dense channel here, the satellite-radar arc and the open-broadband arc included, since they share the same root stack. The corrected fetch was pre-registered and launched the same day: 2,179 gate-eligible events, 1,815 of them new, in five strictly serial slices because station selection on this network is account-global state, with the physical event key carried per record so the new index space cannot silently collide with the old bundles. Separately, the fourth mis-windowed lever from update 45, multi-station coherence, finished re-extracting on the corrected clock and returns minus 0.027 with interval minus 0.112 to plus 0.044 on 22 covered positives; it had no positive control, so one was run, reaching plus 0.089 -- learnable in principle, but an interval that wide excludes a five-hundredths effect, not a three-hundredths one, so it stays an underpowered null and its earlier verdict is unchanged in substance. Nothing here is a ceiling: the directivity parameterisation is mapped at the resolution this cohort allows, the bundles are reusable offline for the dense levers that follow, and the power defect that made the mapping coarse has been diagnosed and is being fixed. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 48 (2026-07-27)

**2026-07-27 update 48 (the corrected six-times-larger fetch lands, and it settles three levers at once: the dense-network directivity gain is reproduced exactly by a station-geometry control and is therefore hollow, while the post-foreshock coda, the source-spectrum and the seven-and-a-half-day pre-window levers all converge on decisive nulls -- and the negative point estimates that had accumulated across this arc are shown, by direct measurement, to be the price of block width rather than a property of the earth).** The cohort repair announced in update 47 completed. The dense high-sensitivity fetch collected 1,798 per-event bundles from the 2,179 gate-eligible plan, and the directivity evaluation now rests on 244 covered forecast-only positives instead of 38 -- a factor of six and a half, and enough that the 2012 fold, which previously ignored the new columns entirely at nine tenths of one percent covered training data, now sits at twenty-one percent and moves like every other fold. On that cohort the azimuth-fitted directivity block gives plus 0.008, interval minus 0.006 to plus 0.022, positive in all six folds, with a fold-clustered bootstrap of plus 0.006, interval minus 0.000 to plus 0.015, and every leave-one-fold-out estimate positive. Read alone, that pattern looks like the first non-catalogue signal in this project. It is not, and the pre-registration is what makes the call unambiguous: it required a geometry-only control -- station count, azimuthal gap and distances, carrying no source physics -- to run beside the primary, and stated in advance that if the control reproduced the primary gain the result would be declared hollow. The control returns plus 0.010, interval minus 0.002 to plus 0.022, which does not merely reproduce the primary but slightly exceeds it; on the full forecast-only cohort the two are likewise indistinguishable, plus 0.007 against plus 0.006. So the gain is a proxy for where the earthquake was, not for how it ruptured, and it belongs to the same spatial-climatology hollowness that this project has documented since the 0.861 location-only baseline. The synthetic positive control reaches plus 0.089 on the identical rows, so the cohort can carry a real effect; the gates all pass, with the 2012-fold baseline bit-identical to the reference run in all three arms, no dead columns, augmented predictions genuinely differing in every fold, and the pooled evaluator reproduced twice by hand rather than trusted from the chain log. The same cohort repair was applied to the three open-broadband levers, pre-registered before any of them was extracted, with the sizing measured first: the fetch plan had been restricted to magnitude 5 and above, so expanding it to the evaluation cohort takes covered forecast-only positives from 62 to 403 within 200 km and from 48 to 308 within 150 km, with 1,709 new events fetched and one whole test window, 2024 onward, that previously had no coverage at all. The clock was re-anchored on a known earthquake before extraction began, as update 45's rule now requires: the plan's largest event resolves to 14:36:32 UTC, matching the official origin of the magnitude 7.4 off Fukushima, and the correct window shows a post-to-pre amplitude ratio of 915 against 1.0 for the nine-hour-late alternative. The results are then unambiguous where they used to be provisional. The post-foreshock coda lever, at 201 covered positives instead of 31, returns minus 0.002 with interval minus 0.018 to plus 0.014. The source-spectrum apparent-stress lever, at 241 instead of 41, returns minus 0.006, interval minus 0.017 to plus 0.004. The seven-and-a-half-day pre-window emergent-energy lever, at 369 instead of 56, returns minus 0.004, interval minus 0.014 to plus 0.007 -- which dissolves the robust negative reported in update 46, minus 0.043 with interval minus 0.085 to minus 0.001, and confirms the interpretation offered there rather than overturning it: the degradation was confined to a small covered subset and vanishes once the subset stops being small. All three positive controls are strongly positive on the identical rows, at plus 0.118, plus 0.100 and plus 0.082, so these are nulls of the observable and not failures of the harness. The width penalty is now measured rather than inferred. Adding uninformative columns of the same shape to the same covered rows costs, on six random draws each, about minus 0.027 for seventeen columns at 38 positives, minus 0.019 for sixteen columns at 201, minus 0.0065 for eighteen columns at 369 and minus 0.008 for six columns at 241. Every negative point estimate this arc has produced falls inside those bands. One exploratory comparison, run after the pre-registered endpoints and reported as exploratory, separates two things the primary endpoint charges together: pairing each real block against the mean of its own noise draws on identical rows gives plus 0.0169, interval minus 0.0004 to plus 0.0331, for the post-foreshock coda, and minus 0.0002, interval minus 0.0110 to plus 0.0110, for the source spectrum. The source-spectrum columns are inert -- indistinguishable from noise, and more power will not change that. The coda columns are not inert; they recover roughly what sixteen columns cost, which is why the net delta is zero. That distinction is testable rather than rhetorical, and the follow-up has been pre-registered before being run: compress each block to a single column with a train-only stacking layer, so the width penalty is paid once instead of sixteen times, with the inert source-spectrum block included deliberately as a second negative control and with an explicit rule that a positive result on the noise block voids the round. The fourth open-broadband lever, multi-station coherence, is still extracting on the expanded cohort. Nothing here is a ceiling: three levers are now mapped at a resolution the earlier cohort could not reach, one apparent signal was correctly identified as geometry by a control that was written down before the result existed, and the defect that made all of it coarse is gone. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 49 (2026-07-28)

**2026-07-28 update 49 (the pre-registered follow-up runs: compressing each waveform block to a single column removes the width penalty exactly as intended, and not one of the five channels changes verdict -- the cost was real, but it was not hiding a signal; the fourth open-broadband lever also lands, completing the repaired-cohort sweep).** Update 48 measured what block width costs on a small covered subset and pre-registered the fix before running it: compress each channel's block to one column with a stacking layer trained only on that fold's covered training rows, so the main model pays the width once instead of sixteen or nineteen times, with the inert source-spectrum block included deliberately as a second negative control and an explicit rule that a positive result on the noise block would void the round. First the missing fourth lever from update 48. Multi-station coherence finished extracting on the repaired cohort and now rests on 163 covered forecast-only positives instead of 22, a factor of seven and a half. Its primary is plus 0.005, interval minus 0.018 to plus 0.028, with a positive control of plus 0.097 on the identical rows and six matched noise draws averaging minus 0.012; the exploratory pairing against its own noise gives plus 0.0170, interval minus 0.0065 to plus 0.0404. That places it with the post-foreshock coda rather than with the inert source spectrum: columns that are not indistinguishable from noise, but whose contribution is cancelled by what a nineteen-column block costs. The earlier reading of the same channel, minus 0.027 on 22 positives, is superseded, and the interval half-width falls from 0.078 to 0.023. One process note is recorded rather than buried: the noise-control values had never actually printed in the previous run, because the log filter matched a section heading instead of the data line; the stored per-fold predictions were intact, so all six draws were recomputed from them, and the filter is fixed. Then the compression round itself, run on all four open-broadband channels and on the dense-network directivity block, with the primary defined over live folds only -- folds whose covered training set falls below one hundred rows get no column at all and are reported as structural zeros rather than pooled in, a rule fixed in advance. The mechanism worked and can be stated numerically. Six matched noise draws per channel now average minus 0.0045, plus 0.0008, plus 0.0018, minus 0.0057 and minus 0.0048, a band centred on zero within about half a hundredth, against the minus 0.019 to minus 0.027 those same blocks cost at full width. Every positive control stays strongly positive on the identical rows, between plus 0.072 and plus 0.112, so the compression preserves a planted signal rather than destroying it, and the pre-registered void condition never triggers. The result is that removing the cost reveals nothing underneath. The post-foreshock coda gives plus 0.005, interval minus 0.005 to plus 0.015, on 169 covered positives; the source spectrum minus 0.003 on 222; the seven-and-a-half-day pre-window energy minus 0.002 on 281; multi-station coherence minus 0.004 on 135; the dense directivity block minus 0.003 on 244. Not one interval excludes zero, so by the pre-registered decision rule no channel is promoted. The sharpest single finding concerns multi-station coherence: at full width its columns had looked non-inert at plus 0.0170 against their own noise, and compressed to one column that becomes plus 0.0020, interval minus 0.0103 to plus 0.0134. Since the earlier interval already contained zero, the honest reading is that the apparent non-inertness was itself inside the noise band rather than information the width penalty was masking. The post-foreshock coda is the one channel that stays marginally on the positive side, plus 0.0095 against its noise with a lower bound of exactly zero, which the pre-registration had already named in advance: the correct statement is suggestive, not established, and the next step is more coverage, not a stronger claim. Its per-fold signs are three of five positive, short of the four-of-five the rule required. The source spectrum behaved exactly as it was included to behave, inert at both widths, which is what licenses reading the other four at all. The dense-network directivity verdict from update 48 also survives the estimator change, and gets sharper rather than weaker: at one column the directivity block sits inside its own noise band, plus 0.0014 against noise with an interval from minus 0.0047 to plus 0.0073, while the geometry-only control that carries station count, azimuthal gap and distances but no source physics gives plus 0.005 -- the same ordering that produced the hollowness call, now shown not to be an artefact of giving the block too many columns. Neither clears zero, and the geometry control's own per-fold signs split three of six, so the small gain this cohort offers tracks where the earthquake was rather than how it ruptured. All gates pass in every arm: the 2012-fold baseline is bit-identical to the reference run, every live fold carries exactly one non-dead column whose predictions genuinely differ from the baseline while every frozen fold is verified identical to it rather than assumed so, and the headline was recomputed independently and reproduced to the printed digit. What this maps is five hand-built feature blocks, at the coverage the repaired cohort allows, under an estimator chosen specifically to be generous to them; it is not a statement about what the underlying observables contain, nor about denser or differently constructed inputs. One concrete direction was measured today rather than assumed: catalogued magnitude-3-and-above events appear inside the 180-second post-origin window for only 11.1 percent of covered events, so the early-aftershock record the catalogue carries in the first three minutes is demonstrably incomplete, and the 1,798 dense-network bundles already sitting offline contain the waveforms for exactly that window. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 50 (2026-07-28)

**2026-07-28 update 50 (a lever aimed at the catalogue rather than at the model: the dense network can demonstrably detect aftershocks the catalogue omits in the first three minutes -- 81 percent recall on the ones it does list -- and feeding them in changes nothing, because a control needing no waveform at all shows that entire three-minute window is already inside the ETAS-based baseline).** Every waveform lever from update 41 onward added COLUMNS to a stack whose skill comes from catalogue statistics, and every one returned null. This one was built to be different in kind: it targets the catalogue those statistics are computed from. The motivation was measured, not assumed -- catalogued magnitude-3-and-above events appear inside the 180-second post-origin window for only 11.1 percent of the covered events, because the mainshock coda raises the completeness magnitude exactly when the aftershock rate is highest. If the dense network sees what the catalogue misses there, the input to the established lever is improvable rather than the lever itself. The detector was fixed in a pre-registration written before any feature met a label, and calibrated against a ground truth that cannot leak: the origin times of aftershocks the catalogue DOES list. Per station it takes the eight-to-twenty-hertz vertical envelope, subtracts a ten-second running median so that smooth coda decay is removed while a discrete arrival is not, normalises by that station's own scatter, and shifts each station earlier by its S traveltime to the mainshock epicentre, so that a source near that epicentre aligns across the array; the network stack is then searched for peaks three standard deviations above the window. On sixty-three catalogued aftershocks it works: the stack at the listed origin time has a median z of 3.92 and reaches three or more for fifty-one of them, eighteen of nineteen in the magnitude three to three-and-a-half band. That number matters because it means the null that follows is not a failure to observe. One diagnostic was pre-registered as decisive about honesty and is reported before the endpoint: across all 1,798 offline bundles the detector fires 1.85 times per event against 0.08 catalogued events, but running the identical detector on the pre-event window implies a false-alarm rate equivalent to 1.60 detections per 150 seconds. The pre-registration said that if that proxy came out comparable to the detection count then the detections are mostly noise and it must be stated plainly; it is comparable, so it is stated. On the six walk-forward folds, all six live, with 206 covered forecast-only positives and the single-column stacking estimator that update 49 validated, the detection block gives minus 0.004, interval minus 0.012 to plus 0.003. The geometry-only control gives plus 0.002 and the positive control plus 0.112 on the identical rows, so the cohort can carry a real effect; six matched noise draws average minus 0.0025 and an exploratory pairing of the real block against its own noise gives minus 0.0015, interval minus 0.0092 to plus 0.0058, meaning the columns are inert. One of six folds is positive. By the pre-registered rule the lever fails on three of its four conditions and the verdict is null. The result worth keeping is the fourth control. The hypothesis was specifically about the SUB-catalogue part of that window, so the pre-registration required a variant carrying only the listed magnitude-3 count and largest listed magnitude -- no waveform whatsoever -- as the thing the waveform block had to beat. That control is itself exactly zero, minus 0.000 with an interval from minus 0.006 to plus 0.006. So this is not a case of the unlisted events failing while the listed ones helped. The entire first-180-seconds aftershock channel, catalogued events included, is already absorbed by a baseline that contains an ETAS fit: a model conditioned on the sequence up to the mainshock already anticipates that window's productivity, and measuring it directly adds nothing on top of the anticipation. That is a sharper statement than rounds 33 through 36 could make, because it cleanly separates "the instrument cannot see it", which the 81 percent recall refutes, from "seeing it does not help here", which is what was measured. What this does not establish is that early-aftershock completeness is irrelevant in general: the window is 180 seconds because that is what the existing bundles hold, the detection threshold has measured recall but unmeasured precision, and Omori productivity accumulates over hours rather than minutes, so the longer window that would test the general claim needs a refetch and was not run. Nothing was refetched for this round and nothing was tuned after the diagnostics were seen. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 51 (2026-07-28)

**2026-07-28 update 51 (the follow-up update 50 left open is answered the same day and without fetching anything: asking the catalogue where it is actually incomplete shows 84 percent of the entire six-hour missing-event budget sits inside the first three minutes -- so the longer window has nothing to recover, and the same measurement turns the previous round's weakest caveat into its strongest evidence).** Update 50 ended on an honest limitation: the test used a 180-second window because that is what the offline bundles hold, while Omori productivity accumulates over hours, so a longer window might carry what three minutes did not -- at the cost of a multi-hour fetch from the dense network. That question turns out to be answerable from the catalogue alone. The method uses no waveforms and no labels: count catalogued magnitude-2-and-above aftershocks inside the leak-safe six-hour window, restricted to those below the escalation threshold so that no feature could encode "the escalation already happened" and thereby predict the rows the forecast-only cohort removes; fit the Omori decay on the half-hour-to-six-hour bins, where the completeness magnitude has recovered; extrapolate back and read the deficit. Across 2,591 sequences the shape is unambiguous. In the first 180 seconds the catalogue lists 0.203 events per sequence against an extrapolated 2.245, a 91 percent deficit. From 180 to 600 seconds the deficit is 20 percent, from 600 to 1,800 seconds 6 percent, and from half an hour onward the catalogue matches its own Omori trend to within one percent. The total missing budget over six hours is 2.42 events per sequence and 84 percent of it lies in the first three minutes. The load-bearing part of that result is the late bins agreeing with the fit, which does not depend on the fitted decay exponent. Two things follow. First, the longer-window follow-up is closed and no fetch will be run for it: the region a multi-hour dense fetch would newly cover is already essentially complete in the catalogue, and what the catalogue holds there is already inside the baseline, whose sequence block carries aftershock count, rate, largest magnitude, b-value and inter-event-time statistics over that same post-mainshock window. There is no missing-data budget out there to go and get. Second, and more interesting, the previous round's null becomes considerably harder to dismiss. Update 50 reported a pre-registered false-alarm proxy suggesting most of the detector's 1.85 detections per event might be noise. The independent, catalogue-only budget says 2.042 events per sequence should be missing in exactly that window -- agreement with the detector's count to about ten percent. The two estimates were arrived at by completely different routes, one from array waveforms and one from the decay law of the catalogue itself, and they meet. So the fair reading of the previous round is not that noise was counted and nothing was learned; it is that the missing early-aftershock record was recovered at roughly the rate the physics says it is missing, and recovering it moved the forecast by minus 0.004 with an interval from minus 0.012 to plus 0.003. Sub-catalogue completeness in the minutes after a mainshock is measurable, it was measured two independent ways that agree, and it is not where this target's remaining information lives. That is a specific statement about one window and one target, reached by measurement rather than assumption, and it says nothing about whether completeness matters for other targets. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 52 (2026-07-29)

**2026-07-29 update 52 (before spending anything on the two InSAR levers still listed as open, the noise floor they were sized against was measured instead of assumed -- and it is nineteen times larger than the assumed value, because the limiting term is per-epoch atmospheric delay that stacking interferograms cannot touch; both levers fail, and the eight nulls before them turn out to have been run below SNR one).** Two levers survived rounds 25 through 32: redefining the measurement patch so that offshore epicentres are read on the nearest land instead of being dropped, and running pixel-level time-series inversion rather than the scalar version already tried. The rule adopted in round 37 is that a lever gets sized from data already on disk before anything is fetched, with the decision thresholds written into the sizing script before it runs, and that is what happened here. The offshore lever had been sized in July against two numbers that were never measured: 1.7 mm for a single interferogram and 0.4 mm for a twenty-pair stack. Measured on the 11,054 extracted pair records, the median single-pair displacement is 6.13 mm, and a twenty-pair stack does not reach 0.4 mm -- it reaches 7.7 mm. The gap is not a calibration slip but a missing decomposition. Splitting each event-and-frame group by least-squares epoch inversion, over 570 groups with at least eight pairs and six epochs, an epoch history explains 89 percent of the per-pair variance; the part it cannot explain has an RMS of 2.89 mm, and the epoch estimates themselves scatter by 5.42 mm about their own linear trend. Only the first part averages down when pairs are added, because interferograms in a network SHARE epochs -- a twenty-first pair contributes no new acquisition. The floor for any two-epoch difference is therefore the square root of two times the epoch scatter, 7.7 mm, which is exactly what the direct measurement returns; recomposing the two terms predicts a median single-pair value of 6.06 mm against 6.13 observed, one percent apart. Which term is which is settled by coherence: sorted into terciles of inner-disc coherence, the non-epoch residual falls from 6.55 to 1.28 mm, a factor of five, while the epoch scatter moves only from 5.97 to 4.85 mm. Decorrelation noise is coherence-dependent; turbulent atmospheric delay is not. That also answers the atmospheric-correction question without running the software, because the extracted quantity is already ring-referenced -- an inner-disc median minus a surrounding-annulus median -- so the long-wavelength stratified delay that external weather models remove best has already been differenced out, and what remains is the short-wavelength turbulent part they handle worst. The decomposition was then checked by a route that uses no inversion at all: across 8,864 closed interferogram triples the robust closure error gives a non-epoch term of 2.77 mm against 2.89 from the inversion, and against the 3.1 mm misclosure measured in round 32 on a different grouping, with the epoch term recovered by difference at 5.97 mm against 5.42. The lag-one autocorrelation of the detrended epoch series is minus 0.244, so the epoch term is white in time -- it is atmosphere, not slow ground motion, and averaging over epochs is licensed. Both gates then fail on their own numbers. Of 290 uncovered live forecast-only positives, 264 have land within 1.5 degrees at a median distance of 72 km, but only 8 clear the measured floor under a magnitude-six preparatory-slip scenario, and only one of those sits in a frame that already lists ten or more usable pre-event pairs, against thresholds of 30 and 60 percent; the previous count of 326 was produced entirely by the wrong floor. Pixel-level inversion attacks the smaller of the two terms, and even granting a generous 40 percent reduction of the atmospheric term the floor only improves to 4.64 mm, still above the scenario signal for 55 percent of the covered positives. Extending the pre-event window does lower the floor as promised -- 90 days give 8 epochs and 3.83 mm, 730 days give 60 epochs and 1.40 mm -- but the count of covered positives reaching signal-to-noise two rises only from 7 to 10, because the binding term is the signal, which falls with the square of hypocentral distance, not the noise. The most useful consequence is retrospective. At the window those eight rounds actually used, the median covered event had eight pre-event epochs, a floor of 3.83 mm, against a median scenario signal of 3.39 mm. The interferometric arc was run at signal-to-noise below one across roughly two thirds of its own cohort, so its nulls were bounded by the instrument rather than by the Earth, and that could have been known for the cost of an afternoon's arithmetic on records that were already downloaded. None of this bounds what surface deformation contains, and none of it is a ceiling; it bounds what this measurement, on this cohort, at this patch geometry, can resolve, and it replaces an assumed number with a measured one. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 53 (2026-08-01)

**2026-08-01 update 53 (sizing discipline applied to the last unstarted dense lever while the round-38 fetch runs: multi-station pre-window tremor on Hi-net fails both pre-registered gates -- 206 covered positives where 300 is the floor and the existing single-station lever already covers 369, and roughly 8,100 chain-hours of continuous data for the 7.5-day window -- so the branch is recorded as a mapped constraint for the cost of one script, before a doomed multi-month fetch rather than after it).** The lever was the one dense-tier idea still unstarted: Obara-style envelope cross-correlation across borehole stations over the days before each mainshock, aimed at emergent energy below the catalogue, with the catalogue rate detrended -- different in kind from the single-station pre-window RMS that round 35 decided null. Thresholds were fixed in the sizing script before it ran, per the round-37/38 rule. The power gate fails for a reason no window length can fix: envelope correlation physically needs stations within about 100 km, the cohort's positives are dominated by offshore epicentres, and only 206 covered forecast-only positives have six such stations -- fewer than the 369 the gate-free EarthScope lever covers today, so the round could not even have been a power upgrade. The cost gate fails structurally: event pre-windows barely overlap in cell-day space (dedup factor 1.1), so 7.5 days of continuous dense data prices at about 8,100 hours through the win32 request channel -- eleven months of chain time. What is closed is fetching this observable per-event through that channel onto this offshore-dominated cohort; the observable itself remains untested at scale, a bulk continuous-data channel or an onshore-restricted target would reopen it, and the catalogued part of the signal -- the tremor and VLFE the network operators already extract -- was tested long ago and is null. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 54 (2026-08-05)

**2026-08-05 update 54 (the round-38 fetch finishes and its pre-registered readout comes back null at exactly the power it was built to buy -- 418 covered positives against 201, seven live folds against five, and a positive control at +0.100 proving the pipeline would have seen a real signal on those same rows -- while two zero-fetch gates close the S-net idea for a geometric reason that station count alone had hidden).** The chain that had been running since late July terminated on 2026-08-05 at 06:41 JST with the cursor at 3,163 of 3,163 and 2,830 bundles collected; the cursor-and-budget rewrite adopted after the first chain lost everything it had fetched held for the whole run. The pre-registered primary -- the live-fold pooled covered-cohort delta on forecast-only positives, with the fifteen Hi-net shape columns compressed to one train-only stacked column -- is **-0.001 with CI [-0.005, +0.004]** on 418 positives against 1,345 negatives, and all three conditions fixed in the pre-registration fail: the interval contains zero, the real-minus-noise estimate of +0.0017 sits inside the six-seed noise band, and the live folds split three positive to four negative rather than the four required to share a sign. The void condition is not met -- the noise band averages negative -- so this is a valid null rather than a broken estimator, and every gate passed: the fold-2012 reference predictions bit-match, no fold has a dead column, all seven live folds have variant predictions differing from the reference, the headline reproduces bit-identically on a second run, the six noise seeds place the band, and the positive control returns **+0.100 with CI [+0.078, +0.123]** on the identical rows through the identical stacking code. The geometry-only control is likewise flat at -0.003. What makes the round worth its 104 chain-hours is that the coverage it was designed to buy actually arrived -- 418 covered forecast-only positives against a projection of 424 and a previous best of 201, with every one of the seven folds live where round 36 carried frozen ones -- so the null is a bound rather than a shrug: on this instrument, at 300 km, with the across-station median of twelve boreholes, the post-origin envelope-shape effect is smaller than ±0.005. The mandatory confound audit was re-run at the new radius and its advance warning proved correct where the earlier one had not: `burst_frac` correlates with the fraction of the window above twice the pre-origin noise at rho 0.841, `omori_dev` at 0.612 to 0.725, `late_early` with station distance at 0.59 to 0.63 and `acf_periodicity` in the lowest band at -0.603, so at 300 km these columns are restatements of window occupancy and geometry and are recorded as such rather than as nucleation observables -- but the confounds themselves carry no skill on this cohort (0.521 for window occupancy, 0.542 for distance, 0.417 for magnitude), and all fifteen columns sit between AUC 0.482 and 0.541, so the degeneracy is not what produced the null. As the pre-registration stated in advance, this does not retroactively refute the round-36 estimate of +0.0095: different instrument, different passbands, different station geometry, and an independent test rather than a replication. Two further gates were then run without fetching anything. S-net, the 144-station ocean-bottom array over the Japan Trench, has never been used as a cohort-level channel, and the offshore dominance of the positive cohort is the single structural cause behind the InSAR, directivity, tremor and Hi-net coverage failures alike. The nationwide version fails both thresholds -- 173 covered positives against a floor of 300, and 343 hours against a ceiling of 150 -- and the reason is that the premise was half wrong: station counts within 300 km of S-net-era positives are bimodal, with a median of 5, a ninetieth percentile of 78, and 46.3 percent having no station at all, because the array covers the trench and the positives also occur along Nankai, the Japan Sea, Kyushu and the Kurils. Restricting the target to the array's own footprint fixes the cost -- 137 hours, inside the ceiling -- and all four candidate folds are live, but the observable that motivated the round dies on azimuth: 82.1 percent of those positives have the sixteen stations directivity requires, yet the median azimuthal gap is 160 degrees because a trench-parallel array sits on one side of its targets, only 25.4 percent meet the 90-degree requirement, and just 44 positives survive both conditions, spread nine, seven, eight and twelve across folds. Gate-free broadband failed directivity for want of stations; S-net fails it for want of azimuth. The branch is recorded as a mapped constraint for the cost of two scripts rather than 137 hours of fetching, and the array's real density -- a median of 68 stations inside its own footprint -- remains available to any observable that does not need azimuthal coverage. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 55 (2026-08-05)

**2026-08-05 update 55 (a short-horizon result is overturned as an artifact of the forecast grid, the resolution lever is then shown to be an easy-negative artifact in time exactly as it already was in space, redefining the arena rather than the resolution produces a genuine increment that survives a pre-registered select-and-confirm test -- and the AUC above 0.90 that appeared along the way, the first in this project with positive Brier skill, is refuted by replication on an independent catalogue before it could be claimed).** The operational M5+ pipeline's own ceiling analysis had left a monotone trend unfinished: in the aftershock-primed regime, the only arena where its skill is genuine rather than easy-negative, full-model AUC rose from 0.774 at a seven-day horizon to 0.809 at three days and 0.810 at one, and the sweep stopped there. Extending it below a day reversed the trend -- 0.780 at twelve hours, 0.773 at six -- which would have been a tidy physical story had the forecast grid not been read. It is issued every three days, not daily, so a horizon shorter than three days forecasts only the leading fraction of each cycle and discards the rest of the timeline; time coverage runs 100, 33, 17, 8 and 4 percent, and the observed primed-positive counts of 95, 39, 23, 15 and 1 track that almost exactly. The decline was sampling, not physics. Rebuilding with the grid step tied to the horizon -- and with the Omori kernel bin width, which is hardcoded at three days, moved with it -- restores full coverage, and the reimplementation reproduces the production feature matrix to a maximum absolute difference of exactly zero, with primed positives now rising to 129, 150 and 170 instead of collapsing. On that footing the primed AUC does rise, from 0.8087 at three days to 0.8262 at one and 0.8385 at twelve hours. It is not progress, and the rule that says so was fixed before the run: every AUC is reported with Brier skill against the training base rate, and Brier skill falls monotonically across the same sweep, from +0.0694 to +0.0481 to +0.0445 to +0.0144. The project already knew that refining spatial cells raises AUC by making negatives easier -- 0.5-degree cells reach 0.9011 with Brier skill of -0.020 -- and this establishes that the same artifact reproduces in the temporal dimension, closing the resolution lever on both axes and leaving three days as the best probabilistic operating point. What had never been varied was the arena itself: "primed" means an M5.5 within 200 km in the last fourteen days, three numbers that decide which population the genuine skill is measured on. Sweeping 48 arenas with the claim fixed in advance as the genuine increment -- full-model AUC minus a position-only model measured on the same arena rows, so that concentrating spatial climatology cannot be mistaken for skill -- shows raw AUC and genuine increment moving in opposite directions with radius, the same easy-negative mechanism wearing arena clothing. It also corrects a standing figure: the ceiling analysis's "temporal adds +0.040 over climatology" is a full-grid statement, and inside a primed arena the position-only baseline falls to between 0.49 and 0.78, against which the model's increment is +0.10 to +0.22. Selecting on the first five walk-forward folds and measuring on the last five, an arena chosen without ever seeing the confirmation data returns an increment of +0.1856 with Brier skill +0.1011 on 27 positives, and survives. The confirmation table also contained something the project had not seen before: arenas at M6.0 and 400 km reaching AUC 0.9183, 0.9153 and 0.9106 with Brier skill clearly positive at +0.1316, +0.1066 and +0.1733 -- qualitatively unlike the earlier hollow 0.9011. Reading the largest number off a 48-cell table is precisely the selection the select-and-confirm design exists to prevent, so it was recorded as a hypothesis, pre-registered with survival requiring AUC above 0.90, positive Brier skill, at least thirty positives and a positive increment, and tested on the JMA unified catalogue, an independent instrument for the same region that took no part in producing it. All four fail: 0.7459, 0.7895, 0.6612 and 0.8223, with no arena anywhere in the JMA run reaching 0.90. The attenuation is not confined to those arenas -- the reference arena's increment falls from +0.1544 to +0.0686 and the selected arena's from +0.1856 to +0.0784 -- so what replicates is that the arena definition matters, with increments and Brier skill still positive, while what is refuted is that this route reaches 0.90. The methodological residue is the more durable part: measured genuine skill depends strongly on which catalogue defines both drivers and targets, and no single-catalogue figure from this family should be recorded as settled. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 56 (2026-08-06)

**2026-08-06 update 56 (the factor of two between the two catalogues is decomposed and turns out to be the clock and the calendar rather than the catalogues, and the residue leads somewhere better: with the scored rows pinned and only the training start moved, genuine increment rises from +0.071 at three years to +0.159 at forty, and three pre-registered controls kill the three obvious alternative explanations -- it is not row count, not early stopping, and not the 2011 sequence, but the span of epochs the training rows are drawn from).** Update 55 ended with an unexplained factor of two: the same arena measured +0.1544 and +0.1856 on the USGS catalogue and +0.0686 and +0.0784 on JMA, which left every number in that family without a denominator. Two things were measured before any model was run, and both were prior assumptions rather than facts. The clock: USGS times are UTC and JMA times are JST, which the Tohoku mainshock states plainly at 05:46:24 against 14:46:17, and matching events across the two catalogues improves from 186 of 770 to 609 of 770 once nine hours are applied -- after which the matched magnitude difference is Mj minus Mw equals -0.029, so at this magnitude range the two scales are empirically the same scale and the standing caveat that Mj is not Mw is not the explanation. The calendar: the USGS run spanned 2009-01-01 to 2026-08-02 across ten walk-forward folds while the JMA archive stops on 2023-12-31 and gave seven, so the two runs never scored the same years. Harmonising both -- common window, common clock, common completeness at M4, grid step tied to the three-day horizon, identical folds and identical 103,092 pooled rows in every arm -- allows the only decomposition that separates worse features from different labels: a two-by-two of driver catalogue against label catalogue. On the reference arena it reads +0.1061 with USGS on both sides, +0.0896 with USGS drivers and JMA labels, +0.0661 with JMA drivers and USGS labels, and +0.0686 with JMA on both, against a pre-registered fidelity gate that reproduces the update-55 JMA figures to a difference of exactly zero (that gate is close to tautological, since the same archive and the same fold rule are involved, and is reported as a check rather than as evidence). Matching the JMA target by count instead of by nominal magnitude moves the reference arena by +0.008, and defining the arena from the other catalogue moves it by -0.0013 on a row set with a different denominator. So catalogue content explains little of the gap; and on the arena selected in update 55, the same-window USGS figure is +0.0523 on 26 positives, below the JMA +0.0784, which says the +0.1856 was a property of which years were pooled. Reading the increment fold by fold shows why: it climbs across the walk-forward sequence, from +0.0990 in the earliest fold through -0.0129 in 2019 to +0.1779, +0.3028 and +0.1697 in the last three. A pre-registered test at the 2024 boundary came back NOT SUPPORTED and is not claimed -- the late half held 28 positives against a required 30, and the difference of +0.0499 also missed its +0.05 threshold -- and one control in that round was vacuous, because the fold it dropped was on the wrong side of the split and the late half was therefore unchanged. Walk-forward confounds being later with being trained on more, so the next round separated them: hold the scored rows fixed at the five late folds, 73,689 rows and 55 reference-arena positives identical in every arm, and move only where training starts, since every arm still trains up to the same cut-off. Increment goes +0.0775, +0.0741, +0.1121, +0.1449 for three, five, eight and thirteen-point-nine years, and the split by fold index gives +0.0602 with both halves powered at 44 and 55 positives. Calling the position-only baseline flat there was overstated and is corrected here: its spread is 0.0292, which is 97 percent of the guard adopted one round later. Extending to a 41-year JMA archive then tripped that guard at 0.0378 and returned no verdict, and it deserved to fail -- it changed the catalogue and the scored rows at the same time. It also exposed a discrepancy between the prose of a pre-registered rule and its implementation, one two-sided and one one-sided; the stricter reading was taken and the divergence is recorded, because a rule that can be read either way after the numbers arrive is not a pre-registration. The clean version fetched USGS events for 1983 through 2008 into a separate file, leaving the catalogue the operational cron depends on untouched, and pinned the scored rows by absolute date to the earlier round. Increment reads +0.0709, +0.0847, +0.1040, +0.1230, +0.1377 and +0.1588 for three, five, eight, fifteen, twenty-five and forty years, with the three shortest arms landing within 0.011 of the earlier run, so this extends that curve rather than replacing it. The strict guard fired again at 0.0313, and is reported as such, although the one-sided movement is -0.0130, meaning the baseline falls as the increment rises, which is the opposite direction from the easy-negative artifact. An independent audit proposed three deflationary readings and each was tested rather than argued. Row count: subsampling the forty-year span down to the 44,213 rows of the three-year arm, five seeds, returns +0.1473 with a standard deviation of 0.0343, against +0.1588 for the full arm and +0.0709 for three years -- so the same number of rows drawn from forty years beats the same number drawn from three by roughly +0.076, and the effect is not volume. Early stopping, whose validation split scales with training size and could penalise the eleven-feature model more than the two-feature one: refitting without it at fixed iterations gives +0.0600, +0.1028 and +0.1565, a gap of +0.0965, so it is not the mechanism. The 2011 sequence, which the eight-year windows exclude and the fifteen-year windows contain, exactly where the curve steps: excising 2011-01-01 through 2012-06-30 from training gives +0.1478 against +0.1588, with a row-count-matched partner at +0.1420, so it is not that sequence -- consistent with the arithmetic that 65 percent of the eight-to-forty-year gain accrues among arms that all contain it. What survives is a lever, and it is about diversity rather than quantity: what the model needs is training rows drawn from many different epochs, and a system trained only on recent years is structurally handicapped no matter how many rows it has. One caveat is left open rather than resolved -- the regional ETAS parameters used by two of the eleven features were fitted over a period that has not been re-checked, and while they are identical across every arm and so cannot produce the curve, they could inflate its absolute level. The next moves follow from what the controls showed: extend the span further back, and add tectonic diversity from other subduction zones at constant row count. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 57 (2026-08-08)

**2026-08-08 update 57 (a change is adopted on a ten-of-ten replication and withdrawn one round later, when it is finally tested on folds that were never used to select it; both moves update 56 proposed are answered -- extending the span further back flattens, and pooling other subduction zones as training rows is null with its mechanism pinned -- and the catalogues those zones supplied are turned into fitted ETAS parameters instead, which opens a four-round arc on the six kernel columns ending with the deficit located in the functional form rather than in the parameters, the discretisation or the diversity).** The withdrawal comes first, because it is the most instructive thing in this block. Dropping the two regional-ETAS columns and the background column from the eleven-column build measured +0.0204 with ten of ten seeds positive on the window the choice was made on, did not shrink when the seeds were redrawn, and was adopted. Re-testing it on the 2016-2020 folds, which had never been used as test data anywhere in this arc, returned -0.0056 with one of ten seeds positive, and the adoption was withdrawn the same day; a 2026 tail fold read +0.0097 but carried twelve positives against the thirty the pre-registration required, so it reaches no verdict in either direction. The baseline is therefore still the eleven-column build. The lesson is narrow and worth stating plainly: re-running seeds on the window that selected a change tests sampling noise and nothing else, and cannot detect fold specificity, so adoption has to be judged on held-out folds from the start. Then the two next moves update 56 named. Extending the span further back: pushing the training start to 1970 adds +0.0010, so the curve that ran from +0.0709 at three years to +0.1588 at forty flattens rather than continuing. The era-honest reading of that same curve -- that older years are complete only at larger magnitudes, so the comparison ought to be made at M4.8 -- was tested at every rung and is not a performance lever: no arm reached its M4.0 counterpart, stacking it with the nine-column build was consistently worse, and the nine-column build on its own fell from +0.0150 on the window that selected it to +0.0071 on fresh seeds, below the 0.0100 materiality floor fixed in advance. Adding tectonic diversity from other subduction zones at constant row count: null, with the mechanism pinned rather than guessed -- standardising the features per zone and holding the control fixed still gives -0.0212 on full AUC, and the earlier reading in which the increment appeared to improve was an artifact of the position-only control being damaged from 0.71 to 0.52, not of the forecast improving. The 182,516 pooled events those zones supplied were not discarded. If they cannot serve as training rows they can still serve as data to fit physics to, so single-family space-time ETAS parameters were fitted for Kuril, Chile, Sumatra, Tonga, the Philippines and Japan and carried into the feature builder. Appending the six fitted families as extra columns landed in the middle bin at +0.0014. The mechanism is more interesting than the number: it is not that a poor fit fails to help, because re-fitting Japan alone and adding it as a single column reads -0.0033, and it is not that splitting onshore from offshore was the wrong split. ETAS-family columns simply add nothing to the six kernel columns that are already there. That is an argument for replacement rather than addition, and replacement is what the next three rounds tested. Replacing the six hand-tuned kernels with the six fitted families lost heavily, at -0.0442. One candidate explanation had been registered before any result was seen: the production builder samples the Omori kernel at each bin's left edge instead of integrating across the bin, which should punish a sharply peaked fitted kernel more than a broad hand-chosen one. It was correct, and it was quantified without running a model at all -- the first-bin ratio of point sampling to bin integration is 39 to 63 times for the fitted families against 1.6 to 25 for the hand ones, ranges that do not overlap -- and correcting the discretisation recovered roughly 0.016 to 0.019 of the loss. It did not change the conclusion: the correctly integrated fitted basis still reads -0.0196 on the held-out folds with zero of ten seeds positive. That left one registered explanation, diversity, and it was measured rather than assumed. Across all 833,690 rows the six fitted columns have a mean pairwise correlation of 0.959 and an effective rank of 1.180 out of six, against 0.769 and 1.924 for the hand columns -- the fitted basis is close to one direction repeated six times. Rather than build the expensive thing that hypothesis pointed at, a jointly fitted mixture whose spread comes from the likelihood, a cheap falsification was run first. A diagnostic arm, declared un-adoptable in the header before any result because its spread is copied from the hand basis and a win by it would be a win for hand tuning wearing fitted clothing, was handed the hand basis's relative spread. It got the spread: effective rank 2.212, above the 2.024 of the block it was imitating, which is what licenses reading a null as a refutation rather than as an intervention that never took. And it lost, at -0.0339 with zero of ten seeds positive, on both axes at once, spatial -0.0186 and temporal -0.0252. Giving the fitted family more spread makes it worse. The joint mixture was therefore not built, and the cheap round is precisely what prevented an expensive failing one. What survives is a residual of +0.0535 on the held-out folds with ten of ten seeds positive, +0.0611 on the later folds, and it is attributable to functional form: the hand kernels are unnormalised Gaussians of fixed width in degrees, the fitted ones are normalised inverse-power profiles whose width grows with magnitude, and that normalisation is not a constant that quietly cancels, because the width it divides by moves with each event's magnitude. The round running as this is written decomposes that residual one element at a time -- tail shape, magnitude-scaled width, the normalisation, and the sampling -- each arm changing exactly one thing, with a closure check that rebuilds the production hand block through the same generic code path to 3.6e-15. Two pieces of method carried every round in this block. A cross-round arm was included each time, reproducing the hand-versus-production difference at -0.0056 on the early folds and +0.0204 on the later ones across three separate rounds, which is a free demonstration that neither the harness nor the data moved underneath the comparisons. And every script was audited against its own pre-registration before it ran, which caught a gate that would have stamped every round invalid by pooling across ladder rungs, a missing assertion that would have let the feature matrix silently change meaning, and a decomposition whose element sum double-counted a shared term. One framing limit applies to the whole arc and is worth stating so the result is not over-read: all of this is a statement about feature columns on a three-day, two-degree grid fed to a gradient-boosted tree. It is not a statement about which kernel is physically right. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 58 (2026-08-09)

**2026-08-09 update 58 (the decomposition promised in update 57 returns a negative answer that turns out to be more useful than a positive one: only one of the four form elements carries anything, swapping all four at once is worse than swapping none, and the round after it shows the winning element cannot be re-expressed as an explicit feature -- so the deficit sits neither in the functional form nor in the parameter values taken separately, but in the two together, which is what the round now running is built to probe).** The decomposition ran on the held-out early folds with ten seeds, each arm changing exactly one thing, and with a closure check that rebuilt the production hand block through the same generic code path to 3.6e-15. Three of the four elements did not clear the 0.0100 materiality floor fixed in advance: making the spatial tail Gaussian read -0.0057, cutting the magnitude scaling of the width read +0.0058, removing the normalisation read +0.0013. One cleared: sampling the Omori kernel at each bin's left edge instead of integrating across the bin, +0.0185 with ten of ten seeds positive and dominant over the runner-up by +0.0127. The decisive number is none of those four. Swapping all four elements to the hand side at once reads -0.0288, against a sum of the individual effects of +0.0186 -- a recovery of minus fifty-four per cent. Holding the fitted parameters fixed and moving only the form makes the basis worse, so the residual of +0.0535 is not carried by the functional form; it is carried by the interaction between the form and the values the parameters take. Two smaller things in that round are worth recording. The first is an assumption that was turned into a measurement rather than argued.
