The spatial normalisation divides by a width, so when the width is held fixed the normalisation is a constant factor, and a constant factor cannot change a quantile-binned tree -- which means two of the four arms share a term and the naive sum of the elements double-counts it. That argument is correct, but an argument is not a result, so an extra arm was added whose only job was to measure the supposed no-op. It read +0.0000 with a standard error of 0.0000, exactly as the algebra said, and only then was the corrected three-element sum used as the reference. The second is that the winning arm won in the direction that usually indicates a single shared signal rather than a better kernel: it had the lowest effective rank of every block in the round, 1.823 against 2.212 for the fitted basis and 2.024 for the production one, so it made its six columns more alike and still scored higher. With the fitted time constants between 0.0066 and 0.132 days against a three-day grid, left-edge sampling makes the first bin enormous, and the column collapses towards asking whether anything happened in the last three days. That is a recency feature wearing a kernel's clothes, and if the skill really is recency then it ought to be stated as a column rather than left as a side effect of a discretisation choice that any correctness fix would silently remove. The following round tested exactly that, on fresh seeds, with the production spatial weighting and the time kernel replaced by a delta at lag zero. Adding one such column reads -0.0018 with three of ten seeds positive; adding three at different spatial widths reads -0.0014, also three of ten. Neither clears. A control column built by shuffling the same values across time within each cell, included so that a gain from merely widening the matrix could not be mistaken for a gain from recency, also failed to clear at -0.0042 -- which is what makes the main reading legible rather than vacuous. In the same run, rebuilding the six columns from the zone-fitted parameters while adopting the winning point sampling read -0.0404 with zero of ten seeds positive, so the winning element does not travel: point sampling helps under one parameter set and hurts under another, and the same reversal had already been measured in the opposite direction two rounds earlier, on the same folds and the same harness with its cross-round checks matching. That is a fact about parameter sets, not a contradiction, and it is the second independent line pointing at the interaction. One methodological note, because it cost something. Each round validates its two fold sets independently, and a fold set that fails its own gate invalidates only itself; the early folds are declared primary in advance and the later ones are context that never overrides them. A reviewer had argued for gating both sets jointly. Keeping them separate was the right call: in the recency round the later folds missed their cross-round tolerance by 0.0034 while the early folds passed on the same run, and a joint gate would have thrown away the primary verdict along with the context. The later folds' numbers from that round are therefore not reported at all, not even as colour. Putting the arc together gives three of the four corners of a two by two. The production block, hand form with hand parameters, is best. Fitted form with fitted parameters is 0.0535 below it. Hand form with fitted parameters is 0.0823 below it, worse than either. The fourth corner, fitted form with hand parameter values, is not identifiable, because the hand family has no power-law tail exponent and no magnitude-scaling exponent and any number invented for them would be a free parameter of the experiment; it is not measured and no claim is made about it. What the three measured corners say is that moving only the parameters, at fixed hand form, costs more than the entire form plus parameter gap. The hand-tuned values are doing the work, and the question that raises is whether they are special. The round running as this is written answers it with a permutation control: each of the four parameter columns is shuffled independently across the six kernel components, so every parameter's set of six values is preserved exactly and the only thing that changes is which value pairs with which. Because that round can reach a conclusion from a null, and a null is also what a broken or imprecise run produces, it carries two gates that void its own reading: a known-large block swap must be reproduced in the same run, and the permutation arms must come in under a pre-declared precision cap. An audit before it ran caught the failure mode that would have mattered most -- two of the hand kernels share their time parameters, so a shuffle can reproduce the production family in a different column order, which an order-sensitive check would have waved through as a valid permutation and which would then have manufactured the null the round is designed to be able to report. Either answer opens a search direction: if the pairing is special the next lever is the neighbourhood of those values, and if it is not, the object to search is how the family is spread across parameter space. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 59 (2026-08-09)

**2026-08-09 update 59 (the permutation control lands on neither of the two answers it was built to give, and the reason it cannot is itself the finding: the same reshuffle that loses on the earlier folds wins on the later ones, with both fold sets passing every gate in the same run, so what the hand-tuned pairing buys is a property of the window it is read on rather than of the model).** The round ran ten seeds per arm across both fold sets. The arms were the eleven-column production build as baseline, a zone-fitted kernel block as a sensitivity anchor, three independent pairing permutations, and one locally jittered neighbour. A permutation shuffles each of the four parameter columns independently across the six kernel components, so every parameter keeps its own set of six values exactly and the only thing that changes is which value pairs with which. Every gate passed on both fold sets: row counts exactly as pre-counted at forty-four positives in 1651 rows early and fifty-eight in 1508 late, a position-only drift of 0.000000, both cross-round checks inside tolerance, the sensitivity anchor at -0.0362 early and -0.0398 late, and the precision cap satisfied with a worst standard error of 0.0033 and 0.0028 against a pre-declared 0.0060. Those last two gates exist because this round can conclude from a null, and a broken or imprecise run produces the same null. One thing had to be repaired before the round could be read at all, and the repair is worth stating because the diagnosis was not the obvious one. The previous attempt at this same question voided itself on a cross-round check, and the first instinct was to suspect the data or the harness. Three measurements said otherwise: every input file predated the previous round, the row gates were identical, and the position-only model, whose random state is fixed and therefore seed-independent, returned exactly the same AUC to four decimals on both fold sets. Recomputing the failing contrast seed by seed from the earlier log gave a standard error of 0.0034, which makes the difference of two independent ten-seed means carry about 0.0048 -- so the tolerance of 0.0030 that had been set on that check was six tenths of one standard error, a gate that a perfectly healthy run fails most of the time. The belief that the check had four rounds of standing turned out to be wrong as well: the round that had matched its expectation exactly shared seeds with the round before it, so that comparison was paired, and no independent-seed test of the tolerance had ever been run. The tolerance was widened to 0.0100 for both checks, derived from the per-seed spread of an earlier round rather than from the numbers that had just failed, and the run now prints its own audit of that choice: the 0.0100 window is 6.7 and 3.3 standard errors wide on the two checks. The voided round was not re-read under the looser gate; the whole question was re-run on fresh seeds. On the primary early folds the three permutations read -0.0086, -0.0157 and -0.0091, with zero, one and one of ten seeds positive. All three lose, but only one clears the 0.0100 materiality floor, and none of the three lands inside the floor with its interval clearing it too. The pre-registered reading of that pattern is mixed: no claim either way, and the honest next step is more permutations rather than a verdict drawn from three. The number that matters is not in that paragraph. The same three permutations, in the same run, on the later folds, read +0.0070, +0.0069 and +0.0099, with eight, eight and ten of ten seeds positive. Reshuffling the pairing loses on 2016 through 2020 and wins on 2021 through 2025, with near-unanimous per-seed agreement in both directions, and both fold sets passed every validity gate in that same run. The eight-column arm that keeps the hand kernels and drops the three core columns splits the same way, at -0.0005 early against +0.0225 late, so this is not only about pairing: on the later window the regional and background columns are a cost rather than a help. The jittered neighbour, which perturbs all four parameters of all six components by up to twenty per cent, reads -0.0001 early, but it is a single draw and was declared in advance to size nothing on its own. Reading the early folds alone here and calling the hand pairing structural would repeat a mistake this work has already made once and retracted: a column removal that scored +0.0204 with ten of ten seeds positive inside the selection window returned -0.0056 on a fold that had never been used in selection, and the adoption was withdrawn. The instrument that catches this is a contrast across fold sets, not a replication with fresh seeds inside one of them. So the round now running does two things at once. It takes eight fresh permutation draws instead of three and makes the draw, not the seed, the sampling unit, because the question is whether an arbitrary pairing costs, not whether three particular reshuffles do. And it promotes the fold-set contrast from context to a pre-registered question with its own decision rule, so that a reversal is a verdict rather than an observation made after the fact. If the sign reverses and both windows are separated from zero, the reading is that the hand values are fitted to the window they were tuned on, and the round after it retunes the six components on one window and scores them on the other, in both directions. If the signs agree, the pairing effect is period-general at this size, and the object to measure it against is the interaction between functional form and parameter values, which remains the larger unmeasured lever. One practice note, since that run costs five hours. The decision block of the round was executed before launch on synthetic inputs covering every branch it can take, both verdicts, the mixed reading, all three fold-set outcomes and each way the round can void itself, by loading the actual source text of the block rather than a copy of it, so the test cannot drift from what runs. A short run with two seeds and one fold per set exercised the build and the gates from end to end but, by construction, could never reach the readable branches, which is exactly the part a five-hour round cannot afford to discover at the end. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 60 (2026-08-10)

**2026-08-10 update 60 (the pairing question closes inside the materiality floor, the sign reversal that headlined update 59 fails to replicate, and a probe costing no training at all moves the mechanism from the kernels to the operator that samples them).** The permutation control was rerun properly: eight fresh draws replacing the three of update 59, with the draw as the sampling unit rather than the seed, because the question is whether AN ARBITRARY pairing of the hand marginals costs, not whether three particular reshuffles do. Both fold sets passed every validity gate this time, which matters for reading them together: the row gates 44/1651 and 58/1508 were asserted before any training, the position-only AUC came back identical to the previous round at 0.6755 and 0.7149, and all four cross-round anchors landed exactly on the values the previous round had measured for them. On the earlier window the permutation-sample mean is -0.0059 with interval [-0.0092, -0.0027] and seven of eight draws negative: a real cost, entirely INSIDE the 0.0100 materiality floor. On the later window it is +0.0001 with two of eight negative. So reshuffling which parameter value sits with which component costs something too small to matter, and what every permutation preserves -- the multisets of marginal values themselves -- is what is left holding the skill. The striking part of update 59 does not survive: three draws on the later window had given +0.0070, +0.0069 and +0.0099 with 8, 8 and 10 of 10 seeds positive, and eight fresh draws give +0.0001. That is recorded plainly rather than reinterpreted. The methodological habit worth keeping from update 59 is the one about always laying both fold sets side by side; the structural reading taken from them was three draws of noise, and the correction is to raise the sample before calling a split sign a structure. A secondary test had been preregistered while that run was still going, with the number of lines already in the log at the moment of writing recorded in the file, so that the claim of blindness can be checked rather than trusted: if a pairing is chosen BECAUSE it looks good on one window, does the choice carry any value on the other. It does not. The earlier-window winner ranks fourth of eight on the later window and the later-window winner ranks sixth of eight on the earlier one, transfer gaps +0.0015 and -0.0028. That instrument existed for exactly one purpose, deciding whether the expensive follow-up was worth building -- retuning all six hand components on one window and scoring them on the other, in both directions -- and on its answer the expensive version was not built. Then the mechanism changed, and it changed from a probe that trains nothing and runs in minutes. Two results came out of it. The first: the fourth corner of the form-by-parameters square, declared unidentifiable two rounds earlier because the hand family has no tail exponent q and no magnitude-dependence g, is identifiable after all. Setting D = 2 q (111 sigma)^2, g = 0, a = alpha and K = pi D / (q - 1) makes the normalised power-law spatial kernel converge to the hand Gaussian with amplitude exactly one, so no leftover scale factor has to be argued about, and the measured largest column difference falls monotonically as 1/q, from 15.57 at q = 1.5 to 0.137 at q = 1000. The hand family is therefore literally the q to infinity, g = 0 edge of the fitted family, and (q, g) is a two-axis surface joining the two corners. The second result is larger and is about none of that. The operator that turns a kernel into a column evaluates it at ONE POINT, the cell centre. On this grid the cells are 2 degrees, about 222 km, in an 11 by 11 array, while the zone-fitted spatial widths are 6.4 to 14.1 km at the completeness magnitude -- 15 to 35 times narrower than the cell. Measured on the real grid and the real epicentres, the centre-sampled operator sees 0.4 to 7 percent of the mass those kernels carry, and which percent swings by more than a factor of 14 from event to event, so it is not a scale factor that a monotone feature transform would absorb but noise. The hand kernels, 55 to 222 km wide, are resolved at 1.13 to 1.23 by the same measure. A sweep of the codebase then established that spatial cell integration has never been implemented here at all: the bin integration of an earlier round was purely temporal. That reframes four rounds in which every fitted-parameter arm lost. Those defeats may be a statement about discretisation rather than about kernels or about subduction physics, and the single arm that had won under fitted-family parameters, point sampling, is precisely what a sub-cell kernel degenerates into -- an indicator for whether an event happened in this cell recently. Exact cell integration was built next. The integral of a radially symmetric kernel over a rectangle decomposes into four signed corner integrals, each evaluated in polar form as the radial profile weighted by the angular measure of the circle that falls inside the corner, so a kernel far narrower than the cell costs no more than a wide one, and because every kernel here depends on distance only through r/sigma the corner function is tabulated once per shape. The Gaussian is done in closed form with erf instead, which doubles as the accuracy check on the tabulated machinery. Four self-tests pass before anything is trained: the table against the closed form to 1.8e-3, the whole-plane mass of the normalised power law at 1.0000055, the cell average converging monotonically to the centre value as sigma grows past the cell, and a narrow kernel inside a cell retaining 0.989 to 0.999 of its mass where the old operator gave it 0.004 to 0.07. A two-seed sizing was run before committing hours to it, in the pattern that has repeatedly paid for itself: the fitted basis moves from -0.0452 against production to -0.0166, recovering +0.0286, about sixty-three percent of its deficit, with the same sign on both seeds. It is still below production, so nothing is adopted and nothing is claimed. The hand basis under the same operator moves +0.0038, below the floor, which is the outcome that was stated in advance from its 1.13 to 1.23 resolution. The full round is now running under a preregistration that fixes every rule before the data exists: eight arms, ten fresh seeds, both fold sets, a mass-only control that injects the missing total without the shape so that mass and pattern can be told apart, and a union of both integrated bases guarded by a time-shuffled width control, since the earlier null on appending fitted columns was itself measured through the broken operator and so has never actually been tested. Its verdict branches were exercised on synthetic input first, with the verdict source read out of the running script itself so that the tested code cannot drift from the code that runs, and two construction traps were found that way rather than in the results. One reproducibility catch is recorded rather than smoothed over: a Monday cron belonging to the weekly operational product refetched the catalogue in the middle of this arc, 23,670 to 23,698 rows. The evaluation windows were proven unmoved by the row gate and the position-only AUC, both identical to the previous round, but the inputs are now frozen to a snapshot no schedule can touch. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 61 (2026-08-10)

**2026-08-10 update 61 (the operator, not the kernels: replacing one-point sampling with exact cell integration recovers 92 to 99 percent of the deficit that four rounds had been reading as a statement about ETAS).** Update 60 ended with a probe that had cost no training and had found the grid was seeing between 0.4 and 7 percent of the mass of the zone-fitted spatial kernels, because those kernels are 6.4 to 14.1 km wide at the completeness magnitude while the cells are 222 km, and because the operator that turns a kernel into a column evaluated it at a single point, the cell centre. The fix was built and tested before it was used. The integral of a radially symmetric kernel over a rectangle decomposes into four signed corner integrals, each evaluated in polar form as the radial profile weighted by the angular measure of the circle that falls inside the corner, so a kernel far narrower than the cell costs no more to integrate than a wide one; and because every kernel here depends on distance only through r/sigma, the corner function is tabulated once per shape rather than per parameter set. The Gaussian is done in closed form with erf instead, which doubles as the accuracy check on the tabulated machinery. Four self-tests were required to pass before any training: the table against the closed form to 1.8e-3, the whole-plane mass of the normalised power law at 1.0000055, the cell average converging monotonically to the centre value as sigma grows past the cell, and a narrow kernel sitting inside a cell retaining 0.989 to 0.999 of its mass where the old operator had given it 0.004 to 0.07. A two-seed sizing was then run before committing hours, in the pattern that keeps paying for itself, and moved the fitted basis from -0.0452 against production to -0.0166. That was worth a full round. The round itself was preregistered with eight arms, ten fresh seeds and both fold sets, and its verdict branches were exercised on synthetic input first, with the verdict source read out of the running script so the tested code could not drift from the code that ran; two construction traps in the synthetic harness were found that way rather than in the results. Both fold sets passed every validity gate. The result on the primary question replicates on both windows: the cell-integrated fitted basis reaches parity with production, -0.0027 on the earlier window and -0.0005 on the later one, from -0.0346 and -0.0457 centre-sampled, which is a recovery of 0.923 and 0.988 of the deficit. So the repeated defeat of every fitted-parameter arm across four rounds was not a statement about kernels, and not a statement about subduction physics: it was a statement about discretisation, and it is now measured as such. The mechanism control settles which half of the fix did the work. An arm was built that keeps the centre-sampled SHAPE but rescales each event so the total mass matches what integration would give, injecting the total without the pattern. It lands within the materiality floor of full integration on both windows, so the verdict is that restoring the missing total is most of it, not the pattern. The part of the round that did NOT survive is worth stating as plainly as the part that did. A union arm carrying both integrated bases side by side, twelve kernel columns instead of six, scored +0.0167 against production on the earlier window with nine of ten seeds positive, which clears the 0.0100 materiality bar used since update 52 and clears its own width control by +0.0377. On the later window the same arm scores +0.0031 with six of ten seeds positive, below the bar. The cell-integrated hand basis alone did worse than that: +0.0076 on the earlier window with nine of ten seeds positive, and -0.0166 with zero of ten on the later one, a sign reversal. And the width control itself sits at -0.0210 and -0.0234, which is why the union-versus-control contrast is large; that contrast shows the control is bad, not that the union is good. So the union is a candidate that already fails to replicate at material size, and it is recorded that way rather than carried forward as a result. Reporting it as one and then correcting it inside the same round is the honest sequence, and the correction is the more useful half. What happens next was decided by measuring the one thing that cannot be spent twice. Only three windows have ever been used as test sets in this arc, and the years before 2016 have never been one, so a single genuinely held-out window remains. An arm-independent sizing pass that trains nothing measured it: 2011 to 2015 carries 203 positives, far more power than the 44 and 58 of the used windows, but 91.6 percent of those positives fall inside a nine-cell box around the 2011 M9.0 sequence, against 43.2 and 37.9 percent for the used windows, and the comparable later half holds only 32. That window is therefore one shot, and partly a test of aftershock forecasting rather than of the same task. Spending it on an arm that has already failed to clear on a second window would leave nothing clean for a stronger candidate, so it is reserved, and the round now running is the lever the operator work was done for. Because the hand family is exactly the infinite-tail, magnitude-independent corner of the fitted family under the embedding established in update 60, the two parameters that leave that corner -- the tail exponent and the magnitude dependence of the width -- form a surface that can now be swept without the sweep measuring discretisation. Nine points spanning the measured distance between the hand corner and the fitted values are being run through the fixed operator, on the earlier window only, as an explicit SELECTION step: the winner must then clear the bar again on the later window as a separate CONFIRMATION run, and only then is the held-out window spent. A bound on maximum-selection bias is computed inside the round -- the expected largest of nine zero-mean estimates at the observed precision -- and a winner that does not exceed it is downgraded to flat whatever its raw number says. What happens after either outcome is fixed in the preregistration rather than chosen once the surface is visible. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 62 (2026-08-10)

**2026-08-10 update 62 (three axes close, and the most useful thing in the round is a defect in how the comparison was set up: nine arms built with a new operator were being read against a baseline built with the old one, so a flat surface could clear the bar on the operator offset alone).** With the operator fixed, the two parameters that leave the hand corner of the fitted kernel family -- the tail exponent and the magnitude dependence of the width -- became a surface that could be swept without the sweep measuring discretisation, and update 61 said that was the lever the operator work had been done for. It was swept as an explicit selection step: nine points, ten fresh seeds, the earlier window only, with the confirmation on the later window and the single untouched window both held back, and with a bound on maximum-selection bias computed inside the round. Against production the winner cleared everything asked of it, +0.0168 with ten of ten seeds positive, well past the 0.0100 materiality bar and past the selection-bias bound of +0.0044. It was wrong, and the reason is worth more than the number. Every one of the nine arms was built through the new cell-integrated operator while the baseline was the centre-sampled production basis, so the true corner of the surface was not production but the cell-integrated hand block, which update 61 had already measured at +0.0076 on that window. Each arm therefore inherited an offset of about +0.008 that has nothing to do with the two parameters being swept, and with a per-arm standard error near 0.0030 the expected largest of nine perfectly flat arms sits near 0.0121 -- above the bar. The bias bound does not catch this, because it subtracts the spread of a zero-mean maximum and the null here is not zero-mean. Referenced to the integrated corner instead, the same surface spans -0.0064 to +0.0074 and the winner is not significant. The sequence that followed is recorded exactly, including the part that is unflattering. The argument for the correction comes from a number published before the round existed, but it was formulated after an interim nine-point surface had already been seen, because the script that captures a log state prints that surface. So the registered reading was reported as the primary one and the corrected reading was labelled post-hoc, and the question was settled instead on the window that had not been run: there the operator offset is -0.0146, the opposite sign, so a requirement to clear the bar against BOTH references was pinned before that run started. The winner then scored -0.0095 against production and +0.0052 against the integrated corner: NOT CONFIRMED on either. So the two-parameter family carries a small effect that replicates in sign, +0.0074 and +0.0052 on the two windows, and is below the materiality floor on both. It is not adopted, and the single held-out window was not spent on it. Two things are now on the record as durable method rather than as results. When every arm is rebuilt with a new operator, the baseline must be rebuilt with it too, or the operator difference rides on every arm at once. And a selection-bias correction that assumes a zero-mean null is silent when the null is displaced, so the centre of the null has to be checked before the correction is trusted. The next axis was chosen from outside the kernel-parameter family, since that family is now closed on form, values, pairing, tail and width dependence. Every kernel column compresses the whole intensity field inside a cell to one number, its integral, while the physical triggering scale is four to thirty-five times finer than the cell, so two cells with equal integrated intensity but different internal structure are indistinguishable to the basis. Three scale-free descriptors of that internal structure were built on an exact sub-cell subdivision -- the largest sub-cell share, the entropy of the shares, and the displacement of the intensity centroid -- with the subdivision verified as an arithmetic identity to six parts in ten thousand million million. Each carries a standalone association near 0.75 AUC on training rows and correlates only 0.53 to 0.58 with any production column, and they added nothing: +0.0009 on average against a width control that added +0.0010. The check printed inside that probe was itself invalid, comparing an integral against a centre-sampled density, and is recorded as measuring nothing. An explanation was offered -- that six kernels at four widths already sample spatial spread well enough for the trees to reconstruct the descriptors -- and it was then measured as a linear multiple correlation and does NOT hold: 0.47 to 0.55, not redundant. So the honest state of that axis is that it is not redundant and still adds nothing, and the explanation is withdrawn rather than kept as a story. That measurement produced the current lever by asking the same question of scale instead of shape. The six production kernels contain four widths, 55.5 to 222 km, every one at least a quarter of the cell, while the fitted physical width is 6.4 to 14.1 km at the completeness magnitude -- entirely below the narrowest width the basis has ever held. A log-spaced bank of integrated Gaussians was measured for redundancy against the eleven production columns: 0.72 at 10 km, 0.89 at 20, 0.96 at 40, 0.99 at 80, 0.98 at 160 and 0.84 at 320, with training-row standalone AUC 0.859, 0.857, 0.851, 0.832, 0.787 and 0.710 against 0.823 for the production std column and 0.832 for the background. The narrow end is both the least redundant and the strongest, and the middle of the bank is simply what the basis already has. A two-seed sizing then showed something that changes how such a round must be built: adding ANY column to this basis costs something, -0.0040 for an already-held width built the new way and -0.0126 for a time-shuffled column, so a difference against production mixes the value of a new scale with the price of an extra column. The two narrow widths together came in at +0.0084 and +0.0079, the same sign on both seeds, which is +0.0122 against the operator control and +0.0208 against the width control, while a single narrow width alone was inconsistent. The round now running therefore compares against a control that adds the SAME number of columns through the SAME operator at widths the basis already holds, with that comparator fixed in the preregistration before any ten-seed number existed, both windows in one run because nothing is being selected, and the untouched window spent only if the effect clears its matched control and its width control on both windows with agreeing signs. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 63 (2026-08-11)

**2026-08-11 update 63 (a narrow spatial scale wins twice and then fails on the window that was kept back, and the round produces a number for how wrong the usual yardstick was).** The zone fits put the physical triggering width at 6.4 to 14.1 km at the completeness magnitude, entirely below the narrowest width the production basis holds, and update 61 established that such a kernel is only representable once the operator integrates over the cell instead of sampling its centre. Round 72 handed the model that missing region as two cell-integrated columns at 10 and 40 km, with the primary comparison fixed in advance against a control that adds the SAME NUMBER of columns through the SAME operator at widths the basis already holds, because the sizing had shown that adding any column at all costs something and a comparison against the plain basis would mix the value of the new scale with the price of the extra columns. Against that matched control the narrow scales gained +0.0206 on 2016-2020 (positive on 10 of 10 seeds) and +0.0117 on 2021-2025 (9 of 10), a time-shuffled width control cleared on both windows, and the already-held widths built the new way gained nothing (-0.0033 and -0.0002), so the effect was attributable to the new scale rather than to the operator or to capacity. That was the first materially sized effect in this arc to replicate on both windows, and the decision rule written before it was read said what to do next: spend the one window never used as a test, 2011-2015, once. Round 73 spent it. The narrow scales gained +0.0026 there (bar 0.0100, positive on 7 of 10 seeds), the width control did not clear, and the two halves of the window disagreed in sign (+0.0047 on 2011-2012 against -0.0090 on 2013-2015), while both matched controls landed inside the bands fixed for them beforehand, so the reading itself is sound and the candidate simply does not carry there. The useful part is quantitative rather than rhetorical. The same contrast measured under the same design on three windows reads +0.0206, +0.0117 and +0.0026, a spread of 0.0090, while the ten-seed standard error inside a window is 0.0012 to 0.0022; the yardstick that certified the first two windows was therefore four to seven times too optimistic, and no amount of extra seeds or finer controls addresses that, because the limitation is that only three independent windows exist. This is the same lesson that retracted update 59's column removal, now reproduced in a design that had a column-count-matched control, which is the strongest version of the warning: a matched control protects against the wrong comparator, not against having too few windows. Two consequences follow. First, the research record has been corrected: the 0.895 note in the research directory claimed a genuine ceiling, called modelling exhausted and called the limit an information limit rather than a modelling one, and those three statements went beyond what the measurements supported, so they are now marked retracted in place with the original wording left visible and the reasons stated, since an operator defect had already accounted for 92 to 99 percent of a deficit that document read as physics. Second, the next step is structural rather than incremental: build the same harness independently on other subduction zones so that a candidate is judged against between-window variance instead of within-window noise, with each zone getting its own grid, its own folds and its own model rather than being pooled as training rows, which is a different question from the pooling that was measured null in update 55, and gated by a fetch-free count that must show at least three zones reaching the positive count of the smallest window used so far before any of it is built. The narrow-scale question is not settled by one held-out failure; it is waiting for a yardstick that can resolve it. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 64 (2026-08-11)

**2026-08-11 update 64 (buying windows instead of seeds: the narrow scale fails a between-window test across three other subduction zones, and the yardstick that killed it is confirmed twice).** Update 63 ended with a number rather than a claim: the same contrast measured under one design on three windows read +0.0206, +0.0117 and +0.0026, a spread of 0.0090, while the standard error from ten seeds inside a window was 0.0012 to 0.0022. The limitation was the count of independent windows, so this round bought more of them. Five-year windows were built in Kuril, Chile and the Philippine region from catalogues already fetched in update 55, each zone getting its own grid, its own folds, its own arena mask and its own model -- which is a different question from pooling other zones as training rows, the thing that came out at -0.0212 and was recorded null. A fetch-free count decided which windows could carry the load before anything was trained, with the pass mark fixed in advance at the positive count of the smallest window used so far: Kuril returned 42 and 111 positives in two windows, Chile 71 in one, the Philippine box 51 and 69, while Sumatra and Tonga came in at 4 to 23 and were left out. Tonga is instructive for later work -- it has the most M4-and-above events of any box and the fewest targets, which says the rectangle is in the wrong place rather than the region being quiet. One thing was forced rather than chosen: two of the eleven production columns are built from a Japanese station list and a Japan-only regional fit, so they cannot exist elsewhere, and every window here therefore ran on the same nine columns, sliced out of the existing feature builder rather than reimplemented. Whether that mattered was made a gate rather than an afterthought: on the two Japanese reference windows the effect under nine columns came out at +0.0145 and +0.0095 against +0.0206 and +0.0117 under eleven, so removing the Japan-only columns does not remove the effect and the zone numbers can speak to the original question. They say it does not carry. Across the five fresh windows the narrow scales gained +0.0135, -0.0064, +0.0164, +0.0019 and -0.0036 against the matched control, a mean of +0.0044 with a between-window standard error of 0.0046 against a bar of 0.0100 and a required margin of 0.0097, positive in three windows of five. The candidate is recorded as not surviving a between-window test, which is a fact about one tested lever. The durable result is the yardstick itself. The spread across these five windows in three unrelated regions is 0.0102, against 0.0090 across the three Japanese windows -- two independent measurements agreeing that window-to-window variation is the same size as the materiality bar this project has used since update 58. A single-window effect of about 0.01 is therefore one standard deviation of window noise and cannot be told apart from it, which retrospectively explains a string of results that looked positive and then vanished, and which also says plainly that the several candidates still carried as positive-but-unconfirmed, all of them between 0.003 and 0.017, sit below this harness's resolution. The consequence for what comes next is a change of target size rather than another increment: the levers worth building are the ones whose effects are of the order of the training span result or the sequence-context result, five to fifteen times the noise, and the eight windows and the zone harness built here stay as the instrument for judging them. One such measurement is now specified and unspent: the operator correction of update 61 has only ever been measured on the two-degree research grid, while the headline conventional number lives at one degree, where the fitted triggering widths are six to thirteen percent of a cell rather than three to six, so the discretisation it removes should be larger there, not smaller. That is the next thing measured, and it is measured before it is claimed. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 65 (2026-08-11)

**2026-08-11 update 65 (a correction: the reasoning at the end of update 64 had the direction backwards, and the measurement that shows it took twenty-seven seconds).** Update 64 closed by naming the next measurement -- carrying the operator correction of update 61 to one degree, where the conventional headline lives -- and justified it by saying that because the fitted triggering widths are six to thirteen percent of a one-degree cell rather than three to six percent of a two-degree cell, the mass a centre sample misses there would be larger, not smaller. That inference is wrong, and wrong in a way arithmetic settles rather than data: the discretisation error of evaluating a kernel at a cell centre instead of averaging it over the cell shrinks as the kernel gets WIDE relative to the cell, and making cells smaller makes the ratio bigger, so one degree is the regime where centre sampling is MORE faithful, not less. The harness was built anyway and its own diagnostic printed the point before any comparison was read: at one degree, each of the six production kernels correlates with its exactly-integrated twin at +0.9944 to +1.0000, because those kernels have widths of 55 to 222 km against a 111 km cell. An operator change that is numerically almost the identity cannot be worth measuring on nine windows, so the run was stopped after the pipeline check rather than spending several hours confirming a tautology. What survives is the sizing that was done first and the reason the original round 69 result was large: there the kernel widths in question were 6 to 14 km against a 222 km cell, a ratio of 0.03 to 0.06, and it is that extreme regime, not resolution in general, where integrating matters. The hand-tuned kernels were already near-identity at two degrees, which is visible in retrospect in their published anchor values of +0.0076 and -0.0166. Two things are worth stating plainly. First, this is a reasoning error in a published note, corrected here with the measurement that settles it, and the affected sentence in update 64 stands with this correction attached rather than being quietly edited. Second, it does not change what update 64 established, which was a measurement and not an inference: window-to-window variation of about 0.010 was confirmed twice independently, and the several remaining candidates in the 0.003 to 0.017 range sit below this harness's resolution. The instrument built for that -- nine never-used windows at one degree across four regions, on top of the eight at two degrees -- is unspent and is what the next lever will be judged with, and the next lever will be chosen for having an effect of the order of the training span or sequence-context results rather than for being the next available increment. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 66 (2026-08-12)

**2026-08-12 update 66 (three rounds that close the add-a-column family, one implementation fact worth publishing, and a bug that had wrongly excluded a whole subduction zone).** Rounds 76 to 78 spent twenty-six never-used windows on the last three ways of giving the boosted trees more to read, and none of them clears the materiality bar of 0.0100. Round 76 asked whether a model can be pre-trained on other subduction zones and then fine-tuned on the target zone: the primary contrast against the nine-column baseline came in at +0.0071 with a between-window standard deviation of 0.0084, which clears a one-sided t but not the bar, so it is recorded as not real. The same round produced something that does clear it, in the direction of method rather than skill: pre-training and fine-tuning beats pooling the same rows into one model by +0.0129 with eight of eight windows positive, and pooling itself sits at -0.0058 against the baseline, which reproduces the sign found when zone rows were first pooled in rounds 54 and 55. One implementation fact from that round is worth stating publicly because it fails silently. In scikit-learn 1.9.0, HistGradientBoostingClassifier cannot be used to fine-tune on a different dataset through warm_start: the binning mapper is rebuilt on every fit outside the warm-start branch, so the previously grown trees are then applied to data binned differently, and the docstring's "same data only" is a correctness condition rather than a suggestion. No warning is raised. Gradient-boosted fine-tuning in this project is therefore written with LightGBM's init_score instead. Round 77 asked whether the pre-training corpus was simply too small, and widened it from three donor zones to seven, with donor rows growing from 1.37-1.82 million to 2.93-3.64 million. It does not help: against the baseline the wider corpus gives -0.0001, and against the three-zone version it gives -0.0053, both not real, and the purely transferred model without any fine-tuning is also no better when widened. The three-zone pre-training effect itself reproduces across the two rounds at +0.0071 and +0.0052, and a post-hoc merge of all sixteen windows puts it at +0.0062 with a one-sided interval half-width of 0.0036 -- small, consistent, and still under the bar, and post-hoc, so it is an observation and not a claim. Round 77 also corrected round 74. The Tonga box had been dropped there for having too few positives, and the real cause was a missing longitude unwrap: without folding at 360 degrees everything east of the dateline fell outside the grid. Sumatra, which needs no unwrap, reproduced its round 74 counts exactly, and that is what identified the fault. Round 78 asked the sharpest version of the question: are the six convolution columns a sufficient statistic for the recent event stream at all? The six most recent events within 200 km of each cell were added raw, three attributes each, eighteen columns, on the same information cut the kernels get. The answer is no gain: +0.0006 against the baseline with five of ten windows positive, and +0.0030 against a width-matched control that permutes the same block in time, neither real, and the two zones disagree in sign. A pre-flight check confirmed the raw columns are not a restatement of the kernels, with a maximum absolute correlation of 0.7423 and a most-independent column at 0.0317, so the null is about what the trees can use rather than about redundancy. The practical consequence is that this round removed the case for buying GPU hardware to train a learned encoder over the same stream, and it did so for the price of a cheap measurement made first. The last thing round 78 produced is a warning about the yardstick. Between-window standard deviation in that round was 0.0169, against 0.0090 and 0.0102 measured earlier in other zone families, so the noise floor of this instrument is not a constant that can be carried between regions, and any power calculation has to be redone per window family. One earlier note is withdrawn here: a suggestion that the sequence-context result of +0.04 to +0.05 might simply be missing from the gridded pipeline was wrong, because those numbers belong to a different task with a different target and a different baseline, and a one-line index entry is not evidence that two tasks are the same task. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 67 (2026-08-12)

**2026-08-12 update 67 (a planned round is refuted before it is run, the catalogue turns out to be seventy percent incomplete at its own threshold, and the column family that finally clears the bar does not survive the move to the operational frame).** The round that update 66 pointed at was going to integrate the full ETAS branching cascade over the label window, on the argument that a convolution can only express the parent-to-child term and that aftershocks of aftershocks would therefore lie outside the class this project has mapped. That argument is wrong, and a forward simulation settled it before a single window was spent. For a fixed-parameter ETAS the future of the label window is a Poisson cluster process seeded by the observed events, so the probability that no target event occurs factorises over seeds and its negative logarithm is a SUM over those events -- a convolution with a renormalised kernel. Four hundred thousand replicates of the branching process, all generations, with space, put the multiplicativity test at z = -0.65, while a deliberately non-Hawkes control with a saturating productivity breaks the same test at z = +80.76, so the test has power. A backward fixed point reproduces the simulator to within noise and is grid-converged, and the entire effect of the cascade is to reweight the Omori kernel by a factor between 1.015 and 1.182 across the window. Two limits on that reading are stated rather than hidden: the derived kernel is a new column inside the additive class, not a monotone transform of an existing one, so the conclusion rests jointly on it being in-class and on that class having measured flat in earlier rounds; and multiplicativity holds identically inside the model, so the simulation checks the implementation rather than the mathematics. What generalises is a test to apply to any proposed column: ask whether it breaks the additivity of the log hazard over observed events, because nothing that preserves it can leave the convolution class. The replacement lever was the ETAS residual -- how much busier or quieter the recent past was than the model expected -- and choosing its magnitude threshold required measuring the catalogue rather than assuming it. Binning companions of every M6 by time and magnitude, with the whole box as its own control, gives a result that matters beyond this round: the ratio of adjacent magnitude bands, which Gutenberg-Richter fixes at 3.162 whatever the rate, sits at 0.94 to 1.09 for the 4.0-4.5 band in ordinary times across Japan, the Philippine region and the Banda Sea, so roughly seventy percent of M4.0-4.5 events are missing from the global catalogue even when nothing is happening, and the same band falls to 0.28-0.52 in the half hour after an M6. The 4.5-5.0 band is depressed only in the first 0.08 days and is back inside noise of its control by 0.08-0.25 days. A residual built at M4.0 would therefore have been reading the seismic network, and the columns were built at M4.5. Measured on five never-used one-degree windows, precomputing the non-linearity as a Poisson tail did not help: +0.0044 against an arm carrying the same columns with the tail permuted in time, with a between-window standard deviation of 0.0090 that is at the low end of this instrument's realised range, so it is a null at resolution rather than an underpowered one. The linear ingredients did clear the bar, at +0.0187 with four of five windows positive -- the first column family to do so on this task since the training-span result. What they add is not outside the convolution class at all: a plain count of M4.5 events in a hard disc, and a window-integrated expectation, neither of which the six magnitude-weighted continuous kernels were spanning. Two things must travel with that number. One window failed its row gate because a pre-registered expectation had been transcribed from a sizing run whose candidate set still contained a window later dropped from the design; the exclusion rule itself was as registered, an independent implementation reproduced the corrected counts exactly, and the conclusions are unchanged when the disputed window is removed, but the round is reported as a deviation that was corrected rather than a clean completion. And three of the five windows are three periods of one box, so the effective degrees of freedom are below four. The independent test is the operational frame, which shares nothing with the experiment except the catalogue and the columns, and there the same six columns give +0.0007 with the baseline reproducing its published 0.863-0.865. Decomposing the difference by changing one production constant at a time, the label horizon is the only axis that resolves: shortening it from 34 days to 3 raises the gain to +0.0041 against a seed noise of 0.0005, while the effect of restricting scoring to recently-active neighbourhoods is smaller than its own seed noise and is recorded as unresolved. The zone is not the explanation either, and in the direction opposite to the guess: away from Japan the same columns give +0.0012 against Japan's +0.0031. Splitting the six shows the plain count and the model expectation contributing about equally and additively, +0.0019 and +0.0022 over six seeds, which matters because the count uses no fitted parameter and is therefore free of the leak declared for the other half. What remains untested is resolution, the model, and the span of the training rows, and the first of those has a mechanism worth naming: the 100 km disc was sized for a 111 km cell, so at two degrees it covers only part of the cell it is supposed to describe. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 68 (2026-08-15)

**2026-08-15 update 68 (the yardstick was nine tenths a map: under a metric that compares only rows sharing a cell and a year, the fitted model sits exactly at the permutation null while a single count of nearby earthquakes does not).** Every number this project has quoted for the grid task has been a pooled AUC, computed over every cell and every year at once. That statistic answers two questions mixed together: which cells are dangerous, and when. Two new statistics separate them. The within-cell AUC compares only pairs of rows that share a cell, and the within-cell-and-fold AUC compares only pairs that share a cell and the same one-year test window, which is the question a forecast user actually asks. Before either was read, both were checked against three references: a deliberate leakage control reads 0.9690, latitude and longitude alone read exactly 0.5000 as they must for columns that are constant inside a cell, and a permutation null reads 0.4998 with a standard deviation of 0.0051. The production model then reads pooled 0.8620, within-cell 0.5381, and within-cell-and-fold 0.5021, which is the null. About nine tenths of the pooled score is which cell. The information has not vanished; it is on the input side. The plain count of magnitude 4.5 and above within 100 km over the previous three days reads 0.5311, six standard deviations above the null, so the fitted model scores lower on the honest metric than one of the raw quantities available to it. Two zero-fit probes found the mechanism, and it is not a bug. The same information carries opposite signs between cells and within them. A fixed-parameter ETAS intensity, nothing fitted, reads pooled 0.8558, which matches production, and within-cell-and-fold 0.4400, which is below chance. Ordered by how long each feature remembers: a three-day count in the cell reads 0.5360, a thirty-day count 0.5226, the largest magnitude in thirty days 0.5026, days since the last event 0.4603, and the full ETAS intensity 0.4400. The longer the memory, the higher the pooled score and the lower the within-cell one; the two metrics rank the same five features in opposite order. It is not a sign error, because the pooled numbers are normal, the code path is the one that produces 0.9690 for the leakage control, the reversal is continuous across the five features rather than a jump, and the elapsed-time figure is about five standard deviations from the null. The general lesson is worth more than the particular numbers: when a feature carries opposite signs between strata and within them, one pooled model cannot serve both, and maximising the pooled score actively selects the sign that is wrong inside a cell. Production's six kernels all remember nine hundred days, which is why the fitted model lands on the null. This also reprices the earlier rounds honestly. The add-a-column family from rounds 58 to 82 was judged almost entirely on the spatial climatology; round 81's largest effect, plus 0.0048, is spatial climatology, since the raw background column reads 0.4915 within a cell. Three rounds closed on the way. Disc radius does not carry: the matched-area radius is 182 km rather than the 200 km the pre-registration predicted, and every larger radius is worse than the 100 km already in use, so what matters is not how well an area is sampled but how localised the count is to the labelled cell. The training span can be extended to 1970 without fetching anything, and the result is not real, plus 0.0053 against a bar of 0.0100, decomposing into columns plus 0.0048 and rows plus 0.0006, with a non-monotone shape. Removing the nine-hundred-day truncation from the kernels changes nothing, minus 0.0005, even though the truncation discards seventy-two per cent of the weight, because the discarded tail is a slow level the background column already carries. Then the honest metric moved for the first time. Replacing each column by its deviation from that cell's own training mean and standard deviation, computed only on the fold's training rows, lifts the within-cell-and-fold score from 0.5021 to 0.5226, and a paired bootstrap over the same 758 positives puts that difference at plus 0.0228 with an interval of plus 0.0087 to plus 0.0347. Adding short-window columns without any standardisation is worth plus 0.0126 on its own. But the pre-registered primary failed: the best configuration, seventeen columns, two learners and cell-wise standardisation, reaches 0.5302 and cannot be told apart from the plain three-day count at 0.5311, a difference of minus 0.0010 with an interval spanning zero. The headroom is not in the learner. One axis reopened. Fine cells were ruled out years ago because they inflate the pooled score with easy negatives, half-degree cells reaching 0.9011 with a negative Brier skill score, but the within-cell statistic cannot be inflated that way, so the axis became testable again. Sparse and dense catalogues peak at different resolutions: the teleseismic catalogue peaks at one degree, 0.5489, and falls to 0.5373 at a half, while the dense national catalogue peaks at a half degree, 0.5584, and the paired difference there is plus 0.0211 with an interval of plus 0.0059 to plus 0.0366. The label is a different label at each cell size, so these are not one statistic compared across resolutions, and that is stated rather than hidden. Two other probes came back empty in a way worth recording: counting the dense catalogue in coarse cells does not use its density at all, and features describing the shape of local activity, b value, depth, tightness and burstiness, selected at 0.5285 and reversed to 0.4619 on the confirmation half, so they do not transfer. From here every column is reported on both metrics, and columns are preferred that are short in time and local in space. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 69 (2026-08-15)

**2026-08-15 update 69 (one column of arithmetic replicates in three regions and fifty windows, the fitted configuration adds nothing to it, and the binding constraint turned out to be how many independent windows exist rather than how large the effect is).** Update 68 left the honest metric pointing at short windows and dense catalogues, so the next round put the learner exactly where the unfitted signal is largest: half-degree cells, the dense national catalogue at magnitude 2.0 and above, thirteen columns that are all short in time and local in space, fitted with gradient boosting over three point seven million rows. It reached 0.5761 within-cell-and-fold against 0.5584 for the single column, the count of small earthquakes in that cell over the previous day. A bootstrap over the 431 confirmation positives put the difference at plus 0.0177 with an interval of minus 0.0070 to plus 0.0409, which does not resolve. Read instead across the five confirmation years one at a time, the differences were minus 0.0063, minus 0.0040, plus 0.0539, minus 0.0391 and plus 0.0647: a mean of plus 0.0138 with a between-year spread of 0.0439 and two of five positive. The single column, by contrast, beat chance in all five years. Two data axes were tested in the same round and both came back empty. Lowering the catalogue threshold to magnitude 1.0, which is three and a third times as many events, is worse than 2.0 on both halves at every window length, and feature windows shorter than a day are worse than a day at every threshold, so the optimum is a threshold of 2.0 with a window of one to three days. Cell-wise standardisation, which had been the previous round's headline, does not win here either, because these thirteen columns are short and local to begin with and so carry little of the climatology that the standardisation exists to strip; that is a treatment for contaminated columns, not a universal preprocessing step. The between-year spread of 0.0439 is three and a half times the bootstrap standard error, and that comparison is the real finding of the round: five windows can only resolve an effect of about 0.055, so an effect of 0.014 was never going to be judged. Buying windows therefore came before chasing a larger effect. Candidate regions were sized from event counts alone before anything was fetched. Alaska carries a dense driver catalogue and 449 targets in the same public API; New Zealand carries a dense catalogue through its national agency and 376; California was measured and rejected, dense but with only sixty-eight targets; Chile, Taiwan and the Aegean have no dense catalogue in the international archive and are left to their own agencies. With Japan's own earlier years, which the previous round had used only as training rows, that gives fifty fresh windows in three regions and three independent catalogues. Nothing was selected in the new round: the columns, the learner, the seeds and the one-day window were all carried over unchanged, and the harness reproduced the previous round's two numbers, 0.5584 and 0.5761, to four decimal places before any new window was read. Two defects in the first attempt are recorded rather than quietly fixed. The reproduction check compared a ten-window aggregate against a five-window number, which could not have passed as written, and the frozen target catalogue begins in 2009, so Japan's earliest windows held no positives at all and one of them survived carrying a randomly scored model; joining the same instrument's 1983-2008 file and adding a label-coverage gate repaired both, and the two untouched regions returned numbers identical to the first attempt, which is what should happen. The result: the count of small earthquakes in the cell over the previous day beats chance across the fifty windows by plus 0.0352, with seventy-six per cent of windows positive against a threshold of 0.0160, and it holds under every weighting tried, plus 0.0567 weighted by positives, plus 0.0409 weighted by groups, and plus 0.0302 with the largest window removed. Aftershock sequences make neighbouring windows dependent, which flatters the test, but halving the effective number of windows leaves it standing. Until now that timing signal had only been shown in one country with one catalogue; it now replicates in two more, and in Alaska alone. The fitted configuration adds nothing to it: minus 0.0230 unweighted, and not resolved under any weighting. That negative should not be read as the learner being worse, because it is confounded with training density. The two new regions carry about a fifth of Japan's positives per year while the learner's settings were chosen at Japan's density, the per-region differences fall in density order, and treated as three regions rather than fifty windows the difference is not significant. One limit is stated plainly: each agency's magnitude scale is its own, so a magnitude 5 in one region is not the same earthquake size as in another, and effect sizes are not compared across regions. What is claimed is an existence result, that the previous day's count carries genuine within-cell timing information against each agency's own definition. The next question follows from what failed: not a larger learner, which has now been refused twice, but whether any single second column adds to that one, tested two columns at a time so that learner capacity and training density cannot confound the answer, selected in one region and confirmed in the other two. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 70 (2026-08-16)

**2026-08-16 update 70 (nine candidate second columns, combined with the standing arithmetic by nothing larger than two parameters; none of them resolves outside Japan, and the one that works inside Japan turns out to be half explained by which catalogue it is read from).** Update 69 left one column of arithmetic standing on its own, the count of small earthquakes in the cell over the previous day, and left a thirteen-column fit unable to add to it across fifty windows. That negative was entangled with something the round could not separate: the fit's settings were Japan's, and the other two regions carry about a fifth of Japan's labelled events, so a learner with room to overfit was being asked to work where there is far less to learn from. This round removes the entanglement by removing the learner. Nine candidate columns were written down in advance, each with the sign it was expected to carry, and each was combined with the standing column by two devices too small to overfit anywhere: a logistic regression on two standardised columns fitted inside each window's own training years, and a plain average of the two columns' ranks which fits nothing at all. The candidates were the neighbouring cells' count over a day, the same cell's count over three days and over thirty, the largest magnitude of the last three days, the days elapsed since the last small earthquake, a magnitude-weighted sum, a count at a lower magnitude threshold, the count of magnitude 4.5 and above within a hundred kilometres, and a b-value over thirty days. Two of those were expected to fail on what earlier rounds had already measured, and they were kept in precisely so that the list would not consist only of the author's favourites. Selection was confined to Japan's twenty-one windows and the claim to Alaska's eighteen and New Zealand's twenty-one, which were not read until the written rule had picked its one column. Four gates ran before anything was read. The anchor reproduced the previous round's Japanese figure to four digits; the cross-round check reproduced the previous round's per-region figures to four digits in all three fresh regions, which is what says neither the data nor the harness moved underneath the round; a second copy of the standing column added exactly zero, to four decimal places, over all sixty windows, which is what says the combiners are reading the column and nothing else; and a shuffled column added 0.0011. The column selected in Japan was the count of magnitude 4.5 and above within a hundred kilometres, at plus 0.0155 over Japan's twenty-one windows with three quarters of them positive. On the thirty-nine confirmation windows it was plus 0.0013 with 46 per cent positive, which does not resolve, and the unfitted rank average agreed at plus 0.0006. The standing column itself replicated for a third time, plus 0.0379 over those thirty-nine windows with 79 per cent of them positive, which is the same measurement the previous round reported as plus 0.0352 over fifty windows and is arithmetically the same number read over a subset. Two things are worth keeping out of a null. The two long-memory candidates, the thirty-day count and the time since the last event, came out negative in every one of the four regions, by as much as minus 0.046 in the confirmation half; an earlier round had measured the same ordering with no fitting at all, and it now survives with fitting allowed. Short in time and local in space is not a preference, it is what the measurements keep saying. And Japan's exception was chased rather than filed. Japan is the one region here whose driver catalogue, JMA, is not the catalogue its labels are made from, which is the United States Geological Survey; the hundred-kilometre column is therefore the only column in the whole design that is read from the catalogue defining the target. Rebuilding that identical column, same radius, same threshold, same window, out of JMA instead halved it: plus 0.0059 and plus 0.0091 against plus 0.0177 and plus 0.0135. So about half of Japan's gain is the shape, a wider radius at a higher threshold, and about half is that the column comes from a second agency's catalogue. Alaska and New Zealand had only the shape available to them, and there the shape is worth plus 0.0012 and plus 0.0013. That turns a regional exception into a question which can be asked outside Japan, by giving New Zealand a second agency's catalogue for its labels while its driver stays with its own, and it needs one small public query to ask. This round also corrects a sentence in its own pre-registration, which asserted that a two-parameter combination could not do worse than the column it starts from. That is true while the fit is being made and false on held-out windows, and the same run produced a candidate at minus 0.046 to prove it. Nothing here changes the operational forecast, which is unchanged and still published on the same schedule, and nothing here is an earthquake prediction. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 71 (2026-08-17)

**2026-08-17 update 71 (four rounds: a claim about second catalogues that survives the plain comparison and dissolves under a matched one, a verdict that flipped when the significance threshold was computed exactly instead of approximately, and then the frame itself turning out to be the thing that was costing signal).** Update 70 ended on a regional exception: the count of magnitude 4.5 and above within a hundred kilometres helped in Japan and nowhere else, and half of that help came from the column being read out of the catalogue that defines the labels rather than the one that supplies the driver. Japan is the only region here whose driver and labels come from different agencies, so the first of these rounds gave New Zealand the same arrangement: the driver stayed with GeoNet, the labels were rebuilt from the United States Geological Survey's catalogue for the same box, and the two disk columns were paired inside each window so that both arms face the same labels, the same rows and the same standing column. Only which catalogue the disk is read from differs. In New Zealand that paired difference came to plus 0.0033 over eighteen windows against a bar of 0.0034 -- and the run that reported it also produced the reason to distrust its own verdict, because the harness had been computing its ninety-five per cent threshold from a small table with an approximation above ten windows, which is four tenths of a per cent low at seventeen degrees of freedom. Recomputed exactly, the mean sits below the bar by fourteen millionths. The approximation had been in place for many rounds and had never mattered before; it mattered here, it has been replaced by the exact quantity, and the earlier note that it changed nothing no longer holds. Widening to thirty-nine windows -- Japan 2016-2026, Japan 2005-2015 and New Zealand under the new labels -- the same paired difference came to plus 0.0058, sixty-nine per cent of windows positive, above the exact bar of 0.0043 and equally present under an unfitted average of ranks. That round carried a placebo which was supposed to hold the second agency constant while matching everything else, and the placebo did not work: it thinned the driver's events down to the target's count, but in these boxes the driver has fewer such events, not more -- JMA lists 8,504 events at magnitude 4.5 and above in the 2011 box against the Survey's 12,274 -- so in both Japanese window sets the placebo column was simply the driver column and controlled nothing. It operated only in New Zealand, where it returned plus 0.0020 against that box's real pair of plus 0.0033, which is most of it. So the next round matched the counts properly, thinning whichever catalogue is larger down to the other's size, and the pooled effect fell from plus 0.0058 to plus 0.0043 with fifty-six per cent of windows positive, which does not resolve. Redrawing the random thinning at another seed gave plus 0.0039, so the point estimate is stable and what was lost was not the effect but the resolution: thinning adds variance. The honest position is therefore that reading a column from the catalogue which defines the labels is undecided, neither established nor refuted; about a quarter of the plain comparison is explained by the two columns simply holding different numbers of events, and deciding the rest needs a second region with two agencies rather than more random draws. Sizing for that was done from counts alone and is discouraging: Italy carries only fifty-eight events at magnitude 5.0 and above inside its box over twenty-one years against New Zealand's 503, and the one dense national catalogue openly served for a label-rich region is about a fifth as dense per cell as GeoNet and grows by a factor of two across the period, which is a completeness trend rather than a driver. The last of the four rounds stopped adding columns and asked about the frame. Since this grid work began, every label has been "a magnitude 5 somewhere in this cell within the next three days", scored on rows three days apart. An earlier round shrank the feature windows to a quarter and an eighth of a day and found nothing, but it left the bin and the horizon at three days, so the frame itself had never been tested. Running the same code, the same catalogues, the same cells and the same standing column at one-day bins with a one-day horizon, and running the three-day frame alongside it in the same execution so that the old anchors could be checked to four digits, the one-day frame is larger in every one of the four boxes: the standing column sits at plus 0.0463 above one half across three regions and forty-nine windows against plus 0.0352 for the three-day frame, eighty-two per cent of windows positive against seventy-six, and the pooled area under the curve rises in all four boxes as well. Windows do not empty out and positives go up rather than down, because a one-day bin does not collapse an aftershock sequence into a single labelled row. The permutation null stays at one half in the finer frame, so the metric is not being fooled by sparsity. This is stated with the limitation written down before the run: the two frames do not share labels, "within a day" and "within three days" are different questions, and no p-value is placed on the difference between them. What is claimed is what each frame says on its own, and the plain reading of four regions agreeing is that the frame has been costing signal, which makes the column work of the last several rounds worth redoing at the finer resolution before anything else is tried. Nothing here changes the operational forecast, which is unchanged and still published on the same schedule, and nothing here is an earthquake prediction. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 72 (2026-08-17)

**2026-08-17 update 72 (the frame was the lever: shrinking the bin and the horizon together raises the honest metric at every step from three days down to fifteen minutes, and the axis never turns over -- plus the round that had to be thrown away because it was written to fit a machine rather than the question).** Update 71 reported that a one-day bin with a one-day horizon beat the three-day frame in all four regions. The obvious follow-up was to keep going, and the first attempt at it failed in a way worth recording: the harness materialises every row of a region at once, which at a quarter-day bin is fifty-eight million rows and several eight-byte arrays over them, and the run was killed by the kernel after taking six and a half gigabytes on a shared Raspberry Pi. The failure was not the machine. The frame question is asked of a single unfitted column -- the count of small earthquakes in the cell over the previous day -- so there is no training set, no learner, and no reason to hold more than one window in memory at a time. Rewritten to stream one window at a time, the same sweep runs in ten to twenty seconds per region per frame inside two and a half gigabytes, and it reproduces all twelve of the previously published per-region means to six decimal places, which is what licenses reading its new numbers at all. A rewrite that cannot reproduce the old numbers is a different measurement wearing the same name. With that in hand the axis was pushed until it stopped paying. It does not stop. Measured across three regions and forty-nine window-years, judged between windows with an exact t-threshold, the standing column sits above one half by plus 0.0352 at three-day bins, plus 0.0463 at one day, plus 0.0615 at half a day, plus 0.0771 at a quarter day, plus 0.0845 at three hours, plus 0.0892 at ninety minutes, plus 0.0934 at one hour, plus 0.0977 at thirty minutes and plus 0.1019 at fifteen minutes, every one of them resolving. Below a quarter day the gain settles into a steady four thousandths per halving, which is a straight line against the logarithm of the frame length rather than a plateau: there is no turnover inside the range that was tested. The share of windows carrying the sign rises from seventy-six per cent to ninety, and although the spread between windows grows as well, the ratio of the effect to that spread improves throughout, from 0.63 to 1.13. Positives go up rather than down at every step, because a short bin does not collapse an aftershock sequence into one labelled row. Three limits are stated with it, all decided before the numbers were read. The frames do not share labels -- "a magnitude 5 in this cell within the next three days" and "within the next fifteen minutes" are different questions -- so no p-value is placed on any difference between frames; what is claimed is what each frame says on its own. As the horizon shortens the question drifts toward "is a sequence running in this cell right now", which is genuine skill and is also a different product from a three-day forecast, and it will be described that way rather than as an improvement to the same thing. And the number that moved here is the within-cell one, the metric this project switched to after discovering that nine tenths of a pooled score was a map of where earthquakes live; the 0.90 that remains the goal was always stated in the pooled metric, so this result is not yet a step toward it. Translating the frame change into the operational configuration, and measuring whether the published numbers move, is the next thing to do rather than something to assume. Nothing here changes the operational forecast, which is unchanged and still published on the same schedule, and nothing here is an earthquake prediction. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 73 (2026-08-18)

**2026-08-18 update 73 (the frame shortening carried into the production form: on the one degree grid the pooled figure rises past 0.90, the within-cell skill rises with it rather than being destroyed the way finer cells destroy it, and judged between windows it still does not clear the bar).** The frame result of the previous update was measured in a four-box harness and stated as a within-cell quantity; the goal of this project is stated on a different yardstick, the pooled AUC of the production pipeline at one degree over Japan with the dense JMA background, so it had to be measured there.
