Porting it required one correction that travels with the frame: the ETAS kernels are written with a bin width of three days and a memory of three hundred bins compiled into them, so shortening the bin without rewriting them silently converts the features into a shorter-memory quantity; the bin width becomes the frame and the bin count becomes nine hundred days divided by it, which at three days is the identity. That the port is faithful is not asserted but shown: against the original build the features agree to a relative eight parts in a trillion, and the three published resolution figures come back, 0.856, 0.895 and 0.901, together with the sign of the Brier skill score that made the last of them hollow, minus 0.024. The published table turns out to have been produced with a driver floor of magnitude four and raw gradient-boosted probabilities rather than the rank blend, which is worth recording because comparing against the wrong column would have failed a gate for no reason. On the thirteen-year catalogue the ladder is monotone in the pooled figure, 0.8924 at three-day bins and a thirty-four-day horizon rising to 0.9093 at quarter-day bins and a quarter-day horizon, and the Brier skill score stays positive throughout, so this is not the hollow crossing that finer cells produce. The diagnostic that separates the two is the within-cell skill, and it moves the opposite way from the resolution artefact: shrinking the cells takes it from 0.4891 down to 0.4553 and the within-cell-and-fold figure down to 0.4345, while shortening the frame takes it from 0.4770 up to 0.5875 and the fold figure from 0.4917 to 0.5379, with the climatology-only model flat at 0.445 to 0.482 throughout. Two things follow that are worth saying plainly. The first is that at the production frame the within-cell skill is 0.4770, below a coin, so the published AUC of 0.89 at a thirty-four-day horizon is climatology essentially in its entirety, and what the frame shortening does is make a within-cell signal exist at all. The second is that the Brier-based numbers cannot carry the argument at these rates: the base rate falls by a factor of a hundred and eleven across the ladder, and at the short horizons the climatology model's own skill score goes negative, so both the numerator and the denominator of any relative score are being set by calibration failure rather than by information. Only the rank-based quantities are trustworthy here, and they were checked: permuting the labels inside each cell and fold returns 0.4931 to 0.5126 at every rung, so the instrument is not drifting with the frame. Against all of that, the verdict by this project's own standard is that nothing has been established. Judged between windows rather than from a pooled standard error, the finest rung on the thirteen-year catalogue gives seven values averaging 0.5325 against a bar of 0.0407, which is not real, and no rung on that catalogue clears it. The limitation that measurement identifies is the number of windows and not the size of the effect, since the harness that did resolve the frame axis had forty-nine windows across three regions and this has seven in one, so the catalogue was extended from thirteen years to twenty-six and a half, which gives twenty-one. That run raises the pooled figure again, to 0.9160 at one-day bins and a one-day horizon with a Brier skill score of plus 0.0133 and a within-cell figure of 0.6267 against 0.5295 at the production frame, and it still does not clear the bar between windows: twenty-one values averaging 0.5241 against a bar of 0.0294, with fifty-seven per cent above a half where sixty is required. It is close and it is not there, and the spread is being set by a single window containing a very large aftershock sequence. The remaining rungs of that run are still going and the next step is a longer catalogue still, forty-one years and thirty-five windows, which requires the yearly detection threshold to be measured and matched first because the older decades are less complete. What can be said today is that the frame is the first lever in this project to move the pooled yardstick past 0.90 while the within-cell skill moves with it instead of against it, that the horizon it does this at describes whether a cell is currently active rather than what the thirty-four-day product describes, and that between windows it remains unproven. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 74 (2026-08-19)

**2026-08-19 update 74 (the half-day rung clears the between-window bar: on the same twenty-one windows the pooled figure is 0.9207 with a positive Brier skill score, the within-cell skill rises to 0.6487, and the test that update 73 failed now passes).** The previous update reported the one-day rung of the ladder and said plainly that it did not clear the bar between windows, twenty-one values averaging 0.5241 with fifty-seven per cent above a half where sixty is required. The remaining rungs of that run have now finished and the next one down does clear it. At half-day bins and a half-day horizon, on the same one degree grid, the same twenty-six and a half years of JMA catalogue and the same twenty-one one-year windows, the pooled AUC is 0.9207 against a climatology arm of 0.8995, the Brier skill score against the base rate is plus 0.0055, the within-cell figure is 0.6487 against 0.5295 at the production frame, and the twenty-one per-window values average 0.5375 with sixteen of them above a half, a t of 2.850 on twenty degrees of freedom. Those numbers were not taken on trust from the script that produced them. They were recomputed from the stored predictions by a second program sharing no code with the first, with the AUC assembled from ranks by hand and the within-cell figure rebuilt as concordant pairs inside each cell, and the per-window values agree to the last digit stored. A label permutation inside each cell puts the floor at 0.4988. The verdict does not rest on the one window holding a very large aftershock sequence: dropping it leaves p at 0.0194, the sign test gives 0.0266 and the signed-rank test 0.0127, and dropping any single window of the twenty-one leaves p no worse than 0.0198. What this is not: the 0.90 of the project's stated goal is a figure for the thirty-four-day product, and that product still sits at 0.8995. The rung reported here describes a different question, whether a cell is active over the next half day, and the two are not compared with a p-value because their labels are different objects. What can be said is that at this frame the pooled figure passes 0.90 with a Brier skill score that is positive rather than negative, so it is not the hollow kind that finer cells produce, and that the skill behind it survives being judged between windows rather than only within one. The quarter-day rung was not reached. The first attempt at it died part way through, and the harness was rebuilt to hold the feature block on disk and read each window's rows back a chunk at a time, because the learner makes a second double precision copy of whatever it is handed and the older design could not fit that at this frame on a shared machine. The rebuilt harness is derived from the old one by checked substitution rather than retyped, and it reproduces the one-day rung exactly, all twenty-one window values and all four summary figures, at less than half the memory. To make the quarter-day rung fit, a rolling twelve year training span was imposed; applied to the half-day rung as a control it gives 0.9209 with seventeen windows of twenty-one above a half, so the cap costs nothing at this frame, and the quarter-day rung is running under it now. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 75 (2026-08-19)

**2026-08-19 update 75 (the quarter-day rung clears the bar as well, and is the strongest of the four: pooled 0.9234, within-cell 0.6574, seventeen windows of twenty-one, p 0.0022; but the Brier skill score thins to plus 0.0014 and that is the thing to work on next).** The previous update left the quarter-day rung running. It has finished, on the same one degree grid and the same twenty-one one-year windows, with the same rolling twelve-year training span used at half a day. The pooled AUC is 0.9234 against a climatology arm of 0.9001, the within-cell figure is 0.6574, and the twenty-one per-window values average 0.5422 with seventeen above a half, a t of 3.510. Recomputed from the stored predictions by the second program that shares no code with the first, the per-window values agree exactly, and a label permutation inside each cell puts the floor at 0.4990. Judged the way this project judges things, it is the most solid of the four rungs: the t test gives p 0.0022, the sign test 0.0072, the signed-rank test 0.0016, and dropping any single one of the twenty-one windows leaves p no worse than 0.0047, including dropping the strongest. So the ladder has not turned over. Across four rungs the within-cell skill runs 0.5295, 0.6267, 0.6500, 0.6574 and the pooled figure runs 0.8995, 0.9160, 0.9209, 0.9234, and the shortest frame measured is still the best one. What is worth saying plainly is what moves the other way. The Brier skill score against the base rate goes plus 0.0133 at a day, plus 0.0049 at half a day, plus 0.0014 at a quarter, and the reason is visible in the base rate itself, which falls from 4.95 in ten thousand to 2.57 to 1.33 as the frame shortens. The power to rank cells against each other keeps rising while the room to be right about the probability of any one of them keeps shrinking, and a forecast that ranks well but carries almost no calibration advantage over the base rate is not the whole of what a forecast should be. Shortening the frame further would very likely buy more ranking and less calibration again, which is why the next step is not another rung. It is to stop asking one model to do both: build the ordering where the short frame is strong and attach the probability where the longer frame still has room, and judge the two parts on their own terms. None of this moves the thirty-four-day product, which remains at 0.8995 and is the figure the stated goal refers to; the rungs reported here answer whether a cell is active over the next few hours to a day, which is a different question with different labels, and the two are not compared with a p-value. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 76 (2026-08-19)

**2026-08-19 update 76 (the baseline was wrong: a catalogue stripped of its timing was run through the same pipeline, and one arm of this round came back REAL on it, so every arm is now measured against its own floor rather than against one half; the half-day result of the last two updates survives that test, one reading from this round is reversed by it, and one claim is withdrawn).** Two questions were asked after update 75. The first was whether the gain from shortening the frame belongs to the fine time resolution or to the short horizon, and it was settled by holding the horizon at thirty-four days, the production question, and shortening only the bin: three days, one day, half a day and a quarter day give 0.8994, 0.8984, 0.8993 and 0.8987, which is flat, with the within-cell-and-fold figure below one half at every rung. The gain belongs to the short horizon. The second question was whether the model could be handed the contrast inside a cell as its own column, since a column can point one way between cells and the other way inside one, and a single fitted model cannot serve both. Three arms were run at the production frame: the plain columns, those plus cell-demeaned copies, and climatology plus the demeaned copies. They read 0.4799, 0.4951 and 0.4998, which looks like steady improvement toward one half. It is not, and finding out why is the substance of this update. The test this project uses asks whether the per-window figures clear one half between windows. What that test returns when the earthquakes have no timing at all had never been measured. So each cell kept its event count and its magnitudes, every event was given a time that knows nothing about the others, and features and labels were rebuilt from those times by the same code. The floors that came back are not one half. For the plain arm the floor is 0.4853 and 0.4915 on two seeds; for the arm with demeaned copies it is 0.5074 and 0.5101, and on that arm the between-window test returns REAL on a catalogue with no memory, on both seeds. Read against their own floors the three arms are minus 0.005, minus 0.014 and minus 0.009: not improvement, and the plain arm is the best of the three. The apparent climb was the floor moving under the measurement. Two things follow. The claim that the thirty-four-day product is anti-informative inside a cell is withdrawn; it sits at its floor, which says there is no skill in that dimension rather than negative skill. And the half-day result of updates 74 and 75 was put through the same test: its floor is 0.4823 and 0.4887 with a NOT REAL verdict, against 0.6500 and REAL on the real catalogue, so that claim stands as written and now stands on a measured baseline rather than an assumed one. One more thing belongs in the record because it nearly went the other way. The first version of the redrawn catalogue sorted the drawn times while leaving each event's place and magnitude on its own row, which handed every event back the neighbours it had before and left the bursts intact; that broken null scored higher than the real catalogue and would have retracted a correct result. It was caught by measuring the thing the redraw was supposed to destroy: the share of events whose next neighbour in the same cell falls within half a day is 45.6 per cent in the catalogue, 44.8 with the sort, and 22.9 without it. That check now runs at the top of the driver before any rung. The permutation of model scores used until now returns one half by construction and is a check on the arithmetic, not on the instrument. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 77 (2026-08-19)

**2026-08-19 update 77 (the Brier skill that kept thinning as the frame narrowed was not a calibration fault and there was nothing to repair; the score itself turns out not to compare across catalogues, so the axis has been replaced by how much each cell's own worst hours are enriched; and the quarter-day rung, published two updates ago against a borrowed floor, has now been given its own and stands).** Update 76 left one number unexplained. As the frame narrowed the ordering kept improving while the Brier skill fell away, from +0.0593 at the production frame to +0.0133, +0.0049 and +0.0014. Two readings were possible: the probabilities were mis-scaled and a monotone rescaling would recover the skill, or the ordering gain sits on too few rows for any rescaling to buy anything at a base rate of 1.43e-04. The two were separated without refitting a single model, from the probabilities already on disk, and the rule for deciding between them was written down first. A Murphy decomposition splits the score into reliability, resolution and uncertainty. Reliability is the part a rescaling can remove, and at the quarter-day rung it is 3.4e-05 of the uncertainty: there is no mis-scaling to correct. The ceiling of any monotone map is +0.0017, and an isotonic map fitted three ways -- on earlier windows only, on every window but the one being scored, and on that window itself -- moved the median of sixteen scored windows by +0.0001, winning ten of sixteen. The first reading is rejected and the second accepted: the thinning is resolution, and resolution at these base rates is bounded by the base rate. The same measurement then disqualified the score. Run through the identical pipeline, the catalogue whose event times know nothing about each other returns a HIGHER Brier skill at the production frame than the real one, +0.1122 and +0.1153 against +0.0501, because bursts punish squared error. A quantity that rewards the absence of clustering cannot be the axis on which clustering is claimed, and it is no longer reported as one. What does compare across frames is enrichment: flag the top one per cent of a cell's own time steps and ask what share of that cell's events fall inside. At the production frame that is 1.78 times chance, against floors of 0.95 and 0.89. At the half-day rung it is 13.65 against 1.01 and 1.12, and at the quarter-day rung 15.11 against 0.73 and 1.11. Pooled over everything rather than within cells, the same enrichment is 17.32 where its own floor is 17.59 and 18.29 -- at the production frame the pooled figure carries no timing information whatsoever, which is the plainest statement yet of why every result here is read within cells. Finally the gap left by update 76 has been closed. The quarter-day rung is the strongest result on this page and it had been read next to the half-day floor, which the protocol does not allow. Its own floor was measured on two memoryless seeds: window means of 0.4665 and 0.4748, one and seven windows of twenty-one above one half, NOT REAL on both. The real catalogue gives 0.5422 with a one-sided lower bound of 0.5171 and twenty of twenty-one windows above the floor, and its enrichment clears the floor by more than thirteen times. The claim published in update 75 stands exactly as written. One more thing is now settled across three frames: a pooled AUC near 0.91 appears on catalogues with no memory at all, and beneath the climatological arm at that, so a pooled figure is not evidence of anything on its own. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 78 (2026-08-20)

**2026-08-20 update 78 (the catalogue was lengthened to thirty-three and a half years and the half-day result replicated in seven years that had never been used; then the nine candidate columns were re-measured at that frame, each against its own floor, and one of them, with nothing fitted at all, ranks better inside a cell and a year than the eleven-column model trained on it).** Two rounds. The first added windows. The catalogue the ladder runs on covers 2000 onward; a second one reaches back to 1983, and the question was how far back it can honestly be joined. That was settled by counting rather than by choosing: at the driver threshold this pipeline uses, M>=2.5, the years 1983 to 1992 hold between 3097 and 6049 events each while the 2000s hold between 9519 and 12669, so below 1993 the driver field is a different instrument. From 1993 the rate is stationary, and stopping there means the threshold does not have to move -- raising it to M>=3.5 to reach 1983 would make a different arm needing its own floor, which is the opposite of what a round about window count should do. The two catalogues were joined at 2000-01-01, giving 1993.000 to 2026.540, 2,689,194 events, a sorted time axis, and a largest gap anywhere in the catalogue of 0.30 day sitting at 1994.225 rather than at the seam. The harness now asserts the span and the end year when it loads, so a catalogue that stops short can never again pass unnoticed. On 28 windows instead of 21 the half-day rung gives a per-window within-cell AUC of 0.5444 against floors of 0.4770 and 0.4746 from two memoryless seeds, a one-sided lower bound of 0.5207, and 25 of 28 windows above the floor. The standard error fell from 0.0128 to 0.0115, which was the stated reason for the round, but it is not the interesting number. The seven windows the extension added, 1998 to 2004, had never been touched by any run on this page, and on their own they give 0.5476 with five of seven above one half -- the effect replicated out of period. Across the 21 calendar years the two runs share, the windows correlate at 0.946 with a mean shift of +0.0011, so the arm was lengthened rather than changed. The second round went back to a question parked in August. Nine candidate columns had been ranked on a three-day bin and had moved quantities between 0.004 and 0.012, too small to matter, and they were parked. They were never given floors, and floors are what reversed an entire round later. Re-measured at the half-day frame against their own memoryless floors, all nine clear, with residuals between 0.057 and 0.105 -- an order of magnitude larger than the same columns showed at the coarse frame. Two of them have floors at 0.5187 and 0.5195 rather than at one half, so read against one half their signs would have been misread, exactly the error the floor protocol exists to prevent. Then the columns and the model were put on one row set: 13,642,818 rows, 3,355 positives, the same 28 windows, differing by zero rows and zero positives. The fitted eleven-column model scores 0.5608 within a cell and a year. A single raw column, a three-day count in the cell with nothing fitted to it at all, scores 0.6005, and a magnitude-weighted one-day sum scores 0.6008. Within a cell but across years the two are level, 0.6482 against 0.6478. The gap is entirely in the fast, within-year direction. This is not a claim that the model is worse than a count: the columns were chosen for this metric and the model was trained to minimise log loss, most of which is decided by which cell a row belongs to. It is a claim about where the capacity goes. The information is present in the model's own inputs and is not reaching its output, and that is a lever rather than a limit. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 79 (2026-08-20)

**2026-08-20 update 79 (four rounds spent on one question -- whether anything can be made to rank better inside a cell and a year than a raw count fitted to nothing -- and the answer, so far, is no; what the rounds did establish is that fitting is not free).** Update 78 ended with a column that had nothing fitted to it outranking the eleven-column model on the same 13,642,818 rows. Four rounds followed. The first asked whether the two could simply be added together, since a model trained on log loss and a column chosen for this metric might hold different things. The scores were turned into ranks inside each window and averaged; the average lands at 0.5834 where the column alone is 0.6005, and the per-window difference is -0.0194. That figure alone would say the join hurts, and it would be the wrong reading: on memoryless catalogues, where neither arm can know anything, the same join gives -0.0153 and -0.0165. Averaging a score with a weaker one costs about that much by construction. Against its own floor the join is at zero, neither helping nor hurting. The weights say it more plainly than the averages do. Allowed to choose a weight on the earlier windows only, the procedure put zero on the model in twenty-seven windows out of twenty-seven; allowed to look at the answer in its own window, it still chose zero in twenty-three of twenty-eight. The model carries nothing here that the column does not. So the second round left the output alone and changed what the learner is asked to do. Every positive row in the training span was matched with fifty negatives drawn from its own cell and its own calendar year, which makes both the cell and the year uninformative inside the sample, so the only way to separate the cases is the part that moves fast. That moved the model from 0.5608 to 0.6201, the largest single move any intervention has produced on this measure. It also clears its floor by 0.0007, and dropping an arbitrary single window removes the verdict in twelve of twenty-eight tries, so it is recorded as suggestive and not as established, and the decision rule was tightened for everything after it: a result now has to clear its floor, hold in at least sixty per cent of windows, survive the loss of any one window, and clear zero when each window is paired with its own floor. The third round gave that same objective the nine columns measured in update 78, on the reasoning that columns shown to carry information at this frame should beat eleven columns shaped for a thirty-four-day horizon. They do not: 0.5839 against 0.6021, and against the raw column the gain is negative once the floor is taken off. The fourth round stopped fitting altogether and built six more columns by hand, including the triggering rate an ETAS model would write down, evaluated with no parameter fitted. All eight readings sit inside their floors. One of them is worth keeping for the method rather than the result: the magnitude-weighted Omori sum reads 0.5085, which against one half looks like a column pointing the wrong way, while its own floor is 0.4087, which makes it one of the strongest columns measured. Read against one half it would have been thrown away. Set out against their floors the whole set orders as follows: the raw three-day count at +0.0875, the Omori sum at +0.0848, an unweighted rank average of three counts at +0.0839, the matched eleven-column model at +0.0825, the matched nine-column model at +0.0734, and the model as it is trained in production at +0.0674. Fitting raises the floor from about 0.49 to about 0.52 and does not return the difference. Fifteen or so constructions now sit in a band between 0.05 and 0.088, and column work does not leave it. None of this is a statement about the thirty-four-day product, which is where the target is measured and where the next round goes. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 80 (2026-08-20)

**2026-08-20 update 80 (the target's own number is measured on a longer catalogue and a sparser driver stream, comes out at 0.9010 with a positive Brier skill, and fails the bar that was written down before it was measured -- the bar being the point of the round).** Three rounds, and the first of them was aimed somewhere else. The eleven columns this pipeline scores on end with three that stand in for climatology: the running count of events in the cell, its latitude and its longitude. That is a raw per-cell count at one degree with no spatial smoothing whatever, which is what the smoothed-seismicity literature replaced decades ago, so four smoothed columns were added -- three fixed Gaussian widths and one whose width is set for each event by the distance to its eighth nearest neighbour among events from earlier years. They do nothing: the pooled AUC moves by -0.0004, which is inside the floor the same comparison shows on memoryless catalogues, and the Brier skill falls by 0.0068. A cell at one degree is 111 km across and the smoothing lengths that matter are smaller than that, so the result is recorded as a fact about the grid rather than about smoothing, and the same intervention is left open for a finer one. The round's reference arm turned out to carry the news. On the thirty-three and a half year catalogue the production model reads a pooled AUC of 0.9009 with a Brier skill of +0.0648 over 28 windows; cut to the 21 windows the earlier ladder used it reads 0.9029 and +0.0723 where that ladder read 0.8994 and +0.0573, and it is above in 18 of those 21 windows. The whole of that change is seven more years of training history. The second round asked the same question on rows identical to the digit rather than on windows whose edges differ by days, by training one arm on everything and the other only on rows from 2000 onward: +0.0045 per window, which does not clear its floor by the four-part rule now in force, with the Brier skill up either way. It also priced a higher driver threshold, and the price was negative -- drivers at M>=3.5 read +0.0013 per window against M>=2.5 -- which triggered a branch fixed in advance and sent the work to a forty-three year arm. That arm needed a check first, and the check overturned the premise it was built on. The rule written down for choosing how far back to reach keyed on the largest yearly count over the smallest, and 2011 holds 14,444 events at M>=3.5 against a median near 2,400, so the rule started the arm in 2012 and left nine windows. Nothing had been fitted and no score had been looked at, so the rule was replaced with a median-based one on the spot and the replacement recorded as a correction rather than applied quietly. Measured that way the yearly count for 1983 to 1991 against 2000 to 2025 is 0.288 of it at M>=2.5, 0.469 at M>=3.5, 0.845 at M>=4.5 and 0.993 at M>=5.0. The M>=5 rate is level across the whole span while the M>=3.5 rate is less than half, so what is thin before 1992 is the catalogue's completeness and not the earthquakes, and reaching 1983 with one instrument needs M>=4.5. That threshold was priced the same way, cost 0.0010 per window, and the arm was built: 1983 to 2026, 19,614 drivers, 38 windows, 3,086,209 rows, 39,797 positives. It reads a pooled AUC of 0.9010 with a Brier skill of +0.0608 -- indistinguishable from the 0.9009 on 28 windows and the 0.9012 at the intermediate threshold. The bar it was measured against was fixed before the run: the target counts as met only when the 2.5th percentile of the pooled AUC over 2000 resamples of the windows lies above 0.90 and every resample keeps the Brier skill positive. The percentile is 0.8958, the median is 0.9009, 63.5 per cent of draws are above 0.90 where 28 windows gave 50 per cent, and all 2000 draws keep the skill positive. The bar is not met. Ten more years of catalogue moved the interval and not the estimate, which says the remaining uncertainty is between years rather than within them -- resampling cases instead of windows gives a standard error of 0.0006 and an interval of 0.8998 to 0.9022. More windows is therefore not the lever; a better arm is, and the next one is the same smoothing question asked on a half-degree grid where a cell is not 111 km across. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 81 (2026-08-21)

**2026-08-21 update 81 (a finer grid clears the AUC half of the bar for the first time and fails the other half, and the reason it fails turns out to be the same fact that says where the work goes next: the pooled number is climatology and the part that should carry time points backwards).** The bar has two halves and update 80 failed the first: the 2.5th percentile of the pooled AUC over 2000 resamples of the windows stood at 0.8958 where it has to stand above 0.90, with the Brier skill positive in every resample. This round changed the grid, since a cell at one degree is 111 km across and the reading that closed update 80 said the lengths that matter are shorter than that. At half a degree there are 2,565 cells instead of 667 and the same 43-year catalogue, the same drivers at M>=4.5, the same three-day bin, thirty-four day horizon, learner and thirty-eight windows. The pooled AUC reads 0.9148 against 0.9010 and the percentile reads 0.9029, which is above 0.90 for the first time in this project. The Brier skill reads +0.0133 against +0.0608 and is negative in 4.2 per cent of the resamples, so the bar is not met -- and the clause that failed it was written into the pre-registration before the run, because a half-degree run here once read 0.9011 with a negative skill and was recorded as hollow. Splitting the Brier score the way Murphy did says which of the two possible faults this is. Reliability, the part a recalibration can repair, is 1.35 per cent of the uncertainty; resolution, the part that is information, is 3.12 per cent where the one-degree arm has 6.94. A perfect recalibration of the finer grid would reach +0.0312 and stop there. The finer grid did not learn more about when an earthquake happens; it made the negatives easier to reject, which raises an AUC and cannot raise a skill score, and the guard caught exactly what it was put there to catch. Adding the four smoothed background columns at this resolution -- the intervention update 80 left open, on the argument that a finer grid was where smoothing should matter -- moves the pooled AUC by -0.0024 and the skill by -0.0052; no floor was measured for it in this round, so it is recorded as a reading with no verdict attached. Then the folds were read a second way, counting pairs only inside one cell and one window, and this is the part worth keeping. The one-degree arm reads 0.4357 there. Its two memoryless floors -- the same catalogue with every time redrawn, so that nothing can be known -- read 0.4634 and 0.4492. The production model is below its own floor on the within-cell contrast, which means its 0.9010 is carried by which cell a row is in and by nothing else, and that the part of the problem that is about when, inside a cell, is not merely absent but pointed the wrong way. There is a mechanism that would produce that, and it is not being assumed: with a thirty-four day horizon stepped every three days a row is labelled by what happens after it while every column is built from what happened before it, so the columns rise in the bins that follow an event and the label is high in the bins that precede one, and a single fit rewarded for ordering cells can order the times inside them backwards and still score 0.90. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 82 (2026-08-21)

**2026-08-21 update 82 (two rounds aimed at the part of the forecast that carries the score, and then a measurement of how much room that part has left, which turned out to be less than the first version of the measurement said).** Update 81 ended with the production model below its own memoryless floor when pairs are counted inside a cell, which said the pooled 0.9010 is the ordering of cells rather than of times within them. The first round took that literally and stopped asking one fit to do both jobs: the three background columns were fitted alone, the eleven columns were fitted again on a sample matched inside cell and calendar year so neither the cell nor the year could separate the cases, and the two were combined by a two-coefficient logistic whose weights were fitted only on windows earlier than the one being scored. The matched fit does what it was built to do -- within cells it moves from 0.4343 to 0.5829 -- but its own floor moves with it to 0.5812, so the margin is 0.0017, the same order as an earlier 0.0007 that failed, and the pre-registration had already said to record that as not established. What it declared in advance follows: at a thirty-four day horizon the within-cell contrast is not there to be had, which is a fact about the horizon rather than the method, since the same construction works at half a day. The combination itself also landed inside its floor. What did clear the floor was the arm put in as a control, to tell a win by the factorisation apart from a win by the matched sample: the production model combined with the background-only model under the same out-of-sample weights reads 0.9036 against 0.9006 with the Brier skill up from +0.0612 to +0.0694, positive in 89 per cent of windows, clearing all four conditions. It is recorded with the caveat a later cut of the same numbers forces: the gain over the floor is +0.0035 in the first seventeen windows and -0.0001 in the last ten, so it is worth what a short training history is worth and not more. The second round went after the background itself. The column that orders the cells is a running count of the driver stream at M>=4.5, which on this grid is 18,901 events over the 359 cells that hold any, a median of sixteen per cell across forty-three years, while the same catalogue file holds 466,828 events at M>=2.5 and 111,045 at M>=3.5, medians of 223 and 67. Two columns counting those were added in exactly the form the existing one uses, and the arm reads 0.9005 against 0.9010 with the skill down: inside its floor on all four conditions. Fourteen times as many events in the background do not reorder the cells. Then the room itself was measured, and this is the part that changed the plan. Give every row its own cell's label rate over the whole test set and the pooled AUC is 0.9162; give it that cell's rate in its own window and it is 0.9724. Both oracles peek, so both are bounds and not forecasts, and the first of them turned out to be mostly peeking: learn the per-cell rate on the even windows and score the odd ones, and the other way round so the eras match, and an honest time-invariant score reads 0.8956. The production arm is already above that, at 0.9010. So the climatology axis is not a reservoir with 0.015 in it; it is spent, which is consistent with every null this round and the last two have produced -- smoothing, grid, catalogue length, background density. Whatever is still owed on 0.90 has to come from the time-varying side. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 83 (2026-08-21)

**2026-08-21 update 83 (the drift a cell's own history can see is nothing, the stack that was found by accident is real and reproduces under a rule written in advance, and conditioning its weights on how much is triggering nearby adds exactly nothing).** Update 82 closed with an honest ceiling for any time-invariant score of 0.8956, which the production arm at 0.9010 is already above, and with the second oracle -- the one that knows each cell's rate in the window being scored -- reading 0.9724. The distance between those two is the drift, so the first round of this pair went looking for it in the obvious place. Six columns were added to the eleven, each a running count inside a trailing window that ends one step before the row it describes: one year, three years and ten years, on the drivers at M>=4.5 and on the dense stream at M>=2.5. The arm reads 0.9012 against 0.9010 with the Brier skill up a little, and +0.0001 per window. Its floors read +0.0013 and +0.0022, because adding six columns is worth something even on a catalogue whose times have been redrawn and where nothing can be known, so the arm is inside its floor on all four conditions. A cell's own recent history does not recover the drift. Probes with no fitting at all say why, and they are worth more than the null: score every row by its own cell's rate in the previous window and the pooled AUC is 0.7286, by the mean of the previous three 0.8178, by every earlier window 0.8757, and by every earlier window smoothed over the eight neighbours 0.8893. Year-to-year persistence at the cell scale is weak; most of what the second oracle knows is which sequences happened, which is hindsight and not a signal. The second round came from a different reading of the same folds. Per window, the production arm and the background-only arm were put side by side: in the nineteen quieter windows the production arm reads 0.8926 and the background alone 0.8950, in the eighteen busier ones 0.9059 and 0.9012, and the difference between the two arms correlates with how busy the window is at +0.637. The arm is better than climatology when something is triggering and worse when nothing is, which says the weight between them should not be a constant. So the weights were conditioned on an index fixed before anything was fitted: the count of drivers in the trailing ninety days in the cell's own three-by-three neighbourhood, in three strata. Nothing was refitted -- the round is a combiner over scores already on disk, for the real catalogue and for both memoryless ones. Conditioning adds nothing: -0.0002 per window against the unconditioned stack, inside its floor on every condition, exactly the null the pre-registration described in advance. What the round does confirm, this time with the comparison declared before the run rather than found in a control arm, is the stack itself: +0.0035 per window over the production arm against a floor of +0.0015, positive in 89 per cent of windows, all four conditions, pooled 0.9036 against 0.9006 and the Brier skill +0.0694 against +0.0612. Measured against the bar, the stack reads a 2.5th percentile of 0.8985 with the Brier skill positive in all 2000 resamples: the skill half of the bar is met and the AUC half is short by 0.0015. What the two rounds together say is narrow and useful -- the combination is real, the conditioning is not, and so what the stack is missing is not better weights but a member that fails somewhere else. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 84 (2026-08-21)

**2026-08-21 update 84 (three members that differ in resolution, in functional form and in horizon; all three sit inside the floor, and the floor says what a new member is really worth).** Update 83 ended by narrowing the question: the combination of the background and the production arm is real and reproduces under a rule written in advance, conditioning its weights on nearby triggering adds nothing, and so what the stack is missing is not better weights but a member that fails somewhere else. Three rounds asked what somewhere else could mean, each varying one thing and refitting nothing that had already been fitted. The first varied resolution. The half-degree arm built earlier was lifted to the degree grid by a noisy-or over the cells it covers -- the two grids both place a cell centre at 24N 122E, so they do not nest, and assigning each half-degree cell to its nearest degree centre gives 616 cells four children, 50 cells two and one cell one. Added to the two-member stack it reads 0.9043 pooled with a Brier skill of +0.0719, against 0.9036 and +0.0694, which is +0.0006 per window and positive in 73 per cent of them. Its floors, built on catalogues whose times have been redrawn so that nothing can be known, read +0.0006 and +0.0009: the arm is inside them. Aggregated to the degree label, the finer ordering carries nothing the degree arm does not already have. The second varied functional form, and it is the first time in this project that the learner itself was changed rather than what it is fed: a logistic regression on the same eleven columns, standardised by the statistics of the training window, and a slower and deeper forest -- eight hundred trees, learning rate 0.03, seven leaves, L2 of 5.0 -- with every setting written down before the run. The four-member stack reads 0.9051 and +0.0801, the best numbers this project has produced. Per window it is +0.0011 and positive in 57 per cent, against floors of +0.0021 and +0.0024, so it too is inside. Its bar reading is the more useful part. The script printed that the target was met at a 2.5th percentile of 0.9002; rebuilt independently over twenty seeds the same quantity reads between 0.8999 and 0.9003 and clears in fourteen of the twenty, at twenty thousand resamples it reads 0.9001, and with any single window dropped it clears in twenty-three of thirty-seven. That is a verdict sitting on its own boundary, so it is recorded as not met, and from that round the bar was made stricter and fixed in advance: twenty thousand resamples, and it must still hold when any one window is removed. The third varied horizon -- members fitted on whether an M5 follows within seven days and within ninety, on the same rows and the same columns, with the thirty-four-day spacing between steps untouched. That stack reads 0.9047 and +0.0746, +0.0009 per window and positive in 73 per cent against floors of +0.0011 and +0.0013, inside again, and under the stricter bar its 2.5th percentile is 0.8999 and it survives the dropped window in nine of thirty-seven. Taken together the three rounds say something the headline numbers hide: what a stack gains by acquiring another member is mostly variance reduction, and the floors measure exactly that, because on catalogues with no memory at all a new member is still worth between +0.0006 and +0.0024 per window. Judged against zero, all three rounds would have looked like progress. There is also an arithmetic fact about the bar worth stating plainly. With thirty-seven windows and a window-to-window standard deviation of 0.0145, the 2.5th percentile of the window resample sits about 0.0047 below the mean, so the mean has to reach roughly 0.909 before the bar is cleared with any margin, and cutting the windows finer does not help, because the luck being resampled is the luck of a year. Variance reduction alone will not get there; the next member has to be fitted to different data. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 85 (2026-08-22)

**2026-08-22 update 85 (a gate written in advance closed the road that added test windows, and the road it left open -- twenty more years to train on, with nothing else touched -- met the bar this project set for itself in advance).** Update 84 ended with an arithmetic fact: with thirty-seven windows and a window-to-window standard deviation of 0.0145 the 2.5th percentile of the window resample sits about 0.0047 below the mean, so the mean has to reach roughly 0.909 before the bar clears with any margin. Two ways out of that follow, more windows or a better mean, and the catalogue offers both, because the Japan Meteorological Agency's unified hypocentre archive runs back to 1919 in four multi-year bundles totalling 2.67 MB. Whether any of it is usable was measured before it was planned around. Counting events per year against the modern median says no year before 1982 reaches ninety per cent at M5, but that test cannot separate poor detection from a quiet era and the era it compares against holds 1995, 2011 and 2016, so the shape of the magnitude distribution was used instead. By the goodness-of-fit test the completeness magnitude is 4.2 for 1968 to 1982 and 4.4 for the 1960s, both below the label threshold, and the b-value above M5 breaks cleanly at 1960: 0.657, 0.798, 0.800 and 0.777 for the 1920s through the 1950s, then 0.953 and 0.982 for the 1960s and 1970s against 0.901 to 1.049 for every modern decade. From 1961 the catalogue behaves like the modern one where it matters; before 1960 it does not, and it was not used. The extension was built to start exactly 7305 days earlier, which is 2435 three-day steps and twenty years of 365.25 days at once, so the extended block's step grid and window boundaries fall on the same absolute dates as the existing one and the overlap could be checked by equality: 5302 steps of labels identical element for element. The first round then walked the production arm over the longer window set and read its own gate, which had been fixed before the run: stop if the twenty new windows score more than 0.010 below the shared ones. They read 0.8927 against 0.9033, which is -0.0106, so that road is closed and no result rests on it. The same walk carried a second reading. On the thirty-eight windows both catalogues share, the arm trained from 1963 reads 0.9033 per window against 0.8995 for the same arm trained from 1983, and the gain concentrates in the earlier shared windows, which is what twenty more years of history would be expected to do. So the second round changed one thing and nothing else: the training rows begin in 1963. Same windows, same M5 label, same thirty-four-day horizon, same one-degree grid, same learner, same four members, same combiner fitted only on earlier windows, the same 3004835 rows, and the labels asserted equal window by window against the round before. The 1963 to 1982 rows lie strictly before every test window, the first of which opens in 1988. The production arm reads 0.9043 pooled against 0.9006 with the Brier skill +0.0729 against +0.0612; the two-member stack 0.9060 against 0.9036 and +0.0765 against +0.0694; the four-member stack 0.9073 against 0.9055 and +0.0795 against +0.0749. Read against the bar as it was tightened in round 119 -- twenty thousand window resamples, the 2.5th percentile above 0.90, the Brier skill positive in every resample, and all of it still true when any single window is dropped -- the four-member stack gives 0.9025 across five seeds, the skill positive throughout, and it holds with every one of the thirty-seven windows removed in turn, the worst of those readings being 0.9016. The two-member stack, which is the arm three earlier rounds established against memoryless floors, gives 0.9011 and also holds thirty-seven times out of thirty-seven. All of that was recomputed by a second implementation that refits the combiner from the member folds rather than reading the saved one, and agrees to within two parts in a million. Two things must be said in the same breath. No memoryless floors were built for this step, so whether the longer span adds information or only reduces variance is not answered here and is deliberately left to a round of its own. And the pooled figure is still mostly a statement about which cell: round 113 showed there is no within-cell contrast at this horizon. What the bar says is what it was written to say, no more -- that on this task, measured this way, with the rule fixed in advance, the score is above 0.90 and stays there. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 86 (2026-08-22)

**2026-08-22 update 86 (the floor that update 85 left for a round of its own now says the longer training span bought estimation and not information, and a baseline measured at the same time says the 0.90 threshold sits close to where climatology already is on this task).** Update 85 reported that moving the training rows back to 1963, with nothing else touched, met the bar this project fixed in advance, and it said in the same breath that no memoryless floors had been built for that step, so whether the longer span added information or only reduced variance was unanswered. It is answered now. For each of two seeds the driver times of the 1963-2026 catalogue were redrawn uniformly, and from that single redraw two blocks were built: the sixty-three-year one, and the forty-three-year one made from the same redrawn events restricted to 1983 onward. Because both come from one redraw the comparison inside a floor world is the comparison the real catalogue got, differing only in training span, and their labels on the shared steps were asserted equal element for element. The production arm was then walked on both. On the real catalogue the sixty-three-year arm beats the forty-three-year one by +0.0035 per window over the thirty-seven windows, positive in 78 per cent of them. On the floors the same quantity reads +0.0036 and +0.0038, positive in 84 and 92 per cent. The gains are -0.0001 and -0.0003: the real improvement is inside its floor on the first, third and fourth conditions and the two seeds agree closely enough that this is not noise. Twenty more years of rows buy about as much where the times carry no information as where they do, which is what a pooled figure dominated by which-cell-is-it should do when the cells' rates are estimated from more data. So the bar was met by a better-estimated model rather than a better-informed one. That does not undo the bar, which is a pre-registered statement about the score and was verified by a second implementation, but it is the honest account of why the score moved. A second measurement made alongside the floor sharpens the reading further, and it is less comfortable. The background arm alone -- the same learner on the three background columns, trained from 1963 like everything else -- reads 0.9021 pooled on these thirty-seven windows. Climatology with the same data is already above 0.90 here. Crossing 0.90 is therefore a fact about where the threshold sits on this task and not by itself evidence of forecasting skill, and this write-up should have said so earlier than it did. What the four-member stack is actually worth is its margin over that baseline on the same rows: +0.0051 pooled, +0.0047 per window with a 95 per cent half-width of 0.0013, positive in 92 per cent of the windows; the two-member stack +0.0034 per window in 86 per cent, and the production arm alone +0.0014 in 62 per cent. Those margins are small and they are consistent, and they are the part of the number that is about forecasting rather than about geography. For completeness the interleaved static ceiling on these windows measures 0.8952, close to the 0.8956 reported earlier, and it does not move when the training span does because it is computed from the evaluation windows' own labels; but it is a weak upper bound on what a time-invariant score can do when that score is allowed more data, and the background arm's 0.9021 is the demonstration. The next thing to try is written down before it is run. Every member added since update 84 varied something about the model while leaving the input at a three-day bin, and the one asymmetry this project has never bridged is that at a quarter-day frame there is real within-cell ordering -- 0.5422 against a floor of 0.4665 -- while at the thirty-four-day product there is none at all, 0.4357 against a floor of 0.4634. The builder takes the frame as an argument and holds the kernel's reach in days constant, so a member whose columns are computed at a finer frame and read off at the same three-day rows is a small change to make and has not been made. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 87 (2026-08-22)

**2026-08-22 update 87 (the finer input frame that update 86 said it would try changes nothing at thirty-four days, and the floor explains why: the member that was added is nearly a duplicate of the one already there, while a member that is uncorrelated by construction is worth more).** Update 86 ended by naming the next thing to try and by saying it would be written down before it was run. It was. The pre-registration fixed the primary as the four-member stack with the new member minus the same stack without it, per window, under the four-part rule with two memoryless floors, and the co-primary as the within-cell ordering of the new member against the production one. The construction: the same catalogue, grid, windows, learner and columns as update 85, but the columns computed at a one-day frame and read off at every third step. Three is a whole multiple of one, so the rows that are kept are the same absolute dates as the production block, and their labels must therefore be equal to it element for element, nan placement included; that was asserted and it held over 7,737 steps. What changes is only what the columns see: the most recent day is resolved instead of being lumped into a three-day bin, with the reach of the kernel in days unchanged. The answer is that it changes nothing. The new arm reads 0.904219 pooled against the production arm at 0.904294, and its margin over the background arm is +0.0013 per window against +0.0014, so the two arms are the same arm to within the noise. Adding it to the stack moves the stack from 0.907265 to 0.907180, which is -0.0001 per window and positive in 46 of every 100 windows. The floors put a number on how little that is: on two memoryless redraws the same addition is worth +0.0016 and +0.0018, so the real gain is -0.0017 and -0.0019 and all four conditions fail. The co-primary agrees: the within-cell ordering reads 0.4260 against 0.4271, inside its floor. Two corrections belong with those numbers. The first is that there are two different within-cell statistics in this project and they are not close. One counts concordant pairs only inside a cell and inside a single window, which is what the earlier 0.4357 was; the other pools a cell across windows and so also rewards getting the level of a cell right from one year to the next. The same production arm reads 0.4271 by the first and 0.5655 by the second. The reading script had been using the second while the motivation was stated in the first; it now computes both, the verdict is read on the first, and the second is reported beside it. The second correction is larger. The level of that ordering had never been compared with its own construction floor on this block, only with one half. It has been now, using the memoryless production arms already walked for update 86: they read 0.4338 and 0.4351 where the real one reads 0.4271, a difference of -0.0088 per window with a 95 per cent half-width of 0.0117, which is not established. So the honest statement is not that the columns order a cell backwards in time. It is that at thirty-four days this dimension carries no force in either direction, which is what an earlier round concluded and what the short-horizon results have said all along. The most useful thing this round produced is the explanation of its own null, and it comes from the floor rather than from the arm. A member built on a time-scrambled catalogue is worth +0.0017 to the stack. That is not nothing, and it invited the suspicion that the floor is inflated, because a scrambled catalogue keeps every event with its own place and size and only moves its time, so the columns of a floor block describe where events fall over the whole record including after the row they sit on. Scored against the real labels on the same rows, those floor arms read 0.901486 and 0.901165, against 0.902117 for the honest background arm that sees only the past: -0.0008 per window with a half-width of 0.0030, which is not a difference. The floor is not peeking. Its +0.0017 is what a fifth column whose errors are uncorrelated with the other four is worth to a fitted combination, and the real one-day member earned -0.0001 because its errors are the production arm errors. That is the sentence worth keeping: the finer frame failed because it was redundant, not because it was weak, and any member that is another reading of the same catalogue will be correlated with what is already there. Passing the diversity bonus needs a different observation system or a denser catalogue, not another rearrangement of this one. One question about this catalogue is still open and is being run now, pre-registered as before. A thirty-four-day label on a three-day grid is 1 on about eleven consecutive rows of a cell, and those rows come before the event while the columns rise after it, so a fit rewarded for ordering cells can reach 0.90 while leaving the order of times inside a cell untouched. If that is the reason the within-cell dimension is empty, then the culprit is the overlap of the label and not the length of the horizon, and it is testable: at a three-day horizon on a three-day grid the label tiles the timeline exactly and does not overlap at all. The arm, its two floors and the rule for reading them were fixed in writing before the round was coded. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 88 (2026-08-22)

**2026-08-22 update 88 (a claim this write-up has been making since update 82 turns out to be too strong: the thirty-four-day product does have a within-cell time ordering underneath it, and what removes it is the overlap of the label rather than the length of the horizon).** Several earlier updates reported that at thirty-four days there is no ordering of times inside a cell, and update 87 repeated it after measuring the level against its own construction floor for the first time. An explanation for that emptiness had been written down long before and never tested. A thirty-four-day label read on a three-day grid is 1 on about eleven consecutive rows of a cell, and those rows come before the event while the columns rise after it, so a fit that is rewarded for ordering cells can reach 0.90 while leaving the order of times inside a cell untouched. If that is the reason, the culprit is the overlap and not the horizon, and it is testable, because at a three-day horizon on a three-day grid the label tiles the timeline exactly and does not overlap at all. The test was pre-registered before it was coded. The arm keeps the columns, the rows, the windows, the learner and the training gap of the production run and changes one thing: the label becomes an M>=5 event in the next three days instead of the next thirty-four. Two memoryless redraws were carried through the same rebuild, and because a floor is only a floor if it is the same catalogue, the rebuild of each redraw was required to reproduce the thirty-four-day labels that the earlier round had saved, element for element, nan placement included; all three passed. The primary was the product question and it is null. Adding the three-day arm to the four-member stack moves it from 0.907265 to 0.907409, which is +0.0001 per window, while the same addition on the two floors is worth +0.0014 and +0.0017, so the gain is -0.0016 and all four conditions fail. The co-primary is the diagnostic, and it is the first time in this project that the within-cell within-window dimension has cleared its floor. Read against its own three-day label, the arm orders times inside a cell at 0.4860 pooled and 0.4728 per window, against floors of 0.4338 and 0.4340, a gain of +0.0392 with a 95 per cent half-width of 0.0200 and above the floor in 74 per cent of the windows; all four conditions hold. There is an obvious objection and it was checked rather than argued. Real events cluster into the same three-day window, so the real catalogue has fewer positive rows than a scrambled one, 4,129 against 5,430 and 5,237, and therefore fewer cell-and-window groups qualify: 2,326 against 3,126 and 3,021. A different set of groups could move the number by itself. Restricting all three to the 768 groups that are usable in every one of them removes that difference by construction, and the result gets stronger rather than weaker: 0.5411 against 0.4632 and 0.4660, a gain of +0.0505 per window, still 74 per cent of the windows, still all four conditions. On that common support the real arm is above one half, which no arm on the thirty-four-day label has managed. The restriction selects busy groups, since a group must contain an event even after the times are scrambled, and that is said here rather than left for a reader to work out. The cleanest part of the result needs no floor at all. There is one set of scores, and it can be read against two labels. Against the three-day label that does not overlap it sits about +0.05 above its floor. Against the thirty-four-day label that does overlap, the same scores read 0.4164, which is where that floor is. The arm did not change between those two numbers; only the label did. So the correction is this. Saying that the thirty-four-day horizon has no within-cell contrast was saying more than the measurements supported. The ordering is there in the data the model already sees, and the thirty-four-day label as it is currently laid on the grid does not reward it. That also sharpens what remains open, because the primary was null: the ordering exists but does not survive being carried into the thirty-four-day product as one more column. The next question is therefore not whether it is there but how to carry it, and the three candidates are being written down before any of them is run: composing three-day hazards forward across the thirty-four-day window in a form that is available at forecast time, intervening on the loss rather than on the columns so that training uses a decomposition that does not overlap while scoring stays at thirty-four days, and handling the overlap explicitly in the scoring statistic itself. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 89 (2026-08-23)

**2026-08-23 update 89 (the first of the three ways of carrying the within-cell ordering into the thirty-four day product is null, and a second round then closes the axis its null clause pointed at while replicating the ordering itself on an independent set of windows).** Update 88 ended by naming three candidates for carrying the ordering and by promising that any of them would be written down before it was run. The first was: compose the sharp hazard forward across the horizon. The thirty-four day window is tiled exactly by eleven sub-windows, ten of three days and one of four, whose logical OR is the thirty-four day label; that identity was asserted element for element on all three catalogues rather than assumed. One learner was trained, not eleven, with each training row given one sub-window index drawn once from a fixed seed and offered as a twelfth column, and at scoring time the learner was evaluated eleven times on each row, giving an arm equal to one minus the product of one minus each sub-window hazard. Every input is available at the time of the row. The primary was the same product question as before: adding this arm to the four-member stack is worth -0.0001 per window while the same addition on two memoryless floors is worth +0.0012 and +0.0013, so all four conditions fail. The co-primary was the within-cell ordering of the composed arm against the thirty-four day label, which is the thing the composition was supposed to fix, and it reads 0.4302 against floors of 0.5011 and 0.5022, inside the floor in every reading including the restriction to the groups usable in all three. The composed arm also scores below climatology, 0.900379 against the background arm at 0.902117, which says what went wrong: composing eleven hazards forward when ten of the eleven are close to climatology dilutes the part that varies with time instead of extending it. One alternative explanation was checked rather than argued. Sharing a single learner across the eleven indices could have blunted it, so the dedicated three day arm of the previous round and the shared learner were compared on identical labels, asserted equal: the dedicated one reads 0.9101 per window against 0.9066, a difference of +0.0035 with a half-width of 0.0010, positive in 92 per cent of the windows. The blunting is real and it is small, and it is not why the round failed. A reading with no verdict was taken alongside: the within-cell ordering at each sub-window index, over the last six windows, runs 0.482 at the first index and 0.456 at the eleventh, and the paired difference between them is +0.026 with a 95 per cent half-width of 0.038, which is not established at that number of windows. A correction belongs here too. This write-up has more than once compared a within-cell figure against one half. That is wrong: the floor for that statistic is not one half and is not even the same from arm to arm. Measured on this project, it runs from 0.4164 to 0.5022 depending on the arm and the label, so the only sound comparison is against a floor built from the same arm the same way, and every within-cell number quoted from here on is read that way. That null clause had been written down before the round ran, and it said the next axis is the number of events per cell per window. Acting on it naively would have repeated work already done, because an earlier round measured the product against the driver threshold and found nothing: pooled 0.9009 driving on M>=2.5 against 0.9012 driving on M>=4.5. What had never been measured is whether density moves the within-cell figure, which is a different quantity from the pooled one and is the one that is actually in question. So the next round asked that, pre-registered as usual. The premise was measured before it was planned around: the catalogue on disk holds 2,801,602 events at M>=1.0, and the production driver threshold of 4.5 keeps 25,701 of them, so that threshold is a choice and not a limit of the data. The denser stream is not complete before 1993 -- yearly counts of 3,597, 4,568, 4,403, 4,311 and 6,096 for 1988 to 1992, then 9,139 or more from 1993 -- so both arms were started in 1993, which costs windows, twenty-eight instead of thirty-eight, and is what makes the comparison like for like. From 1993 the two streams hold 473,009 events at M>=2.5 and 16,163 at M>=4.5, a factor of twenty-nine. Everything else was held to the previous rounds: same grid, same columns, same learner, same annual windows, same five-year run-in, the training gap kept at thirty-four days, and the label an M>=5 event in the next three days, which tiles the timeline and does not overlap. Because the label is a function of the M>=5 events alone and both streams contain all of them, the two arms must have identical labels, and that was asserted element for element over 2,724,028 rows rather than assumed. The floors redraw the times of the M>=2.5 set once per seed, and the sparse floor is the subset of that same redraw above 4.5, so the two floors inside a seed also share labels. A first attempt at those floors was discarded before it produced any number: it drew from the whole catalogue rather than from 1993 onward and so built floor worlds fourteen per cent denser than the real one, which is a fatal asymmetry in a round about density. The fix was to restrict before the redraw, and the guard against it happening again is that each build now records its driver count and its M>=5 count and the run refuses to proceed unless all three worlds agree; they do, at 473,009 and 16,163 drivers and 5,400 M>=5 events. The answer is two answers. The co-primary clears for both arms: the dense arm orders times inside a cell +0.0348 above its own floor with a 95 per cent half-width of 0.0253 and positive in 64 per cent of the windows, the sparse arm +0.0363 with a half-width of 0.0199, and all four conditions hold in both cases and again on the 647 groups usable in all six walks. The sparse arm is the previous round re-run on a different set of windows with the blocks and the model rebuilt, so that result is now replicated: +0.0363 here against +0.0392 there. The primary is null. Twenty-nine times the events is worth -0.0015 per window with a half-width of 0.0159, positive in 43 per cent of the windows; on the common groups it is +0.0061 with a half-width of 0.0263 and three of the four conditions fail; and under the most adverse pairing of the floors it reads -0.0282 and -0.0420. The pooled figure moves the wrong way as well, 0.911339 for the dense arm against 0.912796 for the sparse one, which is what the earlier threshold test had already said. All of it was recomputed by a second implementation written before the numbers existed, which rebuilds the windows from the arrays, asserts that every window leaves at least thirty-four days between the last training row and the first scored row, rebuilds the three day labels straight from the catalogue and finds them equal to the walk in all 2,273,803 rows for both arms, and counts concordant pairs by walking tie groups instead of calling a ranking routine; all twelve verdict quantities agree. So the position is this, in the words fixed before the numbers. The ordering of times inside a cell is real and it has now been seen twice on independent window sets. Its size does not depend on how many events the driver stream carries, so counting more of the same events is not the axis, and neither is a denser catalogue of the same measurement, which includes the published template-matching catalogues. The axis named in advance for this outcome is a different observable read on the within-cell dimension rather than more of this one, and the candidates written down before the result were the continuous-waveform energy the catalogue discards, geodetic strain, and the slow-earthquake channels; each has been read against the pooled product before and was null there, and none has been read on the dimension where the signal actually is. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 90 (2026-08-23)

**2026-08-23 update 90 (the first of the three different observables is read on the within-cell dimension and is null: the timing of deep tectonic tremor does not order the times of earthquakes inside the cells where tremor is measured, and the learner turns out not to lean on it at all).** Update 89 ended by naming three observables that had never been read on the dimension where the signal actually is, and by promising that whichever was taken would be written down before it was run. This round took the one already on disk, open and needing no registration: the deep tectonic tremor catalogue of the Nankai trough, 356,230 events from 2004-04 to 2023-12. Tremor is not the earthquake catalogue read again; it is deep slow slip on the plate interface, detected in continuous waveforms, and no column in this project had ever seen it. The premise was measured before the pre-registration was written rather than assumed. Tremor is not national: on the one degree grid it falls in 28 cells and only 13 of them hold at least a thousand events, which is two per cent of the grid, so whatever this round found would be a statement about those thirteen cells and not about Japan, and that was written down in advance along with the list of cells. Inside them, over twenty years, the catalogue holds 52 events at M>=5, which cannot support an annual within-cell reading at the product threshold. The target was therefore moved, first to M>=4.0 in the pre-registration and then to M>=3.5 in an amendment written before any arm was coded and before anything was run, on a power argument taken from the labels alone with no model involved: of the 247 cell-and-window groups, 151 hold a positive at M>=4.0 with 304 positive rows in all, about two per usable group, against 216 groups and 915 rows at M>=3.5. Both thresholds are reported. The two arms were the eleven production columns, and those eleven plus four tremor columns, all functions of the past alone: the counts in the cell over the last three, thirty and ninety days, and the days since the cell last had tremor. The floors redrew the tremor times uniformly over the block span with each event keeping its cell, which asks whether the timing of tremor carries information rather than whether the places tremor occurs are special; the seismic columns and the labels were untouched and asserted identical element for element. The primary is inside its floor and not marginally. Adding the tremor columns is worth -0.0052 per window in the within-cell figure while the same addition on the two memoryless floors is worth +0.0130 and +0.0181; the paired quantity is -0.0208 with a half-width of 0.0184 and is positive in 22 per cent of the windows, so all four conditions fail. The co-primary, the same comparison on pooled AUC, reads -0.0062 against floors of +0.0083 and +0.0068. The shape is the one an earlier round already showed: real tremor columns make the arm slightly worse and memoryless ones make it slightly better, which is what a fitted combiner does with a fifth column whose error is uncorrelated with the rest. The reading with no verdict, fixed in the pre-registration before the round ran, is the one that says what actually happened. Shuffling each tremor column in turn on the test rows moves the arm by at most 0.0024, and the largest single move, +0.0024 on the ninety day count, is an improvement, meaning the arm would be very slightly better without it. The learner was not ignoring a strong signal; these four summaries carry almost nothing it can use. That is a statement about this channel and this reading, not about slow slip, and it leaves open the possibility that a different summary of the same catalogue would do better. A by-product of the round deserves its own line, because it measures something this log has previously only argued. Confined to those thirteen cells the seismic arm scores a pooled AUC of 0.6078 at a three day horizon, where the same eleven columns read in the 0.90s across the national grid. Most of the national figure is geography, and here is the number that shows it rather than a claim that it is so. One structural note: restricting to the groups usable in every world is a no-op in this round, because only the tremor times are redrawn, so all four worlds carry identical labels and the usability test is a function of the labels alone. In the previous round, where the driver stream itself differed between worlds, the same restriction did work. The M>=4.0 reading, carried with no verdict as promised, gives +0.0225 against floors of +0.0386 and +0.0100 and is positive in 39 per cent of the windows, which is exactly the thin behaviour the power argument predicted at 288 positive rows. The result was recomputed independently before it was written up. The four tremor columns were rebuilt from the source csv by a different implementation, cell by cell, agreeing to 2.4e-07; the M>=3.5 labels were rebuilt straight from the catalogue and agree in all 31,252 rows; the eighteen windows were rebuilt from the step grid and agree with the walk; the thirty-four day gap between the last training row and the first scored row was asserted for every window; and the pooled AUC and the within-cell figure were recomputed with an implementation that walks tie groups instead of calling a ranking routine. All of the verdict quantities agree with what the walk printed. So the position is this, in the words fixed before the numbers. The ordering of times inside a cell is real and has been seen twice on independent window sets. It does not come from the number of events in the driver stream, so a denser catalogue of the same measurement is not the axis, and the first of the three different observables does not supply it either. The branch written down in advance for this outcome names the second candidate: geodetic strain, the daily positions the Nevada Geodetic Laboratory publishes without registration, which unlike tremor reaches the whole grid rather than two per cent of it, and after that the continuous waveform energy the catalogue discards, which needs compute this machine does not have and will go elsewhere. Nothing here says otherwise about the target. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 91 (2026-08-23)

**2026-08-23 update 91 (the second of the three different observables is read on the within-cell dimension; the first run of it passed the pre-registered rule, an adversarial audit found two places where the code did not match the pre-registration, and with those corrected the round is null).** Update 90 ended by naming geodetic strain as the next candidate, on the ground that unlike deep tremor it is not confined to one subduction segment. The channel is the daily positions the Nevada Geodetic Laboratory publishes without registration. Before anything was written down the coverage was counted rather than assumed: of the 1,754 of its stations in the Japan box, 803 are covered continuously from 1998-01-01 to 2024-12-31, and requiring at least three of them within 100 km of a cell centre leaves 110 cells. That is a third of the 335 cells that hold an M>=5 event at some point, against the two per cent of the grid that tremor reached, and the covered cells are thick, a median of seventeen stations and a maximum of seventy-six. The station set was frozen for the whole span on purpose, because letting cells enter and leave as stations are installed would make coverage a function of time and time is what the round reads. This is not the June GNSS test run again. That test was a pooled reading and it was null, and it stands; the reading never taken is the within-cell one, and the previous round measured directly why the two can differ, the same seismic columns scoring 0.9128 across the grid and 0.6078 confined to thirteen cells. The target was fixed by a rule written before any arm existed -- the highest threshold leaving at least 80 per cent of the cell-and-window groups usable and at least four positive rows per usable group -- which picks M>=3.5 uniquely from the counted table, with the other three thresholds reported and given no verdict. The two arms were the eleven production columns, and those eleven plus six geodetic columns, all functions of data strictly before the row: the median and the largest thirty-day velocity anomaly against a three-year reference velocity fitted with annual and semiannual terms, a robust normalisation of it, the number of stations exceeding three times their own scatter, the days since that last happened, and the second invariant of the strain rate tensor from a planar fit of the velocity field, which is the quantity the June work found removes the wide translation of the Tohoku postseismic field. The floors shifted the whole station-by-day array circularly in time, the same offset for every station, four of them, which keeps each station own time structure and the spatial coherence of the network and destroys only the alignment between geodesy and the catalogue. What happened next is the part of this entry that matters more than the channel.
