The first run passed the pre-registered rule: the within-cell figure gained +0.0032 per window against floors of -0.0043, -0.0024, -0.0022 and -0.0003, the paired quantity was +0.0055 with a 95 per cent half-width of 0.0033, positive in 73 per cent of the twenty-two windows, and all four conditions held. Rather than write that up, the result was handed to an adversarial review with instructions to break it, and the review found two places where the code did not do what the pre-registration said: the planar fit carried a ridge of 1e-6 where the amendment fixed 1e-8, and the rule that a cell column is undefined when fewer than three of its stations have a defined value had been applied to one of the six columns instead of all six. Neither departure was chosen to help, and both helped. With the code corrected to the contract and all five worlds rebuilt, the primary gains +0.0015 against floors of -0.0041, -0.0010, -0.0001 and -0.0047; conditions (a), (b) and (c) hold and condition (d) fails, the paired quantity being +0.0040 with a half-width of 0.0042. The reading that counts is the one that matches the pre-registration, so this round is null. Both sets of numbers are recorded here, and the pre-correction outputs and code are kept rather than discarded. The co-primary, pooled AUC, is inside its floor in both versions, which is what the June result already said. The no-verdict thresholds are all inside their floors. Three diagnostics were run after the correction, none of them deciding anything. Restricting to the 1,502 cell-and-window groups with no M>=6 within 100 km in the prior year or the window itself leaves a paired quantity of +0.0014 against a half-width of 0.0058, so what little remains is consistent with the geodetic columns re-encoding a large earthquake that has already happened, which the seismic columns describe differently. The per-cell decomposition does not concentrate: the largest single cell holds under half of the pooled difference and dropping the top one, two or three cells leaves the paired quantity at +0.0037, +0.0036 and +0.0039. The permutation reading carries a lesson of its own. Taken on the pooled figure, as the previous round took it, every column moves the arm by at most 0.0003 in both versions, which would have said the learner never looked at geodesy. Taken on the statistic actually under test, the pre-correction run had the strain invariant worth -0.0027 of within-cell ordering while moving the pooled figure by -0.0003 -- exactly the shape this round was built to detect -- and after the correction no column is worth more than -0.0013. A permutation must be read on the statistic the verdict is about. The result was recomputed independently, by code written before the reading was run: the labels rebuilt straight from the catalogue agree in all 321,310 rows, the columns rebuilt by direct least squares agree to 1.4e-04, the twenty-two windows were rebuilt from the step grid, the thirty-four day gap was asserted in every one, and the two verdict statistics recomputed without a ranking routine agree to six digits. The data itself was checked against the outside world rather than against itself, after a first packing of it was found to be reading the wrong column of the source files -- a mistake the first check missed because that check reused the same wrong column indices. The packed array reproduces the raw text to 0.0001 mm, gives a median horizontal rate of 16.7 mm per year over 2001 to 2009, and shows a median eastward step of 2,586 mm across 2011-03-11 at the stations on the Miyagi coast. So the position is this. Of the three observables named in advance as candidates to carry the within-cell ordering, two have now been read and neither carries it, and the branch fixed before this round ran names the third: the continuous waveform energy the catalogue discards, which this machine cannot compute and which will go to a public runner, read on the same 110 cells and the same twenty-two windows so that all three channels stand on one comparable set. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 92 (2026-08-25)

**2026-08-25 update 92 (geodesy is read a second time, in a five-minute kinematic frame instead of a daily one, and it is null on the same dimension the daily solutions were null on).** Update 91 read the daily GNSS solutions on the within-cell dimension and, once an audit had brought the code back to what the pre-registration actually said, found nothing. The obvious objection was the frame: a daily position is a smoothed thing, and whatever a fault does in the days before an earthquake might live below that. So the same 110 cells and the same twenty-two windows were read again with five-minute kinematic solutions, against the same seismic arm and against the daily geodetic arm, with four floor worlds built the same way as before. The primary was the within-cell-and-fold ordering of the kinematic arm minus the seismic arm: the real world gives +0.0199 and the four floors give +0.0214, +0.0147, +0.0148 and +0.0105, so the real world is below the best floor and the gain is -0.0016. Paired across windows it is +0.0045 against a 95% half-width of 0.0211 and it clears the floor in 64% of the twenty-two windows, which satisfies one of the four clauses and fails three. The co-primary, the kinematic arm minus the daily geodetic arm, gives +0.0041 against floors that are all negative, so its gain of +0.0072 is the largest number in the round, and it too fails the rule: paired +0.0119 against a half-width of 0.0270, above the floor in 55% of windows. Restricting everything to the 497 cell-and-window groups that are usable in every world moves neither figure. Two readings that carry no verdict are recorded next to them: the pooled figure moves by -0.0006 and the Brier skill by -0.0003. What makes this a statement about the channel rather than about the apparatus is a reading taken in the round that followed, where the same eleven seismic columns are broken one at a time and the arm loses up to 0.0129, while breaking the kinematic columns costs at most 0.0017. The instrument answers when there is something to answer to. Two of the three observables named in advance have now been read on this dimension and neither carries it. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 93 (2026-08-25)

**2026-08-25 update 93 (three different ways of carrying the short-horizon ordering into the thirty-four day product, and all three are null).** Once the within-cell ordering had been shown to exist at short horizons and to vanish at thirty-four days, the question stopped being whether it is there and became how to carry it. Three carriers were pre-registered and run in turn. The first changed the objective rather than the columns: the per-cell median and interquartile range were removed from all eleven columns, taken from the training rows alone, so that the learner would be asked about a cell's own deviation instead of its level. On the M>=3.5 target the primary is -0.0084 against floors of +0.0017, -0.0038, -0.0030 and -0.0009, and no clause of the rule holds. Two things came out of it that were worth more than the verdict. The seismic channel's own within-cell ordering was measured with a floor in the production harness for the first time, at 0.5200 against floors of 0.5017 to 0.5028, which puts the earlier finding on the operational rows. And a sign flip ran monotonically across the magnitude thresholds, -0.0084 then -0.0001 then +0.0170 then +0.0498, which the pre-registration had already ruled carries no verdict, so it is written down and not claimed. The second carrier tested exactly that: the same intervention on the 557 cells the first round never read, with the largest threshold promoted to the judged one. It gives +0.0075 against a floor at +0.0106 and fails every clause, and the ladder reproduces in sign but is a seventh of the size, so the +0.0498 is recorded as one of four targets rather than a result. An audit of that round found the independent check had been silently comparing a magnitude against the string "3.5" and verifying nothing; it was fixed and the round rerun before anything was reported. The third carrier ran the three-day arm's own score into the thirty-four day product as a twelfth column. It gives -0.0008 on the pooled figure against floors of +0.0022, +0.0016, +0.0019 and -0.0022, and -0.0084 on the within-cell statistic, and fails everywhere. One correction was written into the pre-registration while that run was going, before its numbers existed: the real column has no finite value anywhere in the first window's training rows, while the floors do because rolling gives them one, so windows are now taken only where the real column is finite, which drops one of twenty-two. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 94 (2026-08-25)

**2026-08-25 update 94 (a round that finally passed the rule, an audit that showed the floor it passed was the wrong one, and the two rounds built to settle it, both null).** The ladder of label horizons was run next: the same rows, the same columns, the same floors, with only the length of the label changed. Three days gives -0.0017 and fails everything, seven days +0.0044 and passes two clauses, fourteen days +0.0041 and passes all four, thirty-four days -0.0008 and fails everything. The instrument's detection limit had been calibrated in the same round with a synthetic column made from the label plus noise, so the fourteen-day result was not being read against nothing. Raising the floors from four worlds to twenty-four kept it: first of twenty-five, p = 0.04. Then the audit found the confound, and it was a real one. The floor rolls the derived column along the step axis, which breaks its alignment with the labels and its alignment with the other eleven columns at the same time, so it cannot separate genuine timing from the plain benefit of handing a learner a column that lines up with its own rows. The floor it named instead is a twin: retrain the three-day arm with only its training labels rolled, so the twin occupies the same rows with the same non-finite pattern and the same per-cell level, and differs from the real column in nothing but its correspondence with when things happened. Two rounds were pre-registered to settle it. The first repeated the reading on the 557 cells that had never been read, where the real world gives +0.000178 against a best floor of +0.001620, ranks seventh of twenty-five at p = 0.28, and fails all four clauses; the floor spread there is 0.001071 against 0.002691 on the smaller set, so the more precise conditions give something very close to zero rather than something smaller but still positive. The second ran the twins. The real world gives +0.004104 against the best twin's +0.006373, ranks third of twenty-five at p = 0.12, and fails all four clauses; at thirty-four days it ranks twenty-fourth. Dropping the four twins whose circular distance was under two years leaves it second of twenty-one. What the twins bought is the number worth keeping: their centre sits at +0.000933 while the rolled floors' centre sits at -0.001652, so +0.002585 is the measured size of the alignment benefit that the wrong floor had been crediting to the world. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 95 (2026-08-25)

**2026-08-25 update 95 (two more null carriers, and the arithmetic that explains why all of them were null).** The fourth carrier strips the geography off. Each row is replaced by its rank against that cell's own earlier scored rows, strictly backward, same cell only, undefined until the cell has a hundred and twenty of them, and no label is touched anywhere in the construction. It is null at all four horizons. What the promised readings did to that null is the point of the round. The column keeps its content: pooled within cells it is 0.5430 against a twin average of 0.4946, with all twenty-four twins below it. The learner does use it: it is third of twelve by splits, and taking it away costs 0.002849 with a standard deviation of 0.000588 across draws. So the column is real and the arm reads it, and it still cannot move the judged figure, and the reason turned out to be arithmetic rather than luck. Of the positive-negative pairs that make the thirty-four day pooled figure, the ones that sit in the same cell are 0.7294%, so ordering every one of them perfectly is worth +0.003835, which is the same size as the spread of the floors themselves. Three consecutive carrier nulls stop being three unlucky rounds and become one number. The fifth intervention went at the other side and gave the arm a quieter estimate of each cell's background rate. It is null too: +0.000867 with all nineteen floors positive, thirteenth of twenty, p = 0.65. The readings again say more than the verdict. As a standalone score the column lifts 0.8579 to 0.8939 and does it in twenty-one windows out of twenty-one; the learner puts it second of twelve by splits and loses 0.007997 with a standard deviation of 0.000219 when it is permuted; and adding it to the arm still buys +0.0009, because the other eleven columns supply what it supplies. That difference is worth naming: a permutation measures what a column is worth inside a model that is already fitted, and a rebuilt arm measures what it is worth after the rest have been allowed to rearrange themselves around it, and the gap between the two is redundancy. The floor design was also checked rather than assumed: left to pick their own weight, the floor worlds matched the real world in none of three hundred and ninety-nine window-worlds. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 96 (2026-08-25)

**2026-08-25 update 96 (the frame is shortened with the horizon held still for the first time, and the gain this write-up has been attributing to the frame since update 73 turns out to belong to the horizon).** Shortening the frame is one of only three levers this project has ever measured as positive, and it was carried into the production form and reported here. But every rung of that ladder moved the label horizon down together with the feature frame, and the base rate moved by a factor of a hundred and eleven across it, so nothing on it could be read as a statement about the thirty-four day product this work is judged on. This round holds the horizon at thirty-four days and moves the frame alone. The base rate then comes out identical at three days, one day and half a day, 1.319e-02 in all three, which is the whole point: the easy-negative objection that killed the half-degree cell cannot arise. Every arm is scored on the same rows with the same labels, and the coarse grid is asserted to be a subgrid of both finer ones. The intervention is numerically large: at the shared instants the steepest of the Omori columns moves by 43% of its own spread, and the columns rank by how steep their kernel is, exactly as the physics says they should. The answer is +0.000071, which against nineteen floor worlds ranks eleventh of twenty at p = 0.550, dead in the middle. The floor is worth describing because it is exact: each driving event's time is re-drawn inside the three-day bin it already occupies, which leaves the three-day block bit-identical, maximum absolute difference zero over the whole block, while 83.3% of events change their half-day bin. So a fine frame carrying no sub-three-day information buys a spread of 0.0004, and the real world is inside it. The consequence for the earlier reports is direct: the rise from 0.8995 to 0.9207, and the within-cell rise that went with it, came from the horizon and not from the frame, and the box harness that first measured the lever moves its window and its label together in the same way. A secondary did pass all four clauses, the Brier skill, +0.003193 with 62% of windows positive, and it was not promoted. Recalibrating the coarse arm on the window before, a monotone re-map that cannot change any ranking and adds no information, buys +0.003601, more than the frame does; and with both arms recalibrated the same way the frame's remainder is +0.001138 with half the windows positive. An audit of this round found three places where asserts the pre-registration promised did not execute as written, including one that sat inside the branch of an arm that had been deferred; they were rerun at full density afterwards and passed. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 97 (2026-08-25)

**2026-08-25 update 97 (the pairs that make the judged figure are counted, and the arm turns out to sit exactly on a per-cell rate built from every window except the one it is scoring).** The null in update 96 came with a set of fit-free measurements that say more about where this product can move than the round itself does. Every positive-negative pair that makes the pooled figure falls into exactly one of three classes. Pairs in the same cell at different times are 0.1373% of them, so ordering all of them perfectly is worth +0.000646. Pairs at the same instant in different cells, which is what a forecast that ranks cells right now addresses, are 0.0390%, worth +0.000039. Everything else, pairs that differ in both, is 99.8237% and worth +0.099833. Set against the climatology arm inside each class, the arm is +0.0466 better in the same-cell class, +0.0052 in the same-instant class and +0.0055 in the rest, so the dynamic skill is real and is concentrated where the metric weighs it at one part in seven hundred. Then the level itself was priced. A per-cell rate from earlier windows alone gives 0.8579; one from every window except the one being scored gives 0.9002; the arm gives 0.8995; one read off the scored window itself gives 0.9723; and a static rate over all windows gives 0.9230, which is higher than the two-sided one only because it contains a twenty-first of the answer, so it is not a backward number and is not treated as one. Splitting the oracle's advantage the way this project has learned to split it, +0.0721 is knowing the window being scored and cannot be had, and +0.0423 is estimation and can. Window by window the arm and the two-sided level cannot be told apart, -0.00253 with a third of the windows positive against a half-width of 0.00543, while the arm beats the backward level by +0.05048 in 86% of them. The arm, updating every three days from the past only, has already reached what a rate averaged over twenty other years reaches. Two things follow and are written down as constraints rather than conclusions. Any new observable read on the within-cell dimension is bounded at +0.00065 on this product, whatever it is. And an observable has to improve the level, not the timing, to matter here. The first candidate for that was priced before it was built and did not survive: the long-term strain rate is nearly uncorrelated with the cells' own rate, a Spearman of 0.088, and reaches 0.6636 on its own, so it is carrying something different, yet as a prior for the shrinkage it buys +0.0005 and as a blend +0.0004, because the cells with a thin record are 27.1% of the rows and 4.9% of the positives. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 98 (2026-08-25)

**2026-08-25 update 98 (making the readout coarser does what making the features finer did not, a first floor that no causal transform could ever have passed, and the same reading against a floor drawn from the past).** If a finer feature frame does nothing for a thirty-four day label, the mirror question is whether a coarser readout does something. The features are a three-day-scale quantity and the label asks about thirty-four days, so a score that wobbles inside a window faster than the label can justify is carrying noise into the ordering. The transform is as plain as it sounds: replace each row's score by the mean of that cell's last K scored steps inside the same window, the current step included, never reaching across a window boundary and never reading a step later than the row. K is not swept; for each window it is the candidate that did best over the earlier windows alone, so nothing about the choice sees the answer. It is worth +0.001405 per window over the nineteen judged windows, positive in 89% of them, and all four clauses of the rule hold; pooled it moves 0.8995 to 0.9007 and the Brier skill +0.0593 to +0.0635. The first floor was built wrong and the way it was wrong is worth recording. It averaged the same number of scores for each row but drew them from anywhere in the cell and window, so it could read steps later than the row it was scoring, and it reached +0.009519 and put the real world last of twenty. That is not a conservative floor, it is one no causal transform can pass, and the round's own figures show why: a causal mean over every past step of the window is worth +0.0008, while the non-causal mean over the whole window is worth +0.0114, so almost all of the floor's advantage is knowing what had not happened yet. A floor that cannot be passed is a ceiling wearing the wrong name. The floor was redrawn from what the row could actually have seen: the current step kept, and the other steps drawn at random from the earlier steps of the same cell and window, so the count is the same, the current step is present in both, and the only thing that differs is whether the rest are the most recent ones. Against that the floors give +0.000623 with a spread of 0.000120 and a best of +0.000812, and the real world is first of twenty at p = 0.050. Floors redrawn independently, by different code and different seeds, give +0.000619 and the same rank. Recency is doing the work: the same number of scores taken at random from the past is worth +0.0006 and taken from the recent past is worth +0.0014, and a second floor that does not keep the current step turns the sign negative. The control behaved as the pre-registration said it should: the same transform on the climatology arm, which is nearly constant inside a cell and window, is worth -0.000031 and is positive in 42% of windows. Three things are said about the size of this rather than left for a reader to find. It is +0.0014, and the non-causal ceiling of +0.0114 stays out of reach and stays printed beside it. It was measured on the twenty-six year, single-arm setting, and whether it survives into the forty-three year combined configuration that the published forecasts use is a separate run that has not been done. And it trades the within-cell ordering away, 0.4826 to 0.4672, which is affordable only because the arithmetic in update 97 caps what that ordering was worth at +0.00065. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 99 (2026-08-26)

**2026-08-26 update 99 (the coarser readout is carried onto the configuration the published forecasts actually use, where it is worth nothing, and the reason turns out to be that there was nothing there to win).** The previous entry measured the trailing-mean readout on a twenty-six year, single-arm setting and said in as many words that whether it survives into the forty-three year combined configuration was a separate run that had not been done. This is that run. The four members were walked again from the raw catalogue by the round 122 scripts, unedited, which took three and a half hours, and they came back at 0.904294, 0.906047 and 0.907265 pooled on 3,004,835 rows with 38,839 positives across thirty-seven one-year windows. Those are the same six digits as the run that first produced them, so a round that rebuilds the arms is also a replication of the round that built them, and it is worth saying that a replication happened even though it was not the point of the round. The transform is unchanged: each row takes the mean of its cell and window last K scored steps, the current one included, K chosen for each window from the earlier windows alone. On the production arm it is worth -0.000050 per window over the thirty-five judged windows, positive in 63% of them, and three of the four clauses of the rule fail. Against the nineteen causal floors the real world is still first of twenty at p = 0.050, so the finding that recency is doing something survives the move; what does not survive is any gain. The reason is not that the backward choice of K picked badly. Sweeping K with hindsight, which is a ceiling and is labelled as one, is worth +0.000063 at its best and turns negative from K = 8 onward. There was no gain available to select. Set beside the earlier runs the pattern is monotone in the strength of the arm being read: 0.8995 gave +0.001405, 0.9043 gave +0.000618, 0.9060 gave -0.000055 and 0.9073 gave -0.000050. A readout intervention that is not reported next to the AUC of the arm it reads cannot be interpreted, and that is a lesson about how these entries should be written as much as about this transform. The non-causal ceiling is unchanged and still large: giving every row the mean of its whole cell and window reaches 0.917593 pooled, +0.010299 per window, and splitting that into what is self-knowledge and what is estimation is the next thing to do. The independent re-implementation agreed exactly: the same K for every window, the same gain to six digits, smoothed scores differing by 3e-08, and the same rank against both floor sets. One technical trap is worth publishing because it would be invisible in any result it spoiled. Prefixing a zero to a float32 array with the shorthand form does not promote the array to double precision on this version of the library, so a running-sum helper silently accumulates in single precision and its reconstruction error is 1e-06 rather than 1e-15. On a column with many equal values that is enough to reorder rows and move an AUC, and it would look exactly like an effect. The runs reported here fed the helper double precision and were unaffected. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 100 (2026-08-26)

**2026-08-26 update 100 (the ceiling is split, the arm turns out to beat every static per-cell rate that does not read the year it is scoring, and a claim made three entries ago has to be taken back).** Update 97 reported that the arm sat exactly on a per-cell rate built from every window except the one it was scoring, and could not be told apart from it window by window. That was measured on twenty-six years and the hundred and ten cells a continuous GNSS network covers, and an earlier entry had already shown that subset is not a random one. Redone on the production rows, forty-three years, six hundred and sixty-seven cells, thirty-seven windows, no model refitted, the ordering reverses. Six scores were built on the same 3,004,835 rows, each of them constant inside a cell and window except the arm: the rate over everything resolved before the window opens, 0.894703; the same thing given the future as well but with the scoring window and a thirty-four day gap removed on both sides, 0.902278; the arm, 0.907265; the whole-record rate ignoring which window a row is in, 0.912327; the arm averaged over its own cell and window, 0.917593; and the scoring window own rate, 0.972369. The arm beats the two-sided rate by +0.003333 per window with all four clauses of the rule holding and 70% of windows positive, and it beats the causal rate by +0.011614 with 92% positive. Two defects were found before the run and written down before any number existed. The two-sided estimator had a gap on the past side and none on the future side, so the first eleven steps after each window shared earthquakes with the window last labels; the direction of that bias was stated in advance, and correcting it moved the figure the way it was said it would. And the last twelve steps of the catalogue have no labels at all, and both scripts had been turning them into twelve steps in which nothing happened anywhere in Japan. Now the part that has to be taken back. The difference between the two-sided rate and the backward one was called estimation precision, meaning something a purely backward estimator could in principle reach with a better estimate of the same static quantity. That is wrong here, and the way to see it is to measure the slope. Doubling the backward span is worth +0.010679 and then +0.003058; the two-sided estimator has 1.42 times as many steps as the backward one, which on that slope predicts +0.000441, and the measured difference is +0.007575, seventeen times more. The advantage is not that there are more steps but that the extra steps sit immediately after the window being scored, and that is no more reachable backward than the window own labels are. The ceiling reported in the previous entry also turns out not to be the top of its own family: the scoring window own rate beats it by +0.055580 in every one of the thirty-seven windows, so 0.917593 is the ceiling of smoothing the arm, not the ceiling of knowing which cells are dangerous this year. The instrument checks behaved: every score that is constant inside a cell and window comes out at exactly 0.500000 when scored inside a cell and window, and shrinking the backward rate toward the base rate is a structural no-op because the number of steps is constant within a window, so it cannot change any within-window ordering. The independent re-implementation, which finds each window place on the time axis by matching its labels rather than by re-deriving the rule, agreed on every printed figure to eight decimals. Two pieces of sizing decide what comes next. At this granularity a score constant inside a cell and window can order 99.9967% of all the pairs the pooled figure is made of, so unlike the within-cell readings of earlier entries this family is not boxed in by the geometry. And the year to year movement of a cell rate is not in the catalogue: taking one window worth of steps and sliding it away from the scoring window gives 0.720594 for the year after, 0.718932 for the year before, and 0.684963 for sixteen years before, so the persistence is real and monotone, but there is no asymmetry between past and future and none of it is anywhere near the 0.894703 that the whole backward span reaches. Knowing the future year is worth no more than knowing the past one. What remains is an observable that is not the catalogue, read as a level for each cell and year, and it has to cover the grid: the openly served station metadata for Japan lists fifty-three permanent instruments, and they fall in thirty-four of the six hundred and sixty-seven cells. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 101 (2026-08-26)

**2026-08-26 update 101 (an observable that is not the catalogue is read as a level for each cell and window, the reading is null, the level underneath it is real, and an audit finds that three quarters of the stations had been put in the wrong cells).** The previous entry ended by saying that what remains is an observable that is not the catalogue, read as a level for each cell and year, and that it has to cover the grid. This entry does that with the daily GNSS solutions. For each station a straight line is fitted to a five year baseline that ends before the window opens, at least eight hundred solutions, and the transient is the size of the mean residual of the horizontal components over the trailing year, at least thirty solutions, in millimetres. The value for a cell is the mean over the stations that fall inside it, and it is undefined where no station falls. That level is then blended into the arm score and the blend is judged the same way every round in this project is judged. It is null: the blend beats the arm by -0.000149 and all four clauses of the rule fail. Two things say the null is not the level being empty. Against nineteen causal floors, each of them the same construction with the year scrambled, the real one ranks first of twenty: the geodesy of the right year orders earthquakes better than the geodesy of a wrong year. And on the rows it reaches the level scores 0.600306 on its own, against 0.828241 for the arm and 0.813187 for the backward per-cell rate, while its correlation with that backward rate is 0.1445 and its correlation with the rate of the window actually being scored is 0.2647. It is closer to what happens than to what has already happened, so it is not a restatement of the catalogue. It is also not a failure to choose the blending weight. Sweeping that weight with the answer in view, every positive value is negative, from -0.000214 at 0.05 to -0.006850 at 0.40. There is no addition to be had in this readout at any setting, which is a stronger statement than the null itself. The coverage is the other half of the picture: twenty-nine of the thirty-seven windows can be judged, the level reaches 12.8% of the scoring rows and 28.5% of the earthquakes, in a hundred and thirty-six cells, and the ceiling on the rows it does reach, +0.023028, is untouched. The audit is the body of this entry, and it found four defects, one of which would have carried an eight decimal agreement into the record. The label grid rounds: a cell is a centre plus or minus half a degree. The stations were being binned by truncation instead, so only 399 of the 1,659 stations, 24.1%, landed in the cell their coordinates belong to. The same misunderstanding had been written into the independent re-implementation, so the two agreed to eight decimals and both were wrong. An independent implementation catches a transcription error between two scripts; it does not catch a misconception the author is holding while writing both of them. The rule that follows is to read the code that generates a convention rather than to restate the convention from memory. Second, the vertical component was never sliced per station, so every cell was reading the first station in the file. Third, and found without being prompted, widening the search box added no stations, because the station list itself had been cut from an older and narrower box: a filter can only widen to the width of the list it filters. Rebuilt from the full holdings file of 23,606 stations, the Japan list went from 1,625 to 1,659. Fourth, a download call with no timeout hung for fifteen minutes on one station, which was confirmed by watching the temporary file stop growing rather than by guessing, and is now forty-five seconds with a per-station cache so that a run can resume. The independent check, redone against the corrected convention with its own grid mapping, its own line fit, its own ranking and its own seed, agreed on every printed figure. What the null says is that the level is real and the way it was carried is not the way. The next entry makes it a twelfth feature column and refits the model on it. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 102 (2026-08-26)

**2026-08-26 update 102 (the level is carried the other way, as a twelfth feature column with the model refitted, the model uses it, the arm gets worse, and three statements that had been treated as one turn out to be three).** The previous entry said the level is real and the way it was carried is not the way, so this entry carries it the other way. The transient is handed to the learner as a twelfth feature column and every window is refitted on it. It is null, and the arm is worse: the pooled figure falls from 0.904294 to 0.902970, the per window difference is -0.001866, all four clauses of the rule fail, and only 31% of windows are positive. On the four member stack the same change is worth +0.000019, which is nothing. Restricted to the rows where the column has a value at all, the difference is -0.007002, and the Brier skill of the single arm falls from 0.0729 to 0.0703. The floors were not run, and that was written into the pre-registration before the run rather than decided afterwards: a floor can turn a passing round into a null, but it cannot turn a failing one into a pass, so when all four clauses fail the floor cannot change the verdict. That is the arithmetic of a conjunction rather than a shortcut, and it is worth saying because a different kind of floor does turn out to be needed, which is what the next entry is about. The body of this round is the permutation test. The learner does use the geodetic column: shuffling it costs +0.002613, ninth of the twelve columns, against +0.153086 for the first and +0.064302 for the second. So the column carries something, the model reaches for it, and the arm still gets worse. Those are three separate statements and this round is the case where the first two hold and the third reverses. A weak column that has no value on 87% of the rows does not sit quietly beside the strong ones; it takes splits away from them. Everything that used to be said as one sentence about a feature being useful has to be said as three from here on. A byproduct worth keeping is that the members were scored on their own for the first time: the background member reaches 0.902117, the magnitude four and a half member 0.906291, and the magnitude five and a half member 0.898324, which gives every later round a gate to check itself against. The audit found one defect that would have killed a correct round. The independent check was comparing a float64 recomputation of the column against the column as it is stored, which is float32, with an absolute tolerance of one part in a million. The column reaches 2546.512 millimetres, where the spacing of float32 is 6.1e-5, sixty-one times that tolerance, and on a frozen seed 424 of the sampled steps differed by more than 32 millimetres for that reason alone. The mathematical agreement between the two implementations was 1.8e-12. When the contract says a quantity is stored in single precision, the tolerance that checks it has to be relative, not absolute. One discipline was broken and is recorded as broken: the verifier is supposed to be frozen before the walk starts, and this one was written while the walk was running. No figure existed yet, but the rule exists precisely so that the sentence beginning no figure existed yet never has to be written. One operational accident is also recorded: the disk watchdog deleted a catalogue archive while the round was running, and because the core module reads that file at import time every script broke at once. The file was refetched and the rule now stands aside while a round is in progress. Three ways of carrying geodesy remain. Replace a column instead of adding one, since three of the existing columns rank alongside the geodetic one in the permutation test and an exchange does not create a contest for splits. Reduce the missingness by interpolating a field into the cells that have no station. Or move geodesy to the calibration side instead of the feature side. Before choosing among them the instrument has to be calibrated, because -0.001866 was read against zero and what an uninformative twelfth column costs this pipeline has never once been measured. That measurement is the next entry. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 103 (2026-08-27)

**2026-08-27 update 103 (the instrument is calibrated, the previous entry's reading is withdrawn, an uninformative column with the same shape costs almost exactly the same, and the one family that costs nothing names what comes next).** The previous entry read -0.001866 against zero and concluded that the arm gets worse when geodesy is added. What an uninformative twelfth column costs this pipeline had never been measured, so that comparison had no scale on it. This entry supplies the scale. Twenty walks were run, identical in every respect except the twelfth column: the labels, the time axis, the grid, the window rule, the learner, its settings and its seed, the first eleven columns and the combining rule are the ones the previous entry used, and the primary is read over the same twenty-nine windows. One walk reproduces the previous entry. Nine destroy the geography of the column and keep everything else, which is to say each cell keeps its own series, each step keeps its distribution across cells, and the pattern of which cells and steps have no value at all is untouched. Nine destroy the values and their persistence and keep the real pattern of missing values in its real places and times. One is dense, with a value everywhere, carrying nothing. The direction of the bias was written down before any figure existed: every one of these floors can only make the instrument look worse than it is, so a floor at zero would leave the previous reading standing, and a floor near -0.0019 would mean the reading has to be rewritten. The floor is near -0.0019. The nine geography-destroyed floors centre on -0.00133298 with a standard deviation of 0.00060514 and run from -0.00207264 to -0.00042780, and the real column, at -0.00186592, is the third most negative of the ten, with two floors below it. It is inside the band. Against the weaker family, the nine value-destroyed floors, which centre on -0.00067807, the real column is below all nine, and on the rows where it has a value at all the same ordering holds, -0.00700218 against a family centre of -0.00280764. The dense floor is +0.00016384 with a ninety-five percent half width of 0.000246, which is to say indistinguishable from zero. Those four numbers decompose the cost. The pattern of missing values on its own is worth -0.000842. Carrying real persistence and real value structure on top of that pattern is worth a further -0.000655. A dense column carrying nothing is free. What is left over, -0.000533, sits inside the standard deviation of the geography-destroyed family and cannot be assigned to anything. So the reading of the previous entry is withdrawn. The mechanism it described, that a weak column with no value on 87% of the rows takes splits away from the strong ones, is confirmed as a mechanism, but the part that attributed the loss to geodesy is not supported: a column with the same shape and no information whatsoever costs the same within the resolution available. A byproduct is worth recording because it points the other way. Across all twenty walks and every window, the only place where the walked scores come out bit for bit identical to the eleven column arm is the first walked window, the one with the smallest training set, and it happens for eleven of the eighteen floors, meaning the learner never split on the floor column there at all. It never happens for the real column in any of the twenty-nine windows, and never for the dense one. That is an independent corroboration of the permutation result in the previous entry, and it also biases the comparison: a floor that is ignored contributes exactly zero, which pulls that floor's mean toward zero and makes the real column look more negative by comparison. The verdict of inside survived a bias pointed against it. The checker stopped with an assertion failure before producing any of this, and what it stopped on was not a fault in the instrument. The checker had been written to assert that a window which was walked must differ from the arm, and that is an expectation about how the machinery behaves, not a term of the contract. The frozen pre-registration defines the primary as the difference in area under the curve over every one of the twenty-nine windows, so a window that comes out identical contributes exactly zero and is counted. The main script had always done that; the checker alone had been dropping those windows, which would have averaged some of the floors over twenty-eight windows instead of twenty-nine. The direction of that repair was set by the frozen documents and not by the answer. The pre-registration, the main script and the walk outputs were not touched, the checker's frozen copy was kept beside the repaired one, and the guard that was removed was replaced by the guarantee the pre-registration does make, that the fallback rule fires on exactly the windows the previous round fell back on, read out of the walk logs. Re-run, the independent implementation agrees with the main script to six decimals on all twenty walks. The rule that follows is that an assertion in a checker may encode what the contract promises and may not encode what the author expects the machine to do, because an expectation written there stops a round that ran correctly. What comes next is named by the family that costs nothing. Of the three ways of carrying geodesy left open two entries ago, replacing a column and moving geodesy to the calibration side both leave the charge in place, and only removing the missingness removes it, since a dense column with no information at all is free. The bar for the next attempt is set at the same time and is not zero: a dense twelfth column has to beat +0.000164 to mean anything. The way to make it dense is not to interpolate a scalar, because at two hundred kilometres the hundred and ninety-eight cells that newly acquire a value have stations surrounding them in only twelve cases and are one sided extrapolations in the other hundred and eighty-six, the network being on land and the earthquakes being at sea. The way is to project what the network would do for a source placed in each cell, which is defined for all six hundred and sixty-seven cells by construction. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 104 (2026-08-29)

**2026-08-29 update 104 (the geodetic field is read again with its direction kept and every cell covered, the reading is null, a control that was supposed to hold the result down turns out to have been silently doing nothing, and two ways of reshaping the column are killed in fifteen minutes without running either).** The previous entries read a geodetic transient as a level for each cell and window, then as a twelfth feature column, and found nothing both times. The remaining objection was that both readings threw away direction and covered only the cells that happen to have stations, so this one keeps the direction and covers all six hundred and sixty-seven cells by projecting, for every cell, the response the network would show if a source sat there. Ten walks were run: the real one and nine that destroy only the geography of the column. The primary, the arm with the projected field minus the arm without it, is minus 0.001099 over twenty-nine windows, standard deviation 0.006044, positive in fifty-two percent of them, and all four pre-registered conditions fail. The floor of nine geography-destroyed walks centres on minus 0.00163 with a standard deviation of 0.00073, and the real value ranks fifth of ten inside that band, so neither the sign test nor the floor test is passed. The three gates that prove the rows and labels never moved reproduce to six decimals, and the verification script, frozen before the walks began, agrees with all ten of them to six decimals. The column itself is not empty: the learner ranks it ninth of twelve by permutation and the within-cell figure moves from 0.427078 to 0.440808. It simply does not make the arm better. The part of this round worth keeping is a defect in its own controls. One pre-registered control was meant to repeat the primary on cells with no recent large earthquake nearby, so that a result could not be explained by the settling ground after a mainshock. It reported a hundred percent of cells passing that filter, which is impossible, and the reason is that the catalogue's time axis and the window's time axis have different origins whose ranges do not overlap at all, so the filter matched nothing in every one of the thirty-seven windows. The verdict is null either way and does not move, but a round that had come out positive would have carried that control as evidence while it was doing nothing. Corrected, the filter keeps ninety-one percent of the record and forty-seven percent of the positives, and on that subset the sign of the effect reverses -- which is recorded as a hypothesis to test, not as a result, because choosing a subset after seeing the answer is not evidence. Two candidate reshapings of the column were then retired without being run. Ranking the values inside each time step turns out to preserve the within-step order exactly, so it changes only the scale between steps, and the reshaped column correlates with the original at 0.932; the pre-registration's stated reason for expecting it to matter, that a few epochs dominate the variance, is not what the finished column shows and could not be reproduced from the build log. Subtracting a fitted post-seismic decay explains at most nine percent of the variance and leaves a residual correlating with the original at 0.955. Neither can move a result that sits inside a band of width 0.0007, so neither was run, and two thirteen-hour walks were saved by fifteen minutes of arithmetic. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 105 (2026-08-29)

**2026-08-29 update 105 (an upper bound is proved on what any within-cell ordering can be worth to the pooled score, the horizon turns out not to change the scoring geometry at all, and a waveform fetch that was about to be started is called off before a single request is made).** The plan named in the previous entry was to build a short-horizon product and price it against its own climatology. Thirty-one seconds of arithmetic, with nothing fitted and nothing fetched, closed that plan and replaced it with something firmer. First the geometry. The share of the pairs that make the pooled score which sit inside one cell-and-window group is the sum over groups of positives times negatives, divided by the totals, and since no group is larger than a hundred and twenty-two rows that share cannot exceed a hundred and twenty-two divided by the negative count, whatever the labels are. At the production positive count that is 0.004113 percent, so a perfect ordering inside cells is worth at most plus 0.0000206 of pooled area-under-curve; sharing a cell across windows is bounded at 0.151888 percent, worth at most plus 0.00075. The bound was checked against the two real labellings, which reach 0.003292 and 0.003937 percent, and against five random labellings drawn at the same rate, which reach 98.87 percent of their own bound and stay inside it. Second the horizon. Moving the label from thirty-four days to three lowers the base rate by a factor of nine and a half and removes the overlap entirely, since consecutive rows in a cell no longer share any part of their label window, and yet every level in the climatology ladder moves by less than 0.002: the backward per-cell rate goes 0.894703 to 0.895061, the two-sided one 0.902278 to 0.902639, the whole-record one 0.912327 to 0.913143 and the oracle 0.972369 to 0.970665, with the arcs between them matching as well. The instrument reproduces the previously published thirty-four-day levels to six decimals before the three-day column is read. Together these say that three separate attempts in earlier rounds to carry a within-cell ordering into the thirty-four-day product failed for an arithmetic reason rather than because the carrier was chosen badly: their ceiling was two parts in a hundred thousand while the floors they were measured against had standard deviations a hundred times larger. That is a statement about which metric can see the effect, not about whether the effect exists. Third, the waveform arc. The next observable in the queue was continuous seismic waveform energy read as a level for each cell and window, and a design for it was written and then tested before any data was requested. Reading the actual fetchers corrected two things this project had believed: one request returns every selected station at once, so stations are free and only time segments cost quota, and the continuous archive is retrievable from 2004-04-01 rather than the network's start date, which leaves twenty-one of the thirty-seven windows whole. The design set itself a stopping rule, that a perfect level must beat the window-to-window noise by a factor of two, and on the reachable rows it does: blending an oracle level in at weight one tenth gains 0.01434 per window against a bar of 0.006717. Splitting that gain is what stopped the fetch. Ninety-six percent of it is knowledge of the scored window's own outcome: a level that knows the whole record but not the window is worth 0.00198, a causal backward level 0.00038, and a causal level built from inside the window but strictly before each row is worth minus 0.00139 at weight one tenth and minus 0.10289 at full weight, with not one window positive. Since all of those are catalogue quantities, the conclusion does not depend on the observable, so five hundred and four requests and eight days of quota were not spent. The carried-column variant is worse rather than better: it needs two thousand five hundred and fifty-seven requests, and in every one of the twenty-one readable windows between sixty-seven and ninety-eight percent of the training rows would have no value at all. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 106 (2026-08-29)

**2026-08-29 update 106 (the structural lever named one entry earlier is withdrawn because the same entry's arithmetic refutes it, a per-cell level nobody had measured is measured and lands below the simplest one there is, and the within-cell base a future arm has to beat is put on the record).** The previous entry ended by naming a two-stage arm as the next structural move: one estimator for the level of each cell and window, another for the ordering inside a group, combined by a rule that respects the pair geometry. Its premise was that a single pooled learner does not optimise the between-group ordering. That premise is false, and the entry that named it contains the refutation: since 99.996 percent of the pairs that make the pooled score are between groups, the loss a pooled learner minimises is the between-group problem almost in its entirety. Splitting it in two adds no structure. The second stage is bounded separately at plus 0.0000206, which is five thousandths of the window bar, so it cannot be the reason to build anything. That leaves the first stage, and one candidate for it had never been measured: the arm's own out-of-sample scores averaged over each cell's rows in earlier windows. It is causal, because the windows are a year long and disjoint, and unlike the plain per-cell rate it carries whatever the eleven feature columns saw. It scores 0.892747, below the plain backward per-cell rate at 0.894703 and well below the arm at 0.907265, and blending it into the arm is negative at every weight tried, minus 0.00006 at one tenth and minus 0.02058 at full weight. For contrast the same blend with an oracle level is worth plus 0.02265 at one tenth, so the instrument is demonstrably sensitive and simply finds nothing on the causal side. The shape of the whole ladder is now on the record: every causal quantity measured sits below the arm, and everything above the arm uses information from inside or after the window it is scoring. One correction of method belongs with this. The script that measured the new level carried, written before it was run, the sentence that if the level landed near the plain backward rate then a fitted first stage would have no room either. It did land there, but that inference does not follow: a fixed aggregate failing does not bound a fitted model. The conclusion stands on the pair arithmetic instead, and the pre-registered sentence is withdrawn as written. What may be put in a pre-registration is a proposition the measurement can actually refute. The entry closes by recording the base for the direction that is still open. Within a cell and window, at the three-day label, the eleven production columns and the three saved arms were read on the restricted metric that only compares rows sharing a cell and a window. The saved arm reproduces its previously published figure of 0.411081 exactly, which is the cross-round check. Moving from the thirty-four-day label to the three-day one pushes almost every column toward the middle, the arm from 0.411081 to 0.474325, which is the same overlap effect an earlier round saw in one column now reproduced in all eleven. One column is strongest between cells and most inverted within them, at 0.893488 pooled against 0.382810 within, and two columns sit at exactly one half in both labels because they are constant inside a group, which is the control working. None of that is a verdict, because one half is not the floor for this metric; the floor is a construction-matched twin and had not been built yet. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 107 (2026-08-29)

**2026-08-29 update 107 (the missing floor for the within-cell metric is built out of nineteen worlds that differ from the real one in exactly one thing, eight of the nine informative columns clear it at the three-day label by ten to fifteen standard deviations, the same columns barely clear it at thirty-four days, and the redraw turns out to manufacture forty percent more positives than the real catalogue has).** The previous entry left a table of within-cell figures that could not be judged, because one half is not the floor for that metric. This entry builds the floor. A twin is a world that differs from the real one in exactly one respect: the driver times are redrawn with no memory, uniform over the same span, while the count, the locations and the magnitudes are kept, and both the feature columns and the labels are rebuilt from that redrawn catalogue. Nineteen were built. Which construction to copy mattered more than it looks. An earlier round built its blocks at a one-day frame and read off every third step, which gives the triggering kernels nine hundred lags where the production columns have three hundred, and that round only ever asserted that its labels matched production, never its columns. Copying it would have made the floor differ from the real world in two things instead of one, so the production route was copied instead, and the check is direct: rebuilding with no redraw reproduces the stored production column block exactly, element for element, and the eleven within-cell figures reproduce the previous entry to six decimals. Rows, cells and windows are held fixed throughout; only the world changes. At the three-day label the result is not marginal. Every one of the eight informative columns is the largest of its twenty worlds, with real minus twin means running from plus 0.040393 to plus 0.060373 against twin standard deviations near 0.004. A single-column effect of this kind had been reported twice before, at plus 0.0392 and plus 0.0363 on two independent window sets; it reproduces, and it is not one column but eight. The ninth informative column is inside its band at minus 0.000862, and the two group-constant columns sit at exactly one half in the real world and in all nineteen twins, which is the control behaving. The same columns read at the thirty-four-day label give plus 0.003 to plus 0.015, with only four of them ranking first and two sitting inside their bands. So the overlap that a thirty-four-day label creates between consecutive rows of a cell removes something between four and ten times the effect, and that statement now has a floor under it rather than a comparison to one half. One reading from the previous entry has to be corrected. The column that looked most inverted within cells at the three-day label is not distinguishable from its twins there; its inversion is real only at thirty-four days, where it sits below all nineteen. The by-product is worth recording on its own. The memoryless redraw produces 40.4 percent more positive rows at thirty-four days and 27.6 percent more at three, because real earthquakes cluster in time and several magnitude-five events in one cell collapse into a single positive row when the label window is long. A floor placed at one half would have missed that entirely, along with everything else the construction contributes. The figures above are pooled over windows and are therefore still a base rather than a verdict, since the standing rule is that a judgement is made across windows. What a new arm would have to beat is now a number: plus 0.060373. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 108 (2026-08-29)

**2026-08-29 update 108 (the within-cell effect is read the strict way, one window at a time, and eight of nine columns survive every condition and rank first of twenty at the three-day label while not one of them survives at thirty-four days).** The previous entry's figures were pooled over all thirty-seven windows, and this project's standing rule is that a judgement is made across windows rather than on a pooled number. This entry supplies that reading, and its pre-registration says plainly what it can and cannot establish: the pooled values had already been seen, so no column here is blind and none is selected, all eleven are judged by the same rule and all eleven are reported whatever they do. What this can show is only whether an effect already seen survives a stricter test, not that the effect exists independently. The quantity is, for each window, the real world's within-cell figure minus the mean of the nineteen twins, and a column is called real only if the mean is positive, at least sixty percent of windows are positive, both of those survive deleting any single window, the mean exceeds its own ninety-five percent interval half-width, and the real world ranks first of the twenty worlds. At the three-day label eight of the nine informative columns pass all five, with per-window means from plus 0.028675 to plus 0.045799, positive window fractions from sixty-five to eighty-one percent, and means between 1.9 and 2.4 times the bar. The ninth is null, and the two group-constant columns are null as the pre-registration required, which is the instrument's own falsification check passing. At the thirty-four-day label nothing passes. Three columns still rank first of twenty, but their positive window fractions run from twenty-seven to fifty-nine percent, so none of them clears the sixty-percent condition or the leave-one-window-out condition. The overlap a long label creates between consecutive rows of a cell does not merely shrink the effect, as the previous entry measured; it removes the consistency across windows that a judgement needs. The verification script, written and frozen before the run, recomputes the per-window figures group by group with an independent implementation and the decision rule from the pre-registration's own wording rather than by importing the project's, and agrees with the instrument on all eleven columns to the last digit printed, with a maximum absolute difference of exactly zero. The pre-registration also named in advance the condition that would have made this round worthless -- fewer than eight columns passing, which would have meant the pooled reading was carried by a minority of windows -- and it did not fire. Three qualifications are attached to the result and are not to be dropped when it is quoted. It is not a route to a higher pooled score: the bound proved three entries ago caps any within-cell ordering at plus 0.0000206 of pooled area-under-curve, so this is genuine skill on a different metric, not a change to the published figure of 0.907265. It is not independent evidence, for the reason the pre-registration states. And it is a property of columns that were built for another job; no arm optimised for ordering rows inside a cell at the three-day label exists yet. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 109 (2026-08-29)

**2026-08-29 update 109 (an arm is fitted for the within-cell ordering, it comes out below every one of its nineteen floors, and measuring why finds that the feature columns carry a one-step shadow of each earthquake which the arm learned instead of the signal).** The columns clear their floor at the three-day label, so this entry fits an arm for that job and asks whether it beats the best single column. The learner is a within-group fixed-effects least squares on the nine informative columns, refitted for every scored window on the earlier windows only, solved without regularisation and without an intercept. Centring at scoring time is unnecessary because the metric compares rows only inside a group, so a per-group constant cannot change it, and centring at training time looks only at earlier windows. The whole procedure is repeated inside each of the nineteen twin worlds with that world's own columns, labels and training data, so the floor is drawn from what each world could have known. A design audit found one fatal defect before the run and it was confirmed by measurement rather than argument: the row block begins at the first window, so that window has no earlier window and therefore no training set at all, and scoring it would have been scoring a model fitted on nothing. It is training-only, thirty-six windows are scored, and the column the arm is compared against had its figure recomputed on the same thirty-six. Two further corrections came from the same audit: ninety-one percent of the groups contain no positive at the three-day label and therefore supply no pair to the metric while still steering the fit, so training is restricted to groups that contain one; and the comparison column, being the largest of the eight, carries a winner's curse, which was measured at plus 0.003650 against a selector that picks each window's column from earlier windows only, making the chosen baseline the harder of the two. The result is unambiguous. The arm minus the column is minus 0.121244 per window, positive in three percent of windows, failing all four conditions and ranking twentieth of twenty. The arm against its own floor is minus 0.075212 and also ranks twentieth of twenty, so it does not merely fail to beat a column, it sits below every twin. The comparison column reproduces its published figure on the thirty-six windows and remains real, and the zero-coefficient control is exactly one half in all twenty worlds. The verifier, frozen before the run, refits the arm by an independent route and reproduces all thirty-six coefficient vectors and every per-window figure with a maximum absolute difference of exactly zero. Why it failed was then measured rather than guessed. The twin arms reach 0.620030 on the metric where the real arm reaches 0.544818, so the same fitting procedure gains about three times more in a world with no memory than in the real one. The cause is in the construction: an event at a given time is binned into the feature columns at the row after the one whose three-day label it satisfies. Measured in units of each column's own within-group spread, the positive row sits between plus 0.42 and plus 1.57 above its group mean while the row immediately after it sits between plus 1.88 and plus 9.87. Inside a group, therefore, a large column value mostly says that the previous row was positive, and a learner maximising the association with the label at the same row exploits that with a sign flip. In a memoryless world that shadow is the only structure there is, so the fit takes all of it; in the real world genuine precursory elevation pushes the other way and cancels part of it, which is why the fitted arm loses to its floor. This does not overturn the previous entry, it explains it: the raw columns sit below one half in both worlds because the shadow pushes them there, and the real world sits less far below because something real is pulling back. The method lesson is that a floor is not only a test of an effect but an instrument for finding out what a model learned, and that comparing to one half would have read the arm's 0.545 against the column's 0.481 as an improvement. The next step is to control the shadow before fitting, and the plainest way is to give the model the previous row's label, which covers a window that has already closed by the time the current row is scored and is therefore known to a real forecast. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 110 (2026-08-30)

**2026-08-30 update 110 (the step this log announced last time is measured before it is spent and then withdrawn, because the metric hands any causal recency feature a free ordering tautology worth more than the signal; the arm built on it fails exactly as the pre-registration said it might, and two single features clear the floor).** The previous entry ended by naming the previous row's label as the way to control the one-step shadow, so this round measured that family first, with no columns built and nothing fitted: only each world's three-day label grid, fifty-seven seconds for all twenty worlds. Three causal recency scores were read on the within-cell metric. A content-free marker that says only "a positive has already happened earlier in this group" reaches 0.664555 in the real world and 0.669613 in a memoryless twin, because a group that bears a positive usually bears exactly one and that one is by construction the first, so ranking it above every later row is free in any world. The previous round's fitted arm reached 0.544818, which is below that free gift. Raw within-cell area-under-curve is therefore not a scale on which anything can be read at this label, and nothing in this round is compared to one half. The announced control is withdrawn, in the pre-registration and before the run: the previous row's label carries almost none of that channel, 0.504142 in the twins against 0.669613 for the cumulative marker, because it marks a single row of a tail that runs to the end of the group. The same sizing found something that was not being looked for. The elapsed time since a cell's own most recent earlier positive, read with the physical sign so that a cell which fired recently is the more dangerous one, has a per-window distance from the floor of plus 0.052103, larger than the best production column's plus 0.046031; under midranks the identity that reverses a score also reverses the figure is exact, so that number is the sizing's printed one with its sign flipped and nothing refitted. The round then pre-registered a twelve-feature arm -- the nine informative columns plus the previous row's label, the cumulative marker, and one over one plus the capped elapsed time -- with the learner, the floor, the rows, the windows and the decision rule all identical to the previous round's, and the primary comparison against the harder of the two available bases. A design audit before freezing found two defects that would have failed a valid run rather than a bad one: the cap on the elapsed feature was described as applying only to cells that never fire when the code also caps cells that fired long ago, and one gate could not have passed as written because the sizing log prints thirty-six-window means rather than per-window values and prints them in the reversed signs. Both were corrected before the freeze. The primary is null. The arm minus the base is minus 0.105450 per window, positive in three percent of windows, failing all four conditions and ranking twentieth of twenty; against the column the previous round used it is minus 0.099378 with no positive window at all. The recency-only arm is worse still at minus 0.190157 with zero positive windows and a twin figure of 0.778912, near the ceiling of the free gift. All three fitted arms sit below every single feature, so the problem is the fitting and not the features: a within-group least-squares objective is maximised by the tautology, and the floor then removes what was maximised. The pre-registration named this failure mode in advance and it is not offered afterwards as a discovery. Two single features are real under the full rule including the rank test. The elapsed-time feature is plus 0.052103, positive in eighty-one percent of windows, first of twenty; the previous row's label is plus 0.025556, positive in ninety-seven percent of windows, the most consistent quantity measured here, and also first of twenty. The cumulative marker is null at plus 0.005057, which is the round's sharpest contrast: the feature that carries the most tautology carries the least skill. Four qualifications are attached and are not to be dropped when this is quoted. The elapsed-time feature has not been shown to beat the column: the difference is plus 0.006072, it fails the interval condition and ranks second of twenty, so the honest statement is that it is the largest measured and is not distinguishable from the column. The three real features' per-window values correlate between 0.72 and 0.85, so this is one channel worth about plus 0.05 above its floor, not three. The elapsed-time feature's raw figure is 0.340666, well below one half; its floor is 0.288563, so the distance is the whole statement and the raw number is not a good number. And these quantities were seen in the sizing before the pre-registration was written, which that document states, so this is a stricter re-reading and not independent evidence. The bound also stands: within-cell ordering is capped at plus 0.0000206 of pooled area-under-curve, so the published 0.907265 does not move. Five gates were checked, among them a full replication of the previous round inside this one -- its nine-column arm and all eleven columns reproduce in the real world and in all nineteen twins to six decimals -- and the verifier reproduced the flip identity, the three features rebuilt by a third route, the thirty-six coefficient vectors and the per-window figures with a maximum absolute difference of exactly zero in every case. One honesty item belongs in the record. The frozen verifier crashed on cells that have never fired, of which there are 332 of 667, because it indexed an empty array. That was not a disagreement with the instrument but the verifier failing to implement its own frozen contract, which says the elapsed feature takes the cap for such a cell, so the direction of the repair was fixed by the frozen text and not by any result: the frozen copy was kept beside it, one line was repaired, and the instrument, the pre-registration and the output were not touched. The within-cell fitting line is closed on measurement rather than on discouragement -- one channel, a fitting procedure that always walks into the tautology, and a pooled ceiling of two parts in a hundred thousand -- and the object this round newly produced, the days since a cell's own last magnitude-five event, is a causal channel that is not among the eleven production columns and is now taken to the side where the pooled budget actually lies, to be sized without fitting before any walk is spent on it. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 111 (2026-08-30)

**2026-08-30 update 111 (seven sizing rounds ask whether anything is being left on the table by the inputs, the loss or the combination rule, and the answer measured in every one of the six directions tried is no; the arc costs seconds and minutes instead of walks, and the one operational finding pays for the rest of it inside the same session).** The previous entry left a channel that is real inside a cell -- the elapsed time since a cell's own last magnitude-five event -- and a bound saying within-cell ordering cannot move the pooled figure, so the first thing done here was to take that channel to the thirty-four day side where the pooled budget actually is. It is not a re-encoding of what the arm already has: its rank correlation with the eleven columns runs from 0.210 to 0.551, and the thing it resembles most is not a column at all but the causal per-cell climatology at 0.572, which says its pooled content is mostly which cell rather than when. On its own it reaches 0.771106 against the climatology's 0.895128 and the arm's 0.907265, and blending it into the arm has an answer-seeing ceiling of plus 0.000235 at a weight of two percent, an order of magnitude below the per-window interval half-width, so no walk was spent. One trap in that measurement is recorded because it was nearly walked into: collapsing the same quantity to a constant per cell and window scores 0.962643, above every causal reference and near the oracle, but a window mean includes rows later in the window and a cell that fires during a window necessarily has a small elapsed time, so that number is close to the label itself and is not a lever. Attention then moved from the inputs to the learner, because the production member minimises log-loss while the score is pooled area-under-curve, a pairwise ranking criterion whose pairs are 99.9967 percent between groups, and at a base rate of 1.3e-2 log-loss is dominated by calibrating the negative mass. Balancing the class weights is worse in all three probe windows. More iterations are monotonically worse: fifteen hundred is below three hundred in every window, and so are deeper trees, which retires the whole family of doing the same thing harder and says the shipped iteration count sits near its own optimum. Downsampling the negatives five to one lands inside its own seed noise for accuracy -- the effect and the noise are the same size, measured both ways -- but fits nine to ten times faster, and that is the finding that paid for everything after it. A guess that the arm ignores neighbouring cells was retired by reading the code rather than by testing it: the fields are already convolved with isotropic Gaussians at four spatial scales. What no isotropic kernel of any scale can carry is direction, and the local epicentre clouds have a median axis ratio of 1.514 with the strike varying by 43.4 degrees of circular standard deviation across cells, so an elliptical field aligned to each cell's causally estimated strike is a change to how the field is built rather than another column. Rebuilding the isotropic field by the same path reproduces the stored column exactly, at a rank correlation of 1.000000 and a maximum absolute difference of 1.9e-9, which is what makes the elliptical number comparable; the ellipse is worth plus 0.003907 as a single column and correlates 0.9948 with the column it would replace, and replacing it across all thirty-six scored windows, paired on the same rows and the same negative draw, gives plus 0.000130 against an interval half-width of 0.000549. Ranking each field across the 667 cells of its own instant, so that the arm sees which cell is most dangerous today rather than how strong this cell is, improves all eight fields as single columns, by between plus 0.0004 and plus 0.0135, and still makes the arm worse: minus 0.000932 when the fields are replaced and minus 0.000510 when both versions are given. Finally the combination itself, which had never been fitted, was fitted causally on earlier windows only, and it loses to the shipped fixed rule by 0.000444 per window while equal weights lose by 0.000617, so the rule is not leaving anything either. The member figures reproduce an earlier round's to the last digit, which is the cross-round check passing. What all of this reproduces, for the fourth time and now under the strongest condition yet, is a single mechanism: a column can be real, be used by the learner, and be better on its own, and the eleven-column arm will still absorb it. The direction not yet touched is not what the arm is given but which data it is fitted on -- every earlier row currently counts the same, although the catalogue is not stationary and an earlier round has already shown that moving the training start twenty years earlier mattered -- so time-weighted training rows and an ensemble over training spans are next, and both fit the same paired thirty-six-window apparatus. None of these are judgements: there is no pre-registration and no twin floor behind any of them, and anything that passes will be frozen and judged properly before it is claimed. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 112 (2026-09-02)
