**2026-09-02 update 112 (which rows the members are fitted on is measured for the first time: down-weighting the older rows is negative and monotone in the time constant, an ensemble over training spans is indistinguishable from an ensemble over negative draws, and what survives is the averaging itself, which is carried onto a production member and judged REAL).** Everything measured up to here changed the inputs, the loss or the combination; this entry changes which rows the fit sees. Weighting the training rows by exp(-(t_window - t_row)/tau) is negative at every constant tried and monotone in it, -0.003027 at five years, -0.001700 at ten, -0.000828 at twenty and -0.000165 at the nearly flat forty-year control, so the old rows are not stale. An ensemble over training spans is also null: its +0.000932 is matched by a floor of three fits that differ only in which negatives were drawn, at +0.000768, leaving +0.000164 inside its own bar. The first run of that comparison returned the floor and the span arm agreeing to six digits, which was not a bug but an arithmetic fact -- in the early windows there are not fifteen years of training rows to cut, so the span never removed a single row -- and the fix is to print how many rows each cut actually removed, per window, before reading any difference. What remains is the averaging: five bagged fits at +0.001196 with all four conditions, three at +0.000767, two at +0.000383, and the single full-negative fit indistinguishable from one bagged draw at +0.000001, so the five-draw average beats the full fit by +0.001195 while costing a sixth of its time. The rank average has to be a midrank average; ordering by argsort splits ties by position and moves the AUC, which is not academic here because one scored window holds 31,905 tied rows out of 80,707. Carried onto the production reference member under a pre-registration frozen before the walk, five bagged draws beat the published member by +0.001062 with 68% of windows positive and all four conditions met, while a single draw gives +0.000154 and fails the threshold written in advance, so the gain belongs to the averaging and not to the downsampling. The gate for that walk was run before it: refitting one window without bagging reproduced the published file bit for bit. Bagging all four members leaves the published stack unmoved at +0.000229, and the mechanism is measured rather than assumed -- averaging five draws inside the combination step is worth +0.000121, indistinguishable from zero, because combining already reduces variance and the two operations are the same operation done twice. The four members are Spearman 0.9347 to 0.9844 with each other, and even a combiner allowed to see the scored window's own answer beats the published fixed rule by only +0.001035, so the limit is the members rather than their weights. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 113 (2026-09-02)

**2026-09-02 update 113 (a fifth and a sixth member are built and both leave the stack where it was, so the limit moves from the members to the combiner; then a ranking loss is found that gains three times more than anything else this year, is carried to production, and reverses -- and taking the reversal apart shows the gain exists only at a base rate the production fit never sees).** Changing the learner family lowers the correlation with the published members far more cheaply than building a finer grid would have: the same eleven columns fitted by a logistic model correlate 0.87 with the production gradient boosting on the sizing windows, and extra trees are stronger by +0.001996. Adding that fifth member to the stack is null at -0.000027, and a sixth makes it worse at -0.000176, while a combiner allowed to see the scored window's own answer gains +0.000448 and +0.000851 -- the members carry something the causal combiner cannot spend, and the gap widens with every column added. Six standard repairs to the combiner -- scaling the columns, three strengths of regularisation, shrinking toward the fixed rule, fitting on the last ten windows only -- are all null, and the one that helped slightly at four columns reverses sign at six. A screening statistic used to pick that fifth member was not the statistic the bar is made of, which is written down here because it nearly justified a much larger build. The next lever had been named a year of rounds earlier and never run: the metric is an area under a curve, which is a rule about pairs, yet every member has been fitted against log-loss. With the library held fixed as a control, the ranking objective gains +0.003303 with 83% of windows positive and all four conditions met, while merely switching library loses 0.000807, and the gain survives three independent seeds. The design reason given for it -- that grouping rows by scored window makes the objective the judged quantity -- is then refuted by its own floor: a world that keeps the loss and the group sizes but destroys which rows share a group gains the same amount, so the credit belongs to the loss. Carried to production under a pre-registration frozen with its verification before the walk, it reverses: -0.003362 with 16% of windows positive and every condition failing, and the independent recomputation agrees to the last digit. The hypothesis written before the walk -- that windows would be full of groups with no positives -- was simply counted and was wrong; the real difference is the base rate, 0.1667 on the sizing device against 0.0124 in production, and the argument for the intervention was itself an argument about low base rates that had never been tested at one. Rather than sweep the failed round for a pass, the promise is measured directly: the gain is +0.003119 at one positive in six, +0.000441 at one in sixteen, -0.004199 at one in forty-one and -0.007037 with every negative, and separately it is worth about +0.004 more when the query blocks hold 6667 rows than when they hold 667, which is a size effect and not a membership effect. An undocumented external limit surfaced only by running it: the library refuses any query holding more than ten thousand rows. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 114 (2026-09-02)

**2026-09-02 update 114 (the positives are split into the ones a larger earlier shock already claimed and the ones it did not, and the arm turns out to be an aftershock predictor: 0.963 on the claimed positives, 0.737 on the ones that arrive after a year of local quiet, where its margin over the causal climatology no longer clears its own bar).** Every number this log has reported pooled all positives together, and 39% of the positive rows are driven by a shock that Gardner and Knopoff's (1974) windows assign to a larger predecessor. Before splitting anything, the labels are rebuilt from the driving shocks themselves and matched against the labels the walks were scored on, with zero mismatches, so the event-to-row mapping is the one that made the target. Restricting the positives and leaving all 2,965,996 negatives in place, the arm scores 0.9632 on the aftershock-driven positives, 0.8716 on the ones with no larger parent, and 0.7372 on the 11% that arrive with no shock above magnitude four within fifty kilometres in the preceding year. Its margin over the causal per-cell climatology is +0.0183 on the first, +0.0068 on the second and +0.0098 on the third, where the last fails the width test, and eight different definitions of "no local warning" all order the scores the same way. On those unheralded positives, flattening the arm to a constant per cell and window gains +0.0365 across every window measured: its own time axis is a liability there. Two ways to sharpen the static per-cell rate without new data both fail -- estimating it from the far more numerous smaller shocks moves nothing, and only a half-cell spatial smoothing helps at +0.0125, which the arm already contains. A reading published in the previous entry is withdrawn here: the level that includes the scored window's own labels is not an estimate of anything reachable, so the honest bound is the two-sided level, +0.0250 over the causal one rather than +0.0640. Geodesy is then re-read on this subset rather than on the pooled metric that three earlier nulls were judged against. The first pass returned 0.49 in nine configurations and was nearly written down, until checking the instrument found stations fitted through coseismic steps returning three metres per year and cells with five stations on one side returning an extrapolation rather than a gradient. With a speed cap and a requirement that the stations actually surround the cell, the secular strain rate beats the arm on the unheralded positives inside the cells where it is defined, 0.580 against 0.536, and correlates with the long-run rate at 0.345 instead of 0.164 -- but it is defined on 18% of them, so blending it in moves nothing. The saved geodetic columns from the earlier rounds are read on the same subset: the transient one scores 0.600 on all positives and 0.507 on the unheralded ones, which is what a post-seismic detector does. Seafloor geodesy is priced before any of it is requested: replacing the arm by the two-sided level inside the cells a network can surround is worth -0.0010 for the land network, +0.0099 for a hypothetical 117 offshore sites and +0.0148 for 436, and the two real arrays hold about forty between them, so nothing is fetched. An elastic inversion for slip deficit on the Slab2 interface, which needs no surrounding stations in principle, is null and its own controls say why: a run using another window's velocities scores the same, and a column made from the geometry alone with no measurement in it scores higher. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 115 (2026-09-02)

**2026-09-02 update 115 (counting the pairs behind the unheralded positives retracts a reading from the previous entry, moves the target to a cleaner definition of mainshock, and then a change of form -- factoring the label into "is this row positive" times "given that, is its driver a mainshock" -- clears its floor on a production member, on the published stack, and under three different declustering conventions).** The four classes of pair reconstruct the pooled area exactly, and they say the arm's weakness inside the shallow interface cells is between cells rather than inside them: 0.0395% of pairs share a cell, 3.4009% are interface cells against each other at 0.4055 concordance, and perfecting the first is worth +0.000244. The reading that those cells are ordered backwards because locked segments are quiet is withdrawn here, because it is manufactured by the label: concordance there is 0.7469 over all positives, 0.6381 over the ones with no larger parent and 0.4055 only once a year of local quiet is required, so a rate-based score is handicapped by construction. The target therefore moves to the shocks no larger predecessor claims -- 61% of the positives, no quiescence condition -- where the arm beats the causal climatology by +0.006804 with all four conditions met, while the two-sided level does not beat the arm at all. Training the members against that label instead of the pooled one is null: calling the aftershock-driven positives zero costs 0.0094, dropping them gains +0.0004 against a bar of 0.0026, and both damage the published target. The mechanism proposed before running that was checked and refuted -- the eleven columns separate the two kinds of positive row at 0.82 -- and measuring what they actually do finds every column running both ways at once: 0.79 to 0.89 for positive against negative, 0.18 to 0.32 for mainshock against aftershock among the positives, and the arm itself 0.907 one way and 0.196 the other. One monotone score cannot serve both, which is why fitting it against the product label trades one for the other; so the product is not fitted as a product. Scoring by log p1 + alpha log p2, with p2 a separate model of the second factor fitted on positives only, gains +0.001165 on the sizing device under an exponent chosen from earlier windows alone, against nine floors that permute which positive is which and return -0.000008. Carried to the production reference member under a pre-registration frozen with its verification before the walk, it gains +0.003326 with 86% of windows positive, all four conditions and floor rank one of ten, and costs -0.005582 of pooled area, which is stated every time the gain is. Carried into the published stack -- one column of its design matrix, the combiner left as it is and refitted only because that column moved -- it gains +0.000768 with floor rank one of six, while the pooled cost disappears at +0.000146 and fails its own width test, so the combiner reweights the members and absorbs the price. The baseline for that had to be earned: rebuilding the published stack from scratch first missed it by 7.4e-2, because the window list that scores the folds drops a window the stack keeps in its history, and only after reading the code that wrote it did the rebuild match to zero in every scored window. Finally the definition itself is tested rather than assumed, with the reading fixed in writing beforehand: swapping Gardner and Knopoff's windows for Uhrhammer's and for Gruenthal's changes how many shocks count as mainshocks by nineteen percent, and the gain survives both at +0.000759 and +0.000703, all four conditions and floor rank one of six each. A gate written against a printed six-digit number rather than the stored value could not be met as worded and was re-run against the stored table, where it agrees to twelve digits. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 116 (2026-09-03)

**2026-09-03 update 116 (changing what the combiner is trained against is not the lever, but moving the mainshock readout onto every member column is, and then raising the magnitude threshold shows that the design cannot resolve the effect at the sizes that matter -- which is the resolution of the instrument, not a ceiling).** The first attempt to retrain the combiner against the mainshock label was voided before its verdict was read, because the gate demanded that a logistic fit over three million rows reproduce a previous run bit for bit, and it does not: the same process run twice agrees exactly, but across runs the reduction order in the underlying linear algebra moves the answer by about 1.7e-08, five digits below the quantity being judged. The round was re-run with a tolerance argued for in advance -- one part in a million, three digits below the judged quantity and two above the observed drift -- and it recorded, honestly, that the per-window prints from the voided run had already been seen, so the repeat was not blind. The verdict was null: the mainshock area moved by +0.000382 with 51% of windows positive, failing three of the four conditions, and by the contract the floor was not consulted. So the combiner's objective is not what limits this. The readout itself is. Carrying the same correction onto all four member columns instead of one raises the mainshock area by +0.002100 with 84% of windows positive and all four conditions met, and it clears a floor of five worlds in which only the assignment of which positives are mainshocks is broken -- those worlds land between -0.000715 and -0.000753, so putting a meaningless second factor into four columns actively hurts, and the real result ranks first of six. The cost is real and is stated with it: the area over all positives falls by -0.003296. That leaves a measured frontier rather than a single number: the published stack sits at 0.871676 on mainshocks and 0.905850 over all positives; correcting one member column moves the first to 0.872445 and leaves the second unchanged within measurement; correcting all four moves the first to 0.874545 and the second down to 0.902699. Which point to occupy is a decision about what the work is for, not a measurement, and the preregistration said so before the numbers were read. Two further questions were asked and answered flatly. Feeding non-catalogue information -- plate interface geometry, geodetic strain rate, the causal climatology -- into the second factor rather than the first, which had never been tried, moves nothing: eleven catalogue columns already separate mainshocks from aftershocks at 0.830485, and all four additions came back negative. Raising the driving threshold shows where the real limit sits. The areas themselves hold up at every size, between 0.85 and 0.88 for the climatology, the published stack and the two-sided level alike, but the arm's lift over the climatology only survives the four conditions at magnitude five. At 5.5 and above it does not, and the reason is counting: the isolated driving shocks fall from 3,778 to 1,496 to 619 to 233 to 86, and the number of judgeable windows from 37 to 26. At the sizes that matter most, this scoring design cannot resolve a difference of one to three parts in a thousand. That is a statement about the instrument, not about the earth, and the fix it names is arithmetic: more events. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 117 (2026-09-03)

**2026-09-03 update 117 (the two replication boxes named last time do not supply the missing events, the global catalogue does, and in an arena built from it -- where every cell fires and no easy negatives exist -- the mainshock readout clears its floor for the first time outside Japan).** The previous entry named more events as the fix and named two boxes, Alaska and New Zealand, as the source. Forty-seven seconds of fitting-free counting killed that plan before a single byte of dense catalogue was fetched: those two boxes hold 52 and 21 isolated mainshocks above magnitude 6.5 against Japan's 233, so together they improve the standard error by a factor of 1.15 and turn a resolution of three parts in a thousand into 2.6. The whole global catalogue holds 1,993 at that size, 8.6 times Japan alone. Selecting an arena by where large shocks later happened would be the same self-knowledge mistake this log has recorded before, so the cells were chosen from a fixed seed period that ends five years before the first scored row: every one-degree cell that held at least two magnitude-five shocks in the first fifteen years. That gives 2,280 cells, 9,993,240 scored rows, 36 windows, and 1,058 isolated mainshocks above 6.5. It also gives something Japan does not have. Every cell in it fires at least once, so there are no empty-cell easy negatives and no geography to score against; the causal climatology reaches 0.722 on mainshocks there rather than 0.867, and that lower number is the honest one. The column builder was freed from the rectangular grid and checked against the production files it must reproduce, agreeing to one unit in the last place of single precision on the six kernel columns and exactly on the other three. Two findings then arrived that were not expected. The first is that the mechanism this whole line of work rests on -- every column ranks positives above negatives and ranks mainshocks below aftershocks inside the positives -- reproduces in the global arena at almost the same magnitudes as in Japan: 0.69 to 0.76 against 0.22 to 0.30. The second is that the fitted member loses to its own climatology there, by -0.018882 with none of the windows positive, while the identical member in Japan beats it by +0.007665 with all four conditions. That was chased rather than reported: it is not undertraining, since doubling the training rows moves it by 0.0003; it is largely a missing column, since supplying the causal background level recovers 84% of the gap; and it is not the varying cell area, since restricting to the latitudes where a degree means what it means in Japan made it slightly worse. Splitting Japan's advantage settles it. A fitting-free spatial smooth of the climatology delivers +0.005482 there with 83% of windows positive and all four conditions -- 72% of what the fitted member achieves, with no learner at all -- and the same smooth in the global arena hurts, at -0.007649 with no window positive, because those cells were selected to be dense and their rates are already well estimated. So Japan's lift over its climatology is mostly estimation precision, and the global arena is a box in which estimation precision cannot masquerade as skill. The readout does not need a fitted first factor; it needs a base to correct. Judged against the free causal climatology as its base, under a preregistration frozen before the run, it raises the mainshock area by +0.002283 with 81% of windows positive and all four conditions met, against a floor of five worlds that reshuffle which positives are mainshocks and land between -0.0000076 and +0.0000309, ranking the real result first of six. The second factor scores 0.825200 there against 0.821940 in Japan. The cost is larger than in Japan and is stated with the result: -0.018597 over all positives, with no window positive. This is an existence claim inside that arena and nothing more -- magnitude means different things in the two catalogues, the base is a climatology rather than the published stack, and the floor shuffles rows rather than events, which a stricter version would not. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 118 (2026-09-03)

**2026-09-03 update 118 (the floor the previous entry admitted it had not run is run here and the result holds, but a second control -- one that keeps each cell's own mainshock rate and destroys nothing else -- reproduces about half the effect, so half of it is a static geographic map and half is discrimination between events).** The previous entry recorded a mainshock readout clearing its floor in the global arena, and listed among the things it did not promise that its floor shuffled the mainshock flag among positive rows. One driving shock paints a block of about eleven consecutive rows, so that shuffle destroyed the block structure as well as the assignment, and an event-level reshuffle -- which keeps the blocks and moves only which event is a mainshock -- is the stricter test. This entry runs it, over the 78,071 driving shocks of the arena, repainting each world's flag through the same map that painted the real one. The scored set is the real one in every world, exactly as before: what a floor world corrupts is only the label the second factor is trained on. That the new floor is the harder one was measured rather than asserted. Among adjacent positive rows of the same cell, the flag agrees 0.9928 of the time in reality, 0.991 in the event-level worlds, and 0.5249 -- chance -- under the row-level shuffle the previous entry used. The floor values rose accordingly, from a largest of 0.0000309 to a largest of 0.000249, an eightfold increase, and the real effect of +0.00228302 still stands first of six. The gate deserves a note of its own, because an earlier round of this work was voided for demanding that a fit reproduce bit for bit across runs when the machinery in question cannot do that. Here the tolerance was measured before it was written: the same fit-and-score path was run in two separate processes and agreed exactly, over three alpha tables and two hundred thousand probabilities, so an exact gate was legitimate -- and the previous entry's whole result then reproduced at exactly zero difference, on a different day in a fresh process. Two controls were declared in the same preregistration, and they are the reason this round is worth more than a confirmation. The first permutes the flag within narrow magnitude bins, so that the strong grading of mainshock status by magnitude survives intact; it clears, at +0.0000457 against the real +0.00228302, so the effect is not a restatement of how large the coming shock is. The second permutes the flag within each cell, so that every cell keeps its own long-run mainshock rate and only which of that cell's shocks are mainshocks moves. Two of the nine columns are the cell's own coordinates, so a model can learn a static map of which places produce mainshocks, and this project has measured many times that most of its pooled skill is a matter of which cell. That control also clears, at +0.00106667 -- but it reaches a second-factor area of 0.71 where the other floors reach 0.51 and the real one reaches 0.825, and it delivers 47% of the whole effect, with every one of its five worlds using the channel rather than declining it. The honest reading is therefore not that the effect is not a map, but that it is not only a map: about half of it is reproduced by a static per-cell propensity and about half, seventeen standard deviations above that control's mean, is discrimination between individual events. That split was invisible until the control was built, and building it was the result of an audit rather than of the original plan. One claim made during that audit was tested and did not hold: the suggestion that the magnitude-stratified control would retain the part of the per-cell map that magnitude mediates, which would have muddied the reading, fails on measurement -- the correlation between a cell's real mainshock rate and its rate in a floor world is +0.012 for the plain shuffle, -0.026 for the magnitude-stratified one, and exactly 1.000 for the cell-stratified one. One further discipline was fixed in advance rather than after the fact: a control can clear for the empty reason that the causal rule declines to use its channel at all, in which case nothing has been tested, and the preregistration said before the numbers were seen that such a clear would be recorded as uninformative. Two of five worlds declined the channel in each of the first two families and none did in the third, so the cell control is not empty. Independent verification passed all twenty-two of its checks with no correction after freezing, which four earlier rounds of this work cannot say. The limits are unchanged and are stated with the result: five worlds per family is a rank of one in six at best and not a p-value; no world here preserves magnitude and cell at once; the base is a climatology rather than the published forecast; nine columns are used rather than eleven; magnitudes mean different things in the two catalogues, so no effect size is compared with Japan; and the cost over all positives remains -0.018597. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 119 (2026-09-03)

**2026-09-03 update 119 (three corrections to the previous entry first, then the result they set up: the mainshock readout does not merely fade above magnitude five, it reverses, and a floor built for the purpose shows the reversal is real and not an artefact of the instrument).** Three things in the previous entry were wrong or under-stated, and are corrected here rather than edited there, because an entry that is already published should be corrected in the open. First, it said that about half of the effect is "discrimination between individual events". That is more than the run licenses. What the cell-preserving floor shows is only that the effect is not reproduced by a world that keeps each cell's mainshock rate; the residual could be joint structure finer than a cell rate but coarser than an event -- a cell-and-magnitude map, depth, position within the cell -- and the preregistration itself said that no world preserved magnitude and cell at once. Second, it described the real result as standing "seventeen standard deviations" above that floor's mean. The arithmetic is right and the direction conservative, but the standard deviation came from five permutation worlds, and the same entry correctly said that a rank of one in six is not a p-value; quoting a sigma multiple from five draws dresses it as one. No standard-deviation multiple from a five-world floor will be quoted in this log again. Third, its list of limitations dropped three that the contract carried: the floor tests the second factor only and says nothing about the base; the weighting parameter remains pinned at the top of its frozen grid; and the global catalogue's detection completeness is not uniform in space or time, with the arena's seed period being its least complete era. Smaller: the cell floor's block coherence is 0.992, not 0.991; the cell floor does not "destroy nothing else", since it does break the within-cell relation between magnitude and mainshock status and it moves the isolated row count; and a floor is cleared against its maximum, not its mean. Now the result. The readout judged in the previous entries was scored only on mainshocks of magnitude five and above, which is where an earlier round found the instrument ran out of resolution in Japan -- the isolated shocks there fall from 3,778 to 233 as the threshold rises to 6.5, and the judgeable windows from 37 to 26. The global arena was built to buy that resolution back, and it does: all 36 windows remain judgeable at every threshold up to magnitude seven, with 13,047 isolated shocks at 5.5 and 1,652 at 6.5. Before anything was fitted, three measurements were taken and frozen with the contract. The first was the row counts above. The second was a power calculation, which corrected a mistake of mine: my first reading sized only the confidence bar, when the condition that failed in Japan was the fraction of positive windows, and simulating all four conditions gives a chance of passing at magnitude 5.5 of 0.973 if the effect holds and 0.850 if it has shrunk by a quarter, against 0.696 and 0.483 at magnitude 6.0. The threshold to judge at was fixed at 5.5 from that table alone, which is built from row counts and the previous round's own spread and contains nothing about the effect above magnitude five, none of which had been computed. The third was the mechanism, and it was measured rather than reasoned: the fitted second factor ranks large mainshocks BELOW small ones, at 0.416 for magnitude six and above against the other mainshocks, 0.394 at 6.5 and 0.370 at 7.0, with 36 of 36 windows below one half at the first of those. A gate proved this was the same model the previous rounds used, reproducing their second factor exactly. The raw columns point the other way, at 0.54 to 0.60 on the same task, so this is a property of the fit and not of the columns -- and an audit was right that the inference from the columns alone does not follow, which is why it was measured. The verdict at magnitude 5.5 is null: the effect is -0.00063341 with 39% of windows positive, none of the four conditions met, and the real result ranks last of six against its own floor. That is not a null for want of power; the design had a 97% chance of passing had the effect held. The effect by threshold is +0.002283, -0.000633, -0.006253, -0.012057, -0.016377, and the floor decomposes it. A floor whose second factor is trained on a meaningless label sits between +0.00005 and +0.00020 at every threshold, so the instrument has no bias of its own and the negative numbers are real harm. A floor that keeps each cell's mainshock rate gives +0.00107, +0.00078, -0.00170, -0.00443, -0.00627. The difference between the real arm and that floor -- what is left after the static geographic map -- is +0.00121, -0.00141, -0.00455, -0.00763, -0.01011. So the part of the effect that is not a map helps only at magnitude five and actively harms above it, and the map itself turns harmful above magnitude six. What this does and does not license is worth stating plainly, and was written into the contract before the run: the weighting was chosen on the magnitude-five objective and inherited, so the arm judged here is tuned for a different target than the one it is scored on, which biases the round toward a null; a null therefore means this arm does not survive, not that no readout can. The predicted decay did occur, but decay is also what inheriting the weighting would produce on its own, so it was registered in advance that only a non-decay would be informative, and the decay is not cited as confirming the mechanism. Independent verification passed all twenty-three checks with no correction after freezing, for the second round running; among them a check added on an audit's advice, which rebuilt all 180 window-and-threshold effects from the saved scores with its own masks and its own ranking code and agreed to 2.22e-16, and a gate that reproduced the previous round's fifteen floor worlds exactly. The next step is named by the measurement rather than chosen: the second factor is aimed at the wrong target, so the target changes, from "is this a mainshock" to "is this a large mainshock". The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 120 (2026-09-03)

**2026-09-03 update 120 (a correction that matters more than the entry it corrects -- the harm reported last time was mostly a weight left pointing at the wrong target, and once the second factor is aimed at large mainshocks instead of at mainshocks the sign turns over; the floor for that claim is running as this is written).** The previous entry said the mainshock readout "actively harms" above magnitude five, and that its second factor works against the very events that matter most. A control run afterwards shows that reading was too broad, and the correction is the most useful thing in this entry. In the round it described, the weighting between the climatology and the second factor was chosen on the magnitude-five objective and then inherited by every higher threshold; the contract said in advance that this biases the round toward a null and that a null licenses only "this arm does not survive". Re-running the identical old-label arm with that weight chosen on the threshold actually being scored gives +0.00037925 at magnitude 5.5 and EXACTLY ZERO at 6.0 and at 6.5, because the causal rule then selects a weight of zero in every window. Two gates make that comparable: its second factor reproduces the earlier one exactly, and its magnitude-five table and weight path reproduce the previous round's exactly. So the harm was the inherited weight, not the readout. What survives is narrower and sharper: the mainshock-label second factor carries nothing usable about large mainshocks -- offered a free choice, the rule declines to use it at all above magnitude six. Two smaller corrections to the same entry. It said a floor of meaningless labels shows "the instrument has no bias of its own"; the floors actually sit uniformly at a small positive value, between +0.0000546 and +0.0002031, which is a bias, though one that works against the negative sign and so still supports the reading. And two of the five printed differences were truncated rather than rounded: +0.00121 and -0.00455 should read +0.00122 and -0.00456. Now the work those corrections set up. If the second factor is aimed at the wrong target, aim it at the right one: train it on "is this row a LARGE mainshock" instead of "is this row a mainshock". Three fitting-free or cheap measurements were taken before any contract was written. The first asks whether the new label can be learned at all, and the answer is barely: a model fitted on it separates large mainshocks from the other mainshocks at 0.501, 0.518 and 0.568 for thresholds 5.5, 6.0 and 6.5, against 0.416, 0.394 and 0.370 for the old label on the same task. The sign turns over, which is the defect reversing, but the magnitude is small, and it is not for want of data -- the last window has 47,733 training rows at magnitude six. The nine catalogue columns barely see the size of the coming shock even when asked directly, and that is a statement about the data rather than about any model. The second measures the effect itself, with both the label and the weight aimed at each threshold: +0.00337972 at 5.5 with 81% of windows positive and all four conditions, +0.00270274 at 6.0 with 72% and all four, and +0.00110282 at 6.5 with 47% and only the first condition. The effect at 5.5 is larger than the one the readout delivers at magnitude five. The third is the control described above, which is what allows the gain to be attributed to the label rather than to the aiming: aiming the weight alone buys exactly nothing at 6.0 and 6.5. Only one diagonal of that two-by-two was measured, so the attribution is supported in one direction only and is written that way in the contract. A preregistration was then frozen and a run launched; its verdict is NOT in this entry. The primary threshold is magnitude 6.0, chosen as the highest threshold at which sizing met all four conditions rather than the one with the largest margin, and the contract records that the rule itself postdates the numbers and that this round is therefore not an independent test of the effect size -- what it adds is a floor. An audit before freezing changed two things worth reporting. It pointed out that a floor world whose weight stays at zero has an effect of exactly zero by construction, so a floor made only of such worlds would test nothing beyond "the real mean is positive" -- something sizing had already shown; the contract now says that if every world of the primary floor stays at zero the verdict is at most "real, floor uninformative", and the caveat is not struck. It also pointed out that none of the three floor families tested the claim actually being made: they all destroy which events are mainshocks along with which mainshocks are large. A fourth family was added that permutes the large flag only among the 35,605 mainshocks, keeping the mainshock set exactly as it is and moving only which of them are large. That is the world the claim has to beat, and it was added before anything was frozen. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 121 (2026-09-03)

**2026-09-03 update 121 (the walk on the large-mainshock aim comes back REAL, and the controls say what the gain is -- a map of which cells produce large mainshocks).** The run frozen in the previous entry has finished: 36 windows, twenty floor worlds, 7,596 seconds, peak 1,367 MB. The frozen verifier passed all 22 of its checks with no post-freeze edits, rebuilding the 36 primary window effects from the saved row scores with its own masks and its own ranking code at a worst difference of 1.11e-16, regenerating all twenty floor worlds from their declared seeds, and reproducing the previous round's arm exactly at the magnitude-five gate. The primary verdict, at magnitude 6.0: the effect is +0.00270274 with 72% of windows positive against a bar of 0.001029 -- all four conditions -- and the primary floor, five worlds with the large-mainshock flag permuted over all 78,071 driving events, sits far below it at a mean of +0.00017 and a worst of +0.00031, rank 1 of 6. Every one of those five worlds moved its weight off zero, so the floor is not vacuous under the provision written for exactly that. REAL. The effect equals what sizing measured to the last printed digit, which the contract said to report and not to gate, and the weight path settled at 0.35 rather than at the top of the frozen grid, so for once the caveat about the optimum lying outside the grid is not needed at the primary (at 5.5 the path does sit at the top). The controls are where the information is. Family I -- added by an audit before freezing, permuting the large flag only among the 35,605 mainshocks, so that mainshock-ness is kept exactly and only WHICH mainshocks are large moves -- is CLEARED, and emphatically: its five worlds average -0.00003, and in three of the five the causal rule never left zero, meaning that offered a shuffled largeness label the rule declines to use it at all. Aiming at largeness therefore adds something real beyond aiming at mainshock-ness. Family C -- permuting the flag within each cell, so that each cell's rate of large mainshocks is kept exactly while the individual identities are destroyed -- is NOT CLEARED, and by a margin that names the channel: all five of its worlds EXCEED the real effect, mean +0.00664 against the real +0.00270, the largest 2.98 times it. Read together, what the largeness label adds is, to the resolution of these controls, a map of which cells produce large mainshocks. A world that keeps only that map does better than the truth -- plausibly because a label that is purely the map is a cleaner function of geography than the real one and easier for the second factor to learn -- and a world that keeps everything about mainshock-ness but scrambles the map carries nothing. Family M, permutation within magnitude bins, is not cleared either at a mean of +0.00306, but the contract said in advance that at a magnitude threshold this family is nearly the identity -- 96.5% of flag positions cannot move -- and that NOT CLEARED was the likely reading whatever the truth; it is recorded, not presented as evidence of anything. What is not claimed, from the contract's own list: the secondaries, including the larger +0.00338 at magnitude 5.5, have no floors and are not REAL; the round is not an independent test of the effect size, since sizing saw the numbers before the contract was written and the floor is what the round adds; five worlds give a rank of one in six, not a p-value below 0.05; no world preserves the magnitude grading and the cell map at once, so the residual is not thereby shown to be per-event; the base is a climatology and not the published forecast; magnitudes are not compared across catalogues, so nothing here is a statement about Japan; and the global catalogue's detection completeness is not uniform in space or time. The controls also name the next measurement. If the channel is a per-cell map of large-mainshock rate, that map can be built directly and causally -- each cell's past rate of large isolated shocks, no fitting at all -- and compared to the fitted readout on the same windows. Whether the direct map matches or beats the fitted second factor is a fitting-free sizing question, and it decides whether the fit is doing any work beyond carrying a map. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 122 (2026-09-04)

**2026-09-04 update 122 (three corrections to the last entry first, then the result they set up: counting beats fitting -- a per-cell map built by counting alone, with no model at all, is three times the fitted readout it replaces, and is judged REAL against the same floors).** Three corrections to update 121, found by an audit of that entry against the run log and the frozen contract. It said family M is nearly the identity because "96.5% of flag positions cannot move". Those are two different numbers conflated: the contract says 91.5% of events sit in a magnitude bin that is entirely flagged or entirely not, so 6,663 events CAN move; 96.4 to 96.5% is the fraction that DID NOT move in the five worlds actually drawn. It said the verifier passed "all 22 of its checks"; the individual checks number 21, and 22 was a count that included the summary line. And its list of what is not claimed, offered as "from the contract's own list", dropped three items from that list: the floor tests only the second factor, so the base arm is identical in every world and nothing there tests the base; no new observable is introduced, since the label is built from the same catalogue as the columns; and nine columns are used, not eleven. Now the work those controls set up. The previous entry's controls said the channel was a map of which cells produce large mainshocks: a world keeping only each cell's rate, with the individual identities destroyed, beat the truth in all five draws. The obvious question is whether the fitted second factor is doing any work beyond carrying that map, and it can be asked without any model at all. At each window, among the positive rows strictly before that window's training boundary, count how many fall in each cell and how many of those are painted by a large isolated shock, shrink the ratio toward the global rate, and add its logarithm to the climatology with a weight -- shrinkage and weight both chosen causally on earlier windows only, from frozen grids. Nothing is fitted; the nine columns are never read. A fitting-free sizing run measured it first, and a preregistration was frozen before the judging run, with the admission that sizing had seen every number including the direction of the comparison. The result at the primary threshold of magnitude 6.0: the counted map is worth +0.00855605 with 83% of windows positive, against +0.00270274 for the fitted readout on the same windows, the same rows and the same scored set. Judged against the same twenty floor worlds as the previous round -- carried in bit for bit, which the run asserts -- the primary floor of five worlds with the large-mainshock flag permuted over all 78,071 driving events sits at a mean of -0.00006 with a maximum of exactly zero, rank 1 of 6. REAL. The secondary comparison, map minus fit per window, is +0.00585331 with 78% of windows positive and all four conditions: CONFIRMED. The fitted second factor is not merely failing to add anything to the map it carries -- it is carrying the map worse than counting does. Its separation of large mainshocks from other mainshocks, 0.5792, is indistinguishable from the counted map's 0.5805, so the two agree about which events are large; what the fit adds is per-row variation inside each cell, and on this evidence that variation is noise. Two things the floors say that the previous round's could not. Family M, permutation within magnitude bins, is CLEARED here at +0.00597 against the real +0.00856, where for the fitted arm it was not; the counted arm is far enough above the magnitude-graded world to be separated from it. Family I, which keeps the mainshock set exactly and moves only which mainshocks are large, is CLEARED at -0.00009, as it was before. A prediction written into the contract before the run was wrong, and in the direction that makes the test easier rather than harder: it said a counted map should copy a shuffled world's residual per-cell roughness more faithfully than a fit does, so the primary floor might sit HIGHER than the previous round's +0.00017. It sat lower -- every one of the five worlds at or below zero -- and two of the five never left a weight of zero at all. The prediction is recorded as made and as wrong; what it was for was to say in advance which direction a surprise would come from. Family C, the within-cell shuffle, is a third case the contract did not name. That contract classified it in advance as a consistency check rather than a control, because a within-cell permutation preserves the very quantity this arm counts, and said a mean near the real effect would confirm the map reading while a mean far below it would contradict the premise. It came in at +0.03256, nearly four times the real effect, which is neither. The honest record is that the contract failed to write down that case, and the fact is reported without a reading attached: shuffling which mainshocks within a cell are large, holding each cell's rate fixed, produces a stronger arm than the truth, as it also did for the fitted factor at a smaller size. What is not claimed. The secondaries at magnitudes 5.0, 5.5 and 6.5 have no floors and are not REAL, though all four conditions hold at each. This is not an independent test of the effect size, since sizing saw every number before the contract was written; the floor is what the round adds. Five worlds give a rank of one in six, not a p-value below 0.05. The twenty worlds are the previous round's own, so the two rounds' floor ranks are correlated and are not independent evidence about each other. The shrinkage path settled at the top of its frozen grid, so that optimum may lie outside it, and no grid is widened after results. The floor permutes the flag the map counts; the climatology underneath is identical in every world, so nothing here tests it. No new observable is introduced -- the same catalogue is re-read, and this arm uses no columns at all, only event history, cell and magnitude. The base is a climatology and not the published forecast, magnitudes are not compared across catalogues so nothing here is a statement about Japan, and the global catalogue's detection completeness is not uniform in space or time. The verifier, frozen before the run, passed all 21 of its checks with no post-freeze edits, including a check added after an audit pointed out that no floor world's arm was being independently recomputed anywhere -- a configuration error there could have produced a false null that every other check would have passed. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 123 (2026-09-04)

**2026-09-04 update 123 (a floor that was really a partial oracle, and a lever that only worked once its shape was changed).** Two of the last three entries leaned on a control that this one shows was contaminated, so that correction comes first. Rounds 201 and 202 both reported that a within-cell shuffle -- a world that keeps each cell's whole-record count of large isolated mainshocks and only moves which of that cell's events carry the flag -- produced a STRONGER arm than the truth, at +0.0326 and +0.0333 against real effects of +0.0027 and +0.0086. Update 122 reported that as a fact with no reading attached, which was right; the repository's summary page went further and gave it as the reason for saying the effect is only a geographic map. That reason does not hold. The first explanation tried here was that the shuffle spreads a cell's total uniformly through its history, so the pre-boundary count becomes a better estimate of the whole-record rate; that was tested and it failed, the shuffled map being slightly LESS correlated with the whole-record rate than the real one, 0.8361 against 0.8550. The right instrument found something sharper. Large isolated mainshocks are rare per cell: of the 1,469 cells that ever hold one, 503 hold exactly one. For such a cell, if that one shock falls inside the window being scored, the real causal map is cold by construction, because nothing has happened there yet. A shuffle that conserves the cell's total can move that very shock into the training past, and the scored labels stay real in every floor world, so the shuffle hands the map a copy of the answer. Measured directly, the shuffled map ranks the cells that actually fire in the scored window at 0.6603 against the real map's 0.5928, and of the cells that fire it has already seen 88.7% of them fire against the real map's 81.2%. That family is therefore a partial oracle and not a control, and nothing may be read from it in either direction: it is not evidence that the effect is only a map, and it is not evidence that the effect is more than a map. Round 202's contract had said in advance that a within-cell permutation preserves the very quantity the arm counts and so cannot separate the two; the overreach was in the summary page, which has been corrected. The general form of the mistake is worth stating: a permutation that conserves a group total is conserving a quantity computed over the whole record, including the future, so whenever the counted thing is rare per group it leaks the scored period into the training period. The same argument shows there is no permutation control available here at all, because a shuffle that respects the training boundary leaves the counted map identical bit for bit. Two further measurements set the scale. A map counted from every window except the scored one and its immediate neighbours -- still not causal, but not knowing the answer either -- is worth +0.0115 per window against the causal map's +0.0086, so about +0.0030 is what a better causal estimator of the same rate could reach. A map counted from the whole record is worth +0.0594, and the gap between those two is self-knowledge that no causal arm can have. Then the lever. The obvious way to estimate a per-cell rate better is to borrow strength from neighbouring cells, and the first form tried was to smooth the counts themselves before dividing. Swept with every window in view it looked good, +0.0117 at a smoothing width of one degree against +0.0086 unsmoothed. It does not survive. Holding the shrinkage and weight at the choices the unsmoothed arm had already made causally, and moving only the width, the best width is half a degree, not one, and the gain is +0.00126: the apparent advantage of the wider kernel was confounded with re-choosing the shrinkage, since smoothing and shrinkage do the same job. Worse, a rule that chooses the width causally from earlier windows misses the peak, settling on 0.75 in sixteen windows and returning +0.00086 with only 53% of windows positive. The reason is measurable: the value of smoothing decays as history accumulates, +0.0022 over the first twelve windows, +0.0009 over the next twelve, and -0.0010 over the last twelve, so a rule that learns the width from the past always over-smooths. The gain at half a degree is a swept maximum and is not claimed. Changing the shape of the lever fixed it. Instead of smoothing the counts, shrink each cell's rate toward its smoothed NEIGHBOURHOOD rate rather than toward the global rate -- the same two knobs, one line different, and by construction a cell with many past events is untouched while a cell with few borrows heavily. With the width joined to the same causal joint selection, the arm is +0.01161513 with 89% of windows positive, and the increment over the counted map of round 202 is +0.00305907 with 75% of windows positive and all four conditions. This time it is a plateau and not a ridge: the increment passes all four conditions at every width from half a degree to two degrees, and the causal selection captures 73% of what the best setting captures with every window in view. A width of zero reproduces round 202 exactly, which the run asserts. What is not claimed. All of this is fitting-free sizing, with no floor and no preregistration, so none of it is a verdict; the judging round is written next, with the increment as its primary and the same twenty floor worlds carried in bit for bit. The width grid was chosen after seeing the first form fail. The base is a climatology and not the published forecast. Magnitudes are not compared across catalogues, so nothing here is a statement about Japan, and the global catalogue's detection completeness is not uniform in space or time. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 124 (2026-09-04)

**2026-09-04 update 124 (a per-cell rate that borrows from its neighbours, and a frozen independent check that failed -- for a reason worth more than the result it was checking).** The counted map of the previous entry shrinks each cell's rate toward the global rate. This round changes one thing: it shrinks toward the cell's own smoothed NEIGHBOURHOOD rate instead, with the smoothing width joined to the same causal joint selection, and with a width of zero defined to be the global rate so that the previous round is reproduced exactly -- which the run asserts against the previous round's saved per-window values at max difference exactly zero. What is judged is not the arm but the INCREMENT: the arm with the width free, minus the arm with the width pinned at zero, window by window, on identical rows. The result at the primary threshold of magnitude 6.0 is +0.00305907 with 75% of windows positive and all four conditions. The primary floor is new and is the part of the design worth reading. The claim is that borrowing from GEOGRAPHIC neighbours helps, so the sharp null is not "the flag is noise" but "the borrowing is not spatial". Five worlds permute the cell centre coordinates that build the kernel, keeping the flag, the counts and the climatology. Permuting a coordinate array permutes the distance matrix symmetrically, so the floor kernel is the real kernel with its rows and columns relabelled: the neighbourhood graph is identical -- same weights, same degree distribution, same number of non-zero entries -- and the only thing destroyed is which cell sits at which node. Those five worlds are read two ways. Read with the selection rule free (family G), the rule mostly declines the meaningless kernel, choosing a width of zero in most windows, and the increments come out at -0.00012, 0, 0, -0.00017 and -0.00062. Read at the settings the real arm actually chose for each window (family H), the kernel is forced to be used and the increments are -0.00222, -0.00220, -0.00248, -0.00318 and -0.00337, a mean of -0.00269 against the real +0.00306, rank one of six. Family H is the primary floor precisely because family G cannot rise: a floor that is pinned at zero makes rank one follow from the effect being positive, which is the first of the four conditions, so it constrains nothing. Now the part that matters more than the number. The contract for this round was audited before freezing, and then revised three times after that audit -- the primary floor moved from one family to another twice, and a branch was added about a third -- and those three revisions were never audited. So while the run was executing, and before any primary, floor or verdict line existed in its log, the frozen contract was checked against the frozen code, and the defects found were written into an addendum, frozen with its own checksum, before the result was known. A defect disclosed before the result cannot be a convenient one, and that is the whole reason for the timing. There are six worth stating. First and worst: the contract says "No judged number was seen in that trial that sizing had not already reported", and that sentence is false. The trial run of the machinery on a truncated grid printed six floor values, and the sizing that preceded this round computed no floor of any kind, so all six were numbers sizing had not reported -- and the floor rank is half of the verdict rule. The paragraph predicting that family H would sit below zero was written before family H existed in the code, but the contract file was last written after those trial values had been seen, and one modification time cannot distinguish two edits. The prediction is therefore discounted here: the floor is reported as a measurement and never as a confirmed prediction. Second, the contract's escape clause for an uninformative primary floor cannot fire at all: family H is evaluated at the real arm's own settings, so it is uninformative only if the real arm never smoothed, in which case the increment is identically zero and the round is null by the first condition. What actually dilutes that floor is partial, and it happened: in the three warm-up windows the real path used no smoothing, so all five floor worlds tie with the real arm there by construction. Three of thirty-six, reported as a bare number, because no threshold for it was pre-registered. Third, one item in the contract's list of things not promised still names the superseded family as the primary floor. Its factual half survives and is worth keeping: the arm with the width free searches 168 settings where the pinned arm searches 24, and it is family G, the one that re-optimises, that carries that asymmetry -- family H, at forced settings, carries none of it. So the asymmetry is tested by a reported family and not by the deciding one, and what that reported family shows is that re-optimising freely on meaningless geography produces no gain. Fourth, two outcomes were left undefined in the branch table, exactly the gap the previous round had to record for a different family, and the code printed labels for them that the contract does not contain. Fifth, the contract says any gate failure stops the run before any judgment is printed, but the world gates run after the arms and the increment have already been printed. Sixth, the checker rebuilds the real increment independently at every one of the 36 windows -- the whole surface, with an independently written rank statistic, an independently derived climatology and kernels built from a different spherical formula -- but the floor rank that co-decides the verdict is re-derived from the run's own saved surfaces, with one floor world rebuilt from its seed at three windows. The reported families, none of which can move the verdict, are worth setting out because two of them say something and one of them exposes another gap. The family that re-optimises freely on shuffled geography lands at -0.00018 with two of its five worlds declining the kernel outright: searching a larger grid on meaningless neighbours produces no gain, which is the only control this round has for the asymmetry between a 168-setting search and a 24-setting one. The uniform permutation of the flag lands at -0.00024, below zero, where rank one follows from the effect being positive and the family constrains nothing. The permutation that keeps the mainshock set and moves only which mainshocks are large lands at +0.00034 with a standard deviation of 0.00056 -- and that outcome, between zero and twice its own spread, is one of the two the contract failed to define. The code printed a label for it that the contract does not contain; the number is recorded here as a bare fact with no reading, the way the previous round had to record a family whose case its own contract had not written down. The permutation within magnitude bins lands at +0.00320, above the real increment, ranked fifth of six. That was pre-registered as expected rather than surprising, because that family moves only about three and a half per cent of the flags and its arm is therefore close to the real one; the reading was fixed before the judged run, after a trial run on a truncated grid produced exactly that. And the within-cell shuffle, declared contaminated in advance in the previous entry, lands at +0.00068 and is printed as a fact with no verdict word and no reading in either direction. And then the checker, frozen before the run, FAILED. Five of its twenty-four checks did not pass, all of them the ones that rebuild the arm's surface from scratch. The cause is in its own third check and is a defect in the contract rather than in the arithmetic. The kernel weights every pair of cells within four widths and zeroes the rest, and the contract specifies the distance between two cells only as "the great-circle distance in degrees" without naming a formula. The run used the spherical law of cosines; the checker, deliberately written to be independent, used the haversine. Those two are mathematically the same and differ here by at most 0.00000085 of a degree. But the arena's cell centres lie on a regular one-degree lattice, so enormous numbers of pairs sit within a billionth of a degree of the cutoffs at two, three, four, six and eight degrees -- 644 to 1,668 of them per width -- and between 358 and 682 pairs per width fall on opposite sides of the cutoff under the two formulas. A hard threshold at exactly four widths is not a well-defined object on a regular lattice, and the contract did not make it one. Worse, the checker's own gate for this was written with a tolerance escape that summed discrepancies across widths against the number of pairs near a cutoff, so it passed the very mismatch that then broke everything downstream -- a defect an audit had flagged as minor before the freeze, and which turned out to be the load-bearing one. A diagnostic written after the failure, and therefore not frozen and not evidence in the way the checker would have been, separates the two possibilities. Given the run's own formula, an independent rebuild reproduces the causal path exactly and the primary increment to two parts in a hundred million, so the arithmetic is not in question. Under the other formula the primary is +0.00309203 rather than +0.00305907, the floor's five worlds are -0.00217, -0.00183, -0.00251, -0.00271 and -0.00294 for a mean of -0.00243, the rank is still one of six, and the verdict is still REAL. So the verdict is robust to the ambiguity that broke the check. That is not the same as having been checked. The honest status of this round is that its verdict stands on the run, that its frozen independent check failed, that the failure is explained and the explanation does not threaten the verdict, and that the round does not have a clean independent verification. The checker is not edited to make it pass: the contract's own section 8 says the direction of a fix is decided by the frozen contract, and on the question of which formula to use the contract is silent, so there is nothing for it to decide. One further defect belongs with it: even given the same formula, the independent rebuild agrees only to two parts in a hundred million, and the checker's frozen tolerance was one part in ten billion. That tolerance had been justified from what previous rounds achieved, but this round's arm stores its climatology in single precision while any independent rebuild works in double, so a realistic floor here was one part in ten million. A tolerance carried over from other rounds is not a tolerance for this one. What is not claimed. The effect size is not independently estimated: fitting-free sizing measured it before the contract existed, and the run reproduces that figure exactly because it is the same computation. Five worlds give a rank of one in six, which is not a p-value. Because all five floor worlds came out below zero, rank one follows arithmetically from the increment being positive; what the floor adds is that the floor worlds were free to come out positive at the settings the real arm chose, and did not. The shrinkage parameter sat at the top of its frozen grid in all 33 post-warm-up windows, so its optimum may lie outside that grid, and no grid was widened after results. The base is a climatology and not the published forecast, the floors move only the counted flag so nothing here tests that base, no new observable is introduced, and magnitudes are not compared across catalogues, so none of this is a statement about Japan. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 125 (2026-09-04)

**2026-09-04 update 125 (a prediction frozen before the machinery that could answer it, and two thirds of it held).** The previous round's contract put one question outside itself on purpose: the earlier smoothing shape had been measured decaying as history accumulated, and whether the shape actually adopted decays the same way was declared untested there. This round answers it, and the point of the exercise is as much the order of operations as the answer. The previous round had to publish an addendum admitting that its own prediction could not be shown to precede the observation it predicted, because the prediction shared a file with a later edit and one modification time cannot carry two claims. So this time the prediction was written into its own file and frozen with its own checksum before the per-window series had been looked at, and the script that reads that series was written and frozen with its own checksum before it was run. Three statements were made, each able to fail: that the last third of the windows would still show a positive gain, unlike the earlier shape whose last third was negative; that the first third would be larger than the last, so some decay rather than none; and that the rank correlation between window index and gain would be negative but weaker than a half. The mechanism offered for them was that the earlier shape smoothed the counts themselves and so had to fade as counts grew, whereas this shape only changes what a cell is shrunk toward, and the shrinkage is strong enough that a cell with many past events is barely moved at all, so the help stays concentrated on thin cells in every epoch rather than only in early ones. The first two held and the third did not. Across the same three blocks of twelve windows the gains are +0.00281, +0.00394 and +0.00242, with seven, eleven and nine of twelve windows positive, against the earlier shape's +0.00219, +0.00090 and -0.00099. The last block is clearly positive, and the first block does exceed the last. But the rank correlation is +0.10, not negative: the series is not a decay at all, it is close to flat with a rise in the middle. The consequence I predicted is there and the shape I predicted is not, and that is recorded as written and as wrong. The floor for this is the part that did not work, and the fault is mine rather than the data's. A series of thirty-six numbers always has a slope, so the frozen criterion required the real profile to lie outside the ten shuffled-geography worlds the previous round had already computed, on BOTH the rank correlation and the last block, before the word decay could be used. The last block separates cleanly: +0.00242 against a range of -0.00376 to exactly zero across the ten. The rank correlation half cannot be evaluated at all, because two of those ten worlds have a gain that is identically zero in every window -- the selection rule declined the meaningless kernel everywhere -- so their rank correlation is undefined, and an undefined value propagates into the range the real value was to be compared against. The frozen criterion therefore returns not separated, and the phrase "the intervention decaying" is not used here. The criterion should have excluded degenerate worlds and did not; it is recorded as a defect rather than patched and re-run, because a criterion rewritten after seeing what it returned is not a criterion. What is not claimed. This describes a series the previous round had already computed, so it is not an independent test of that round's effect and cannot repair that round's failed independent check. The block boundaries were inherited from the earlier measurement so that the two shapes are compared on one partition, and no other partition was tried. Nothing here says where the profile goes beyond the record that exists. The base is a climatology rather than the published forecast, and magnitudes are not compared across catalogues, so none of this is a statement about Japan. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 126 (2026-09-04)

**2026-09-04 update 126 (corrections to the last two entries, two of them mine and both about words I had already struck or checked myself).** An audit of updates 124 and 125 against the run log, the frozen contract and the addendum found six things that need saying, and the first two are errors rather than omissions. First: update 124 says the primary floor was chosen "precisely because family G cannot rise". I struck that exact word in this round's own addendum before the verdict was known, recording that "cannot" was inferred from two worlds on a truncated grid and is not licensed -- and then published it anyway. The run refutes it directly: three of the five coordinate-shuffled worlds did leave a width of zero, in one, two and five windows respectively, with increments of -0.00012, -0.00017 and -0.00062, and only two of the five were identically zero. The same entry says so correctly a paragraph later, so it contradicts itself. What should have been written is that the family which re-optimises freely tends to DECLINE a meaningless kernel, came out at or below zero in all five worlds, and that a floor sitting at or below zero makes rank one follow from the increment being positive -- which is why it is not the deciding floor. Second, and more consequential: both update 124 and the repository's summary page say the contract "never named a formula" for the great-circle distance, and update 124 argues from that silence that the contract's own rule for resolving a disagreement between run and checker had nothing to decide. That is false. The definition in section 2 is indeed formula-free, but section 5's kernel gate names one: it explains that the distance matrix diagonal is set rather than computed "because d(c,c) = 0 is a fact and the spherical law of cosines does not return it in double precision". The contract therefore names exactly one formula, the one the run used, in a gate describing the run. Whether a parenthesis in a gate settles a definition in another section is arguable, and I am not going to argue it in the direction that favours me. What matters is that the conclusion does not depend on it: if that parenthesis does settle it, then the checker deviated and should have used the law of cosines, and the diagnostic already shows what happens when it does -- the causal path is reproduced exactly and the increment to two parts in a hundred million, which still exceeds the checker's own frozen tolerance of one part in ten billion. Either way the round has no passing independent check. The reason moves from "the contract could not decide" to "even deciding it in the run's favour leaves the check failing on its own tolerance", and the claim of silence is withdrawn. Third, update 125 says the round 205 prediction was frozen "before the per-window series had been looked at". That is true as far as it goes and it is what the prediction file claims for itself, but the file also discloses something the entry did not carry over: the aggregate was already known when the prediction was written -- the mean +0.00306, the fraction of positive windows 75%, and the interval half-width. Two of those three matter, because 75% of thirty-six windows is twenty-seven, so the statement that the last block of twelve would still be positive was made knowing that most windows in the record were positive. The prediction is weaker than the entry made it sound, and the third statement failing remains the more interesting half of it. Fourth, an omission the contract explicitly asked not to be left to be noticed: the shape of the intervention was itself chosen after a different smoothing shape failed in sizing, and the width grid was written after seeing that failure, so the round tests one shape arrived at by trying two. Neither entry carried that, and the summary page's sentence about none of the results being an independent estimate of its own effect size named only the two older rounds and did not cover the third. Both are fixed. Fifth, update 124 says the residual between the run and an independent rebuild comes from the run storing its climatology in single precision. That was not measured. The checker's own climatology check, built in double precision, reproduces the run's to one part in ten thousand million million, so single precision is a candidate for the residual and not a demonstrated cause; the entry asserted a mechanism where the record has only a coincidence. Sixth, five smaller things. The checker reports twenty-four checks but the addendum records one of them as a tautology, so twenty-three are substantive. The five failures are not all surface rebuilds -- two of them are downstream of the rebuilt surface, being a path re-derivation and a floor read from saved surfaces. Three worlds were rebuilt from their seeds, one per family, not one world. Grid edges were reported for the shrinkage parameter only; the width sat at the top of its grid in five of thirty-three windows and the weight never touched an edge. The cutoff disagreement occurs at five of the six smoothing widths, not all six: at the narrowest width no pair sits near the cutoff at all. And two gate clauses assert less than their wording says -- the shuffled worlds' kernel counts are printed but never asserted equal, and the gate described as covering a slice actually asserts the arm derived from it -- while the code clips the neighbourhood rate where the contract's formula does not. None of the six changes the verdict, which was and remains REAL on the run with its frozen independent check having failed. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 127 (2026-09-05)

**2026-09-05 update 127 (the failed check taken again: the verdict stands, and this time the instrument can be checked).** The previous round measured something and could not verify it. Its finding was that shrinking each cell's counted rate toward the smoothed rate of its neighbourhood, rather than toward one global number, is worth a further gain on top of the counted map, and that finding passed every condition the contract set and cleared a floor of five worlds whose cell coordinates had been shuffled. Then its own frozen checker failed five of twenty-four checks. Neither cause was in the arithmetic. The first was that the kernel was not uniquely determined: the contract's definition of it gave the separation only as a great-circle distance in degrees, and the one formula the contract named anywhere sat in a gate describing the run, so the checker reached for the other standard one. Those two agree to less than a millionth of a degree, which would be irrelevant anywhere except where the kernel had a hard cutoff at four widths, and the arena is a regular one-degree lattice, so at five of the six widths between six hundred and seventeen hundred cell pairs per width sit within a billionth of a degree of exactly that boundary and three to seven hundred of them fell on opposite sides of it. The second cause was that the checker's tolerance had been inherited from earlier rounds rather than measured, and was tight enough that the check would have failed even with the formula settled. A verdict that survives either formula is not the same thing as a verdict that has been checked, and the honest report was that the round had no clean independent check. This round is that check, and it changes exactly two things. The contract now names the formula, choosing the one that stays accurate at small separations. And the hard cutoff is removed: the Gaussian weight four widths out is three ten thousandths of the peak, it is discarded from the top and the bottom of a ratio so most of it cancels anyway, and without a boundary there is no membership for two formulas to disagree about. Everything else is carried across untouched, down to the twenty permutation worlds regenerated from their declared seeds and checked bit for bit against round 201's. That carrying-across is also a limit on what a reproduction means here: the rows, the windows and the floor worlds are the same ones, so the two rounds' floor ranks are one piece of evidence seen twice, not two independent pieces. Three things were fixed in the method before anything was run. The prediction was written into its own file and frozen with its own checksum before the contract, the run or the checker existed, and the freeze record embeds the failed directory listing that proves those files were not there yet. That is the direct remedy for the previous round, which had to publish an addendum admitting that its prediction shared a file with a later edit, and one modification time cannot carry two claims. The tolerance was measured rather than inherited. The first measurement gave two parts in a hundred million, and that was the wrong thing to measure: the checker had been holding a quantity in double precision that the contract specifies is stored in single, so the residual was an artefact of the checker's own deviation from the specification rather than a property of the arithmetic. Implementing what the specification says is not a loss of independence; ignoring it had inflated the disagreement by eight orders of magnitude. Repeated with the specification implemented, on a truncated trial of six worlds and six windows, the residual was machine precision, and the full checker later saw the same. Third, the checker itself was run against that truncated problem before being frozen, because the previous round's checker ran for the first time in earnest and failed five checks at once. Two things about the process are worth stating rather than leaving to be noticed. The design review that had already caught three defects that would have invalidated this round -- one in the run, two in the checker's rule for handling ties -- was not available for the last set of edits made to the checker, so those edits were reviewed by the author alone; that was written into the contract before the contract was frozen.
