And the frozen contract carries two sentences left over from an earlier draft of the section that measures the tolerance: they still describe the checker as working in double precision and the two implementations as agreeing only to two parts in a hundred million. Both describe a larger disagreement than the one that occurred, so nothing rests on them, and the tolerance the rule actually applies is stated separately and is the one the checker reports against. A frozen document cannot be edited, so the stale lines are named here instead. Four statements were frozen in advance, each able to fail. The verdict would reproduce. The size would move by less than five ten thousandths. The checker would pass every check, which is the whole point of the round. And with no cutoff the two formulas would agree well enough that no kernel entry could move. The size prediction is the one worth stating plainly because it could have embarrassed the claim: the intervention is now worth 0.00290 of area under the curve per window against the counted map, where the previous round measured 0.00306, a difference of 0.00016. Three quarters of the windows are positive, all four conditions hold, and the primary floor of five coordinate-shuffled worlds comes in at a mean of minus 0.00275 with every one of the five below zero, ranking the real arm first of six. The formula prediction was witnessed by the checker, and before the freeze by the measurement that set the tolerance: with no cutoff the two great-circle formulas agree everywhere to eight parts in a trillion, so nothing can flip. The arm as a whole, which has no floor and is therefore never called real, passes all four conditions at every magnitude threshold tested, and its slice with the smoothing switched off reproduces the earlier counted map exactly, per window, at all four thresholds. And the checker passed: all twenty-seven of its checks, including the five that failed last time. The one that matters most is the heaviest. It rebuilt every one of the twenty-five floor worlds from their declared seeds, at every one of the thirty-six windows, with its own kernels, its own areas under the curve and both causal selection paths re-derived from the contract's text, and agreed with the run to within double-precision round-off. This round's contract also added a rule for something the previous ones had left implicit: a selection is the largest value on a surface, so two settings can in principle split between two implementations that agree only to round-off, and a round where that happens must report it rather than present the paths as identical. It did not happen. There were no tolerated ties, and the verdict, every floor rank, every count of worlds that declined the smoothing and every branch code come out the same along the checker's own path as along the run's. One consequence of removing the cutoff can be recorded without being read as evidence, since the family concerned is reported rather than decisive: of the five worlds that shuffle the coordinates and are then free to reselect, four went on to use the smoothing somewhere and none of them profited by it, where before the change two of the five had never left the flat slice, so their increment was identically zero. What a pass does and does not buy has to be said in the same breath. It verifies the arithmetic and the floor of a claim whose size was measured first, in sizing, and no amount of checking makes that size an independent estimate of itself. The shape being checked was itself chosen after a different smoothing shape had failed in sizing, and the grid of widths was written after seeing that failure; the previous round's contract asked for that to be said and both of its public entries left it out, so it is said here. A pass says nothing about a fitted arm, another horizon or another arena, and it cannot say whether the contract asked the right question. The base here is a climatology and not the forecast that is published, and the cost on all positives is outside this round. Five worlds give a rank of one in six at best, which is not a probability below one in twenty. The comparison also pits a path searching a hundred and sixty-eight settings against one searching twenty-four; that asymmetry is judged rather than controlled away, and the primary floor does not carry it. Two of the reported families are not evidence and are marked so in advance: the one that shuffles within cells is contaminated by construction and licenses nothing in either direction, and the one that shuffles within magnitude bins leaves ninety-six per cent of its flags where they were, so its coming in above the real arm is the expected behaviour of a near-identity and is not read as a failure. The uniform floor landed below zero, which means its rank of one follows from the effect being positive at all and constrains nothing. And removing the cutoff has a cost worth naming: every cell now borrows from every other cell with a weight that may underflow to zero far away, so the widths describe where the weight is concentrated and are no longer a radius. Nothing here is a statement about Japan, because magnitudes are not compared across catalogues. One number in the run points at the next question rather than answering this one. The shrinkage parameter, which controls how far a cell's own count is pulled toward its neighbourhood, sat at the very top of its frozen grid in every one of the thirty-three windows that had a history to learn from. The contract forbids widening a grid once results are in, so it is reported and not fixed. But the reading is plain enough: the rule keeps asking to lean as far toward the neighbourhood as it is allowed to. Whether a cell's own count is doing any work at all can be put as a question in its own right, by measuring an arm that drops that count entirely and uses the smoothed neighbourhood field alone, which is a different arm rather than a wider grid and so has a baseline that does not move. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 128 (2026-09-05)

**2026-09-05 update 128 (a kernel bent the wrong way, and the thing that survived was not a direction).** Two rounds are reported together because the second overturned the reading of its own headline. The first asked a narrow question left over from the last one: the rule that chooses how far to lean on a cell's neighbourhood had been sitting at the top of its frozen range in every window, so the obvious suspicion was that the cell's own count had stopped doing any work. Taking that limit is not the same as discarding the own count, because the smoothing kernel gives a cell weight one on itself, and at a width of one degree a cell was still a quarter of its own neighbourhood. Comparing the two arms each at its own best settings said the difference could not be resolved, but that comparison was wrong: the arm without the extra weight had quietly compensated elsewhere. Holding the other settings at the original arm's own causal choice and moving only the one being tested, the extra weight on the own count is worth 0.00211750 in sixty-seven per cent of windows and passes all four conditions. The heading first written for that round said the opposite, and was corrected after an audit; the correction is the one this work keeps having to make, that comparing two arms each at its own best settings hides the intervention being tested. The second round attacked the kernel itself. Earthquakes lie along plate boundaries, so the neighbourhood a cell should borrow from ought to be a line rather than a disc, and the ellipse can be built out of the arena's own past counts with nothing new fetched: the principal axis of the cloud around each cell, with the elongation applied in proportion to how elongated that cloud actually is, so a round cloud stays round and there is no threshold to sit on. Because the split uses a unit direction, an aspect ratio of one collapses to the previous kernel exactly, and that reproduction was used as the gate; it matched the stored surface to zero. Elongating along the axis lost, at every aspect ratio, in three quarters of the windows or more. The control declared before the run -- the same ellipse turned across the axis -- gained 0.00068167 in sixty-nine per cent of windows and passed all four conditions, and it kept 0.00044540 after the amount of smoothing was matched on a grid fine enough to match it, which the frozen four-point grid had been too coarse to do. That looked like a statement about direction. An adversarial audit of the code and the logs said it was not, and it was right. For a cell sitting on a belt the across-belt direction is empty, so an ellipse turned across the belt is, for the cells that carry the counts, simply a narrower circular kernel along it. An isotropic kernel with no direction in it at all, whose width is set separately for each cell by exactly the same elongation factor, recovers 0.00059772 of the 0.00068167, and the difference between the two is 0.00008395 in forty-seven per cent of windows, inside its own bar. The ellipse was the wrong description of what was happening. What survives is narrower and more interesting: the width of the smoothing should depend on the cell, and specifically it should be smaller where the local cloud of past events is more linear. That is not the textbook adaptive kernel, which narrows where the data are dense; the linearity measure is essentially uncorrelated with the past count, at a rank correlation of 0.009, and a density-driven width gains between 0.00003 and 0.00008. Nor is it merely a different average width: keeping the exact multiset of widths and shuffling it across cells gives five worlds averaging 0.00019751, and the real assignment beats their mean by 0.00040021 in sixty-one per cent of windows with all four conditions met. Several things are not being claimed. Neither round was a judgement: nothing was preregistered and there was no floor beyond the declared controls, so the next round has to freeze the adaptive width as an intervention and judge it against the shuffled-width floor. The turned ellipse was declared as a control and is being read as a result after the fact. The adaptive width on its own does not pass all four conditions against the arm it improves, reaching 0.00059772 in fifty-six per cent of windows. Its gain depends on where the older rule had landed: in the seventeen windows where that rule chose a width of one and a half degrees it is worth 0.00108220 in eighty-eight per cent of them, while in the seven windows where the rule chose one degree, which the fine grid shows is the best single width, it is worth minus 0.00031208. Letting the arm re-choose its own leaning strength by the same causal rule removes the four conditions for the ellipse. All of this is at one magnitude threshold, on the mainshock target, inside one global arena, and the sizes are not compared with the Japanese one. Two mechanisms were proposed for why narrowing along the belt helps. The first, that the field being smoothed varies faster along the belt, is refuted by its own test: it varies less along than across, in none of the thirty-three windows the other way, at every distance band and every count cut. The second, that a target which is nearly the same for every cell in a belt erases the small real differences between them, had its predictions written down before the numbers existed; the first held in every window and the second did not, and by the rule written in advance that is recorded as unknown rather than as refuted, since with thirty-three windows the test could only have detected a fairly strong correlation. One further note belongs here because it is the reason any of these numbers can be trusted: the gate that requires the fine grid to reproduce the frozen one caught a mistake in the new code, which had failed to normalise its direction vectors, and without it an entire curve would have been reported as measurement. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

## update 129 (2026-09-05)

**2026-09-05 update 129 (the width was promoted to a judgement, and the judgement failed on the part nobody was testing).** The previous round left one thing standing: the smoothing that turns a cell's counted rate into a neighbourhood rate should not use the same width everywhere, but a narrower one where the past events around a cell lie along a line. That was a sizing, so it was written into a contract, frozen with its checker, and judged. The intervention is a single change, the width of the kernel becoming one number per cell instead of one number for the whole map, with no direction anywhere in it; everything else, including how far to lean on the neighbourhood and how much weight the whole map gets, was held at the choice the older rule had already made for that window. One free parameter remained, how strongly the width responds to the elongation, and the contract made a causal rule choose it from the windows already scored, with "no response at all" in the range so the rule could refuse. Three tolerances were measured before the contract was written rather than inherited from earlier rounds: the code path is bit-identical across two separate processes, the no-response setting reproduces the frozen previous surface to all seventeen printed digits, and the checker's own ranking implementation agrees with the one the run uses to about one part in ten thousand million million. The first two are why three of the gates could be asserted at exactly zero, and the third is why the checker's own tolerance is not zero. The verdict is that the intervention fails. It is worth 0.00030377 and is positive in half the windows, where the contract required at least three fifths and a margin clear of its own error bar, and it clears neither. The floor is reported and, as the contract says in advance, not interpreted: a floor can turn a pass into a failure but cannot turn a failure into a pass. For the record it was a real floor rather than a formality, nine worlds in which the exact collection of widths is kept and only the question of which cell gets which width is destroyed, none of which refused the intervention, and the real assignment came first of the ten. What makes this round worth reporting is where the failure sits. The contract predicted a pass and was wrong, and it also predicted, correctly, that making a rule choose the strength would cost something; and it said in advance that if the primary failed the reading was to be that the rule cannot choose, not that the width does nothing, and it named the numbers that would have to decide that. Those numbers say it plainly. Left at a fixed strength rather than chosen, the same intervention is worth 0.00045305 and is positive in nearly two thirds of the windows and passes all four conditions -- reported, floorless, and therefore not a result. The split by regime says the rest: the gain lives in the twenty windows where the older rule had settled on a wide kernel, worth 0.00081242 in seventy-one per cent of the seventeen at one and a half degrees and 0.00053303 in the three at two degrees, and it is absent or negative in the thirteen windows where that rule had chosen a narrow one. The strength the rule picked in its first few windows was its worst, and those early windows are the ones where the older rule was already narrow, so the selection did its damage where there was nothing to gain. One further question can be answered without computing anything new, from a grid of constant widths scored in the previous round on the same rows: in the seventeen wide-kernel windows, the per-cell width beats the best constant width for those windows even when that constant is chosen with the answer in hand, 0.00085453 against 0.00049927. So this is not a roundabout way of repairing a badly chosen constant. Against a constant chosen with the answer in hand separately for every window it loses, but that comparison is a maximum over ten values and the previous round measured how much such a maximum flatters itself. Several things remain unclaimed. This was not an independent test, since the same rows and windows produced the sizing that suggested it. An adversarial audit of the contract before it was frozen found four serious problems, all of which were fixed first: the checker was rebuilding only three windows and taking the rest of the run's own output as a key, so it now rebuilds every window, every strength and every floor world from the contract text and agrees to two parts in ten thousand million million; one tolerance was inherited and was replaced by a measured zero; a floor world that refuses scores nothing and is beaten automatically, so the count of refusals is now printed; and the floor destroys every per-cell property at once, so it cannot say that elongation specifically is what the width tracks. That last point was measured rather than argued: the elongation measure is essentially uncorrelated with how many past events a cell has seen, but it is correlated, at about minus a quarter, with how many neighbouring cells exist within two degrees, so a cell at the edge of the studied region scores as more elongated, and no floor was run that can separate the two. Twenty checks were re-derived independently and none failed, and nothing had to be repaired after the fact. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.
